## Story & Purpose Auditor

### efigure1.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** When five taper/smoother combinations are tuned to the same design resolution, their main lobes are nearly interchangeable, but their out-of-band skirts differ by ~40 dB — so the choice of taper matters only for leakage, not resolution.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — The visual carries the qualitative story (curves overlap in the center, diverge far out) but three load-bearing pieces live only in the caption: (i) the equivalent degrees of freedom ν, which is arguably the whole reason these five options are being compared, appears nowhere on the figure; (ii) the dotted vertical lines in (b) are unlabeled and read as data rather than as the ±R/2 resolution markers they are; (iii) the "same half-power width" claim cannot be verified from the plot because there is no −3 dB reference line in (b) and no half-power marker in (a). A reader also cannot tell that (a) and (b) show the *same* curves over *different* x-ranges (±10 vs ±25) without checking axes carefully.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — These are continuous functions of frequency; overlaid line plots are the right choice, and the linear/dB panel pair is the standard and correct way to show main-lobe shape and skirt decay simultaneously. No alternative would serve better.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Panel (a) outer regions (|fNΔ| ≳ 7) are flat near zero for every curve; ~30% of the panel width carries no information (this content is better resolved in (b) anyway).
- The "ideal box" drawn in dB in panel (b): outside the band the true value is −∞, so the vertical dotted segments descending to −100 dB are an artifact of plotting, not a kernel property.
- Horizontal gridlines in (a) at 0.05 intervals are unnecessary; no reader extracts numeric kernel heights.
- Legend spans two columns and crowds the top of panel (a), pushing the multitaper ripples toward the frame.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Low — The ν values duplicate Table 2, but the figure's content (lobe shape, skirt decay) is genuinely non-tabular. This is already correctly placed as an eFigure; no further demotion needed.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The caption's two quantitative claims ("nearly the same half-power width", "skirts differ by 40 dB") are unverifiable from the graphic. A reviewer cannot check either one.
- [BLOCKING] ν, the quantity that distinguishes these five kernels and motivates the comparison, is absent from the figure.
- [ADVISORY] Solid blue (multitaper) and dashed light blue (Bohman) are the two most visually similar series yet represent the two most different behaviors in (b); likewise green vs. olive are hard to separate in grayscale or for red–green deficient readers.
- [ADVISORY] Mismatched x-ranges between panels with no visual cue (e.g., shaded inset region in (b) marking the span of (a)).
- [ADVISORY] Legend appears only in (a); panel (b) requires eye travel between panels to decode six curves at their most tangled.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) is clipped at −100 dB, truncating the Bohman curve; the reader cannot tell whether it continues to fall or plateaus.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add a horizontal −3 dB line in panel (b) (or half-power tick marks in (a)) so the "same resolution" claim is readable off the plot.
- Annotate the 40 dB skirt spread directly: a vertical double-headed arrow at a fixed abscissa (e.g. fNΔ = 15) between the green and dashed-blue curves, labeled "≈40 dB".
- Append ν to each legend entry, e.g. "Bohman, then box (ν = 7.3)" — this converts the legend into the comparison table the figure is really making.
- Relabel the dotted lines in (b) as "±R/2" with a small text annotation, and drop the vertical descending segments (leave only the horizontal 0 dB box top plus short tick marks) to avoid implying finite out-of-band response.
- Narrow panel (a) to |fNΔ| ≤ 7 and remove its horizontal gridlines; the recovered width lets the multitaper ripple and the near-coincident lobes separate visually.
- Re-assign colors so the two extreme cases (untapered-then-box and Bohman-then-box) are maximally separated in hue and line style; reserve dashing for the ideal box only.
- Shade the region |fNΔ| ≤ 7 in panel (b) to show which part of (b) corresponds to (a), and repeat the legend (or place a single shared legend outside both axes).
- Extend the (b) y-limit to −120 dB or state in-panel that the Bohman curve is clipped.

### efigure2.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** Discarding the leakiest (7th) DPSS taper — or using adaptive weights, or a tapered-then-smoothed periodogram — substantially lowers the broadband leakage bias floor of a multitaper spectral estimate at comparable resolution/degrees of freedom.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the central comparison the title promises ("dropping the leakiest taper") cannot be seen. The solid ($L=7$), dashed ($L=6$), and dotted (adaptive) blue curves are drawn in the same colour and overlap into one dense band, so in panel (a) the dashed $L=6$ curve is effectively invisible and in panel (b) the reader cannot verify the caption's claim that $L=6$ "halves" the 10 dB bias floor — no distinguishable dashed curve is readable at ~5 dB. Panel (b) also has no legend, and the $\nu$ values that justify "at comparable variance" exist only in the caption text. A reader without the caption would conclude only "untapered box is badly biased; tapered estimates are better."

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate (with execution problems) — overlaid spectra plus a paired residual/bias panel is the right idiom for "establishing a relationship between estimator choice and bias across frequency," and panel (b) is the correct way to make the bias legible. However, plotting raw, highly variable realizations makes the *systematic* quantity of interest (the bias floor) hard to read. Alternatives: plot the running median or a smoothed/expected bias per estimator in (b) (optionally with a light variability envelope), or use small multiples (one mini-panel per estimator) so no curve hides another; a compact inset table of bias floor vs. $\nu$ would nail the quantitative claim.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- The grey raw periodogram in (a) occupies most of the ink and is not part of the claim; it only sets context and buries the curves that matter.
- Excess y-range in (b) (top 12 dB of the -10 to 30 axis is empty) and in (a) (single dotted excursions to -30 stretch the axis).
- Horizontal gridlines add little given the dense traces.
- Repetition of the same estimator family in three line styles of one colour creates visual clutter without conveying distinction.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panels (a) and (b) encode the same data twice; (b) alone carries the argument, and (a) mainly re-establishes what a reader of this literature already expects (periodogram is noisy, true PSD has two peaks). If space is tight, (a) is the supplement candidate, or should be shrunk to a small orientation panel.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The $L=6$ vs $L=7$ contrast — the figure's stated point — is not visually resolvable in either panel; the caption asserts a factor-of-two bias reduction that the graphic does not show.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (b) has no legend or curve labels; identification depends on colour matching to (a), which fails for the three blue variants.
- [ADVISORY] Caption says the untapered box "floors 20 dB high" but the green curve plateaus near 18 dB; align the number with the plot or state it as approximate.
- [ADVISORY] Caption's "the smoothed periodogram track[s] the truth" is ambiguous — the orange curve is a *cosine-tapered* then smoothed periodogram; "smoothed periodogram" also describes the green curve.
- [ADVISORY] Variance/resolution equivalence ($\nu$ values) is asserted only in text; the reader cannot judge whether the bias reduction is free.
- [ADVISORY] Single-realization traces mean apparent differences could be sampling noise; nothing indicates these are systematic.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Give $L=6$, $L=7$, and adaptive distinct colours (not just dash patterns), and/or split them into separate small panels.
- In panel (b), plot the smoothed/expected bias (running median across frequency, or the ensemble-mean bias from many realizations) as clean lines; show raw scatter only as a faint envelope. Add a horizontal annotation at each estimator's bias floor with its dB value.
- Add a legend (or direct inline labels) to panel (b); append $\nu$ to each legend entry, e.g. "Multitaper, $L=7$ ($\nu=14$)".
- Drop or de-emphasise the grey periodogram in (a) — plot it once, very light, or move it to the supplement; shrink (a) to a narrow context panel and give (b) the dominant width.
- Tighten both y-axes to the data range; clip or annotate the few deep dotted excursions rather than expanding the axis for them.
- Rewrite the caption's last sentence with numbers that match the rendered curves, and name the orange estimator consistently as "cosine-tapered, then smoothed".

### efigure3.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** At equal measured resolution, the smoothing methods trade variance against leakage — the methods with the most degrees of freedom (Welch, untapered-then-box) have the worst broadband leakage and side-lobe levels, while Hann- and Bohman-then-box achieve orders-of-magnitude lower leakage for a moderate loss of $\nu$.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the axes are unusually well labelled for a methods figure (panels b and c name the exact quantity), and the legend is complete, but four things are unreadable without the caption: (i) panel (a)'s y-axis is a bare "$\nu$" with no indication that higher is better or that it means equivalent degrees of freedom; (ii) the dotted vertical line is unexplained; (iii) $N=256$ and "exact, no sampling variability" appear nowhere on the graphic; (iv) most importantly, the caveat that for the Gaussian, parabolic and Welch kernels panel (c) measures the main-lobe skirt rather than a side lobe is a *category difference between curves plotted on the same axis*, and it is invisible in the figure. A reader skimming panel (c) will wrongly conclude those three methods have catastrophic side lobes.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — this is a relationship/trade-off figure (quantity vs. swept resolution), and connected line plots with log or dB response axes are the right choice. The one weakness is series count: nine encodings (three near-identical blues plus a light blue, distinguished partly by dash pattern) is at the edge of legibility, and in panel (a) the green, teal, orange and pink curves nearly coincide over most of the range, so the panel mostly communicates "$\nu$ grows linearly with width" rather than the differences between methods.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- The x-axis extension to 25 in panels (a) and (b), driven by a single Welch point; it compresses the informative 1–16 region and creates large empty regions in the upper right of both panels.
- Markers on every sweep point in panel (a), where curves overlap: they add ink without aiding identification in the crowded band.
- Horizontal gridlines in panel (a) are doing little given the near-linear curves.
- The near-linear common trend in (a) itself is arguably "noise" relative to the claim: the interesting signal is the *deviation* between methods, not the shared slope.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panels (b) and (c) produce almost the same rank ordering of methods (integrated leakage and peak side lobe are strongly correlated here), so one of them is a supplement candidate unless the text makes a distinct argument from each. Separately, the dotted line at $8/(N\Delta)$ signals that a one-dimensional slice of this figure is already tabulated in Table 2; the figure earns its place only if the *shape* of the trade-off across widths (not just the values at width 8) is used in the argument.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (c) plots two conceptually different quantities (side-lobe peak vs. main-lobe skirt) on one axis with no visual distinction. This is a correctness-of-reading problem, not a cosmetic one.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (a)'s y-axis label "$\nu$" is undefined in the graphic and its direction of merit is not indicated.
- [ADVISORY] Unexplained dotted vertical line in all three panels.
- [ADVISORY] Three blues plus a light blue distinguished by dash style; risky for colour-vision deficiency, greyscale printing, and for the overlapping region of panel (a).
- [ADVISORY] Axis ranges set by one outlying Welch point, wasting ~35% of the plotting area in (a) and (b).
- [ADVISORY] Panel (c) and panel (a) have differently scaled x-axes from panel (b)'s visual extent only by accident of shared limits; verify all three share identical x-limits and tick placement so vertical comparison across panels is exact.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- In panel (c), plot the three no-flat-top kernels (Hann-then-Gaussian, cosine-then-parabola, Welch) with open/hollow markers or a lighter line weight, and add a short in-axes annotation such as "open markers: main-lobe skirt, not a side lobe." Move the caveat out of the caption and into the graphic.
- Relabel panel (a)'s axis "equivalent degrees of freedom $\nu$ (higher = less variance)".
- Label the dotted line in place, e.g. a small rotated text "$8/(N\Delta)$ (Table 2)" next to the line in one panel.
- Truncate the x-axis at ~17 and either drop the far Welch point or show it as an arrow/annotation at the right edge; this will roughly double the effective resolution of the crowded region.
- Consider replotting panel (a) as $\nu$ normalised by the untapered-then-box baseline (or as $\nu/(\text{width}\times N\Delta)$) so the between-method differences, rather than the shared linear trend, occupy the vertical range.
- Reduce the palette to distinct hues, or highlight the two or three methods the text actually recommends in saturated colour and render the remainder in grey with direct labels; keep the full nine-series version in the supplement.
- If the text draws only one conclusion from leakage, promote either (b) or (c) to the main figure and move the other to the supplement, making this a two-panel figure and freeing the lower-right quadrant.
- Direct-label the curves at their right-hand ends in at least one panel so the legend becomes a redundancy check rather than a lookup table.

### efigure4.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: Four differently-motivated spectral estimators (Welch segment averaging, periodogram smoothing, sliding-sinc, and equal-weight multitaper) are all quadratic estimators that can be placed on a common footing — their eigen-tapers, eigen-weight ladders, degrees of freedom, and leakage kernels are strikingly similar, with the equal-weight multitaper giving the flattest weight ladder and the sliding sinc the best in-band concentration.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — the columns are labelled (top row titles) but the **rows are not labelled at all**, so a reader cannot know that row 2 is "eigen-tapers of Q", row 3 is "normalised eigenweights", row 4 is "leakage kernel" without the caption. Several other elements are caption-dependent: the `ν = 13.4 … 14.0` annotations (equivalent degrees of freedom) are unexplained; the dotted vertical lines in (m–p) at |f| = R/2 are unexplained; the resolution parameter R is never shown; panel (c) uses a different normalisation (h/max h) than (a), (b), (d) with no on-figure cue why; and the key quantitative payoff of the figure (half-power widths, out-of-band power fractions) exists *only in the caption text*, not in the graphic.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — this is a comparison across four methods × four diagnostic properties, and a small-multiples matrix with shared axes per row is the right choice. Line plots for tapers/kernels and bar charts for the discrete eigen-weight ladder both match their data. One caveat: row 1 mixes heterogeneous objects (a: shifted segment windows; b: modulated single taper; c: shifted sinc kernels; d: Slepian tapers) on axes that are not comparable, so it reads as a construction schematic rather than a comparison; consider whether that row belongs with the others or should be a separate schematic panel.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Legends appear in (b), (d), (e) only, with identical *i* = 1–4 semantics in (d) and (e); one legend per row (or a single shared legend) would suffice.
- Repeated y-axis tick labels are fine, but repeated x-axis label "*k*" on every panel of rows 1–2 and "*fNΔ*" on all of row 4 is redundant; label once per column block.
- Horizontal gridlines in (m–p) do little work given the dB axis already has ticks.
- Empty x-range out to *i* = 12 in (i) and (l) — defensible if the point is to show how many terms each method spends, but if not it is whitespace that weakens the bar comparison.
- Four separate "0.20 / 0.15 / …" y-axis label sets in row 3 when the axis is identical.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — the caption states this is "Fig. 5 in full", i.e. an expanded version of a main-text figure, so overlap is by design and appropriate for an extended-data slot. The internal risk is that rows 3 and 4 encode largely the same information twice (a flat eigenweight ladder ⇔ a boxcar-like kernel with high ν); one of the two, plus the numeric ν and half-power widths, could carry the message. The numerical results quoted in the caption (half-power widths, out-of-band power) would be better as a small 4-row table than buried in prose.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] No row labels. The figure's entire logical structure (method × property) is invisible without the caption; add left-margin row titles.
  - [BLOCKING] Key comparative numbers (half-power width, out-of-band power fraction) are in caption text only and cannot be read from the plots; they should be annotated on (m–p) or tabulated.
  - [ADVISORY] `ν` annotations and the dotted |f| = R/2 lines are unexplained on-figure; add a short in-panel key or annotate the first panel of each row.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c)'s y-axis normalisation differs from (a), (b), (d) without visual explanation, breaking the row's implied comparability.
  - [ADVISORY] Row 1 objects are not mutually comparable, which may mislead a reader who assumes shared semantics across a row.
  - [ADVISORY] Inconsistent legend placement across panels.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add row labels on the left: "Taper bank as constructed", "First four eigen-tapers of Q", "Normalised eigenweights c_i/Σc_i", "Leakage kernel K(f) (dB)".
  - Annotate (m–p) directly with half-power width and % out-of-band power; keep ν where it is and add a one-line in-figure gloss ("ν: equivalent d.o.f.") or spell it out in the row label.
  - Label the dotted lines in (m) once as "|f| = R/2" and state R in the row label or a corner annotation.
  - Consolidate legends: one legend for the *i* = 1–4 colour ramp (row 2) and one for the f′NΔ ramp (panel b); drop duplicates.
  - Put x-axis labels only on the bottom panel of each column block; drop repeated y-tick labels on columns 2–4 where axes are shared.
  - Either annotate why (c) is peak-normalised, or rescale it to match the row; if rescaling is impossible, move (c) to its own inset.
  - Move the quoted half-power-width / out-of-band numbers into a compact 4-row table adjacent to the figure or in the supplement text, so the caption carries interpretation rather than data.

### figure1.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: Three apparently different constructions — eigenvalue-weighted multitaper with all *N* Slepians, a box-smoothed untapered periodogram, and the average of sinc-window periodograms slid across the record — yield the *same* PSD estimate, exactly (to machine precision) for the box smoother and to $O(1/N_s)$ for the sliding window.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — three fixable gaps.
- Panel (a) shows three curves that coincide perfectly, which *is* the claim, but a reader glancing at it sees one curve and cannot verify that three were plotted. The equivalence has to be inferred from the legend plus panel (b). An on-plot annotation ("curves coincide to within line width") or a small zoomed inset would make (a) stand alone.
- Panel (b)'s lower trace sits at $10^{-13}$–$10^{-15}$ dB, i.e. the double-precision floor. Nothing on the figure says so, and a reader may read it as a small-but-real discrepancy rather than "exact identity, limited by arithmetic." A horizontal "machine precision" reference line or label would fix this.
- $R$, $\alpha$, $\nu$, $\lambda_i$, $N$, $N_s$ appear only in the caption. $N_s/N$ on the (c) abscissa and $N_s = 16N$ in the legend are the only in-figure anchors; the meaning of $R$ ("design resolution") is not recoverable from the plot.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — (a) and (b) are overlay/difference line plots, the right choice for "same function, three estimators"; (c) is a log–log rate plot with a slope guide, the right choice for establishing a convergence relationship. The one structural quibble is *allocation*: (a) consumes ~55% of the canvas to convey a single binary fact (they overlap), while (b), which carries the quantitative evidence, is cramped into a quarter. Consider making (a) roughly half its current height, or (a) as a small inset over (b), sharing the frequency axis.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- The inline pink annotation "Maximum |difference|" in (c) duplicates the y-axis label "max. |difference| (dB)". Delete one.
- Duplicated "Frequency (Hz)" axis label and tick labels between (a) and (b); if the panels are stacked and aligned they can share a single abscissa.
- Horizontal gridlines in (a) add little — the panel is qualitative (shape agreement), not read-off-values.
- The legend text carries parameter definitions ("weights $\lambda_i$", "box of width $R$") that are doing caption work inside the plot; shorten to "Multitaper", "Box-smoothed periodogram", "Sliding sinc" and keep the details in the caption.
- Panel (a)'s 40 Hz range with many resolved peaks is more spectral detail than the claim requires; it is attractive but not load-bearing.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — the two headline numbers ($3\times10^{-13}$ dB and $0.06$ dB) are stated in the caption, so panel (b) is partly a picture of two scalars; it survives only because it shows the differences are *flat in frequency* (not concentrated at peaks or at the band edges), which is a real and non-obvious result — say so in the caption. Panel (c) is a five-point convergence curve that a single sentence ("the sliding-window error decreases as $1/N_s$; see Table/Supplement") could carry; it is a reasonable supplement candidate if space is tight. Panel (a), if reduced to an inset, loses nothing.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (a) cannot be read as evidence of three-way agreement, since only one curve is visible. Add an explicit in-figure annotation and/or an inset zoom on a narrow band (e.g. 8–9 Hz) where the three line styles are distinguishable.
  - [BLOCKING] The $10^{-15}$ dB floor in (b) is unlabelled numerical precision. Without this, the reader cannot tell "identical" from "very close", which is the difference between an algebraic identity and an approximation — the central distinction of the figure.
  - [ADVISORY] Units on the difference axes: "|difference| (dB)" is a difference *of* dB values; state this ("|difference| in $10\log_{10}\hat S$, dB") so it is not read as a dB ratio.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) has no indication of what the dotted guide line is normalised to, so the reader cannot judge whether the slope match is fitted or asserted. The data visibly steepen past $N_s/N = 8$; if that is a real departure from $1/N_s$, note it; if it is round-off, say so.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel area allocation inverted relative to information content (see CHART TYPE).
  - [ADVISORY] The data provenance ("the authors' archive", one channel, one epoch) sits only in the caption and makes the figure anecdotal; the identity is mathematical, so consider noting in-caption that the choice of record is immaterial.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add an inset to (a) magnifying ~1 Hz of spectrum so all three line styles are separately visible; annotate "three estimates coincide".
  - Draw and label a horizontal "double-precision limit" line in (b) at $\sim10^{-14}$ dB; relabel the traces "Box $-$ multitaper (exact identity)" and "Sliding sinc $-$ multitaper ($N_s=16N$)".
  - Delete the "Maximum |difference|" annotation in (c); keep the axis label.
  - Stack (a) and (b) on a shared frequency axis, giving (a) ~35% and (b) ~35% of the height, with (c) beside (b).
  - Shorten the legend entries and move parameter definitions to the caption; drop gridlines in (a).
  - Rewrite the caption's first sentence so it leads with the claim rather than the data source, e.g. "Eigenvalue-weighted multitaper, box-smoothed periodogram, and sliding-sinc averaging are the same estimator: …"
  - If page space is constrained, move (c) to the supplement and state the $1/N_s$ rate in text.

### figure2.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Every spectral estimate is the true PSD convolved with the estimator's expected-value kernel plus noise, so tapering suppresses broadband leakage and smoothing trades variance (ν) against bias — and the best choice is a kernel about as wide as the spectral feature you want to resolve (too wide merges the peaks).

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the columns clearly read as a progression (noisy → tapered → smoothed → over-smoothed), and the top/bottom row pairing is intuitive once you know the bottom row is the kernel. But several things cannot be decoded without the caption:
- The legend says "True PSD" (black) / "Estimate" (gray), yet the estimate is **orange** in (b)–(d). The gray "Estimate" text is also rendered so light it is nearly invisible.
- `𝒦(f)` is never named in-panel; a reader does not know row 2 shows the *kernel* of the estimator directly above, nor that it is centered at an arbitrary $f\Delta=0.4$ (which has no physical relation to the peaks in row 1).
- "then parabola" in (c)/(d) is elliptical — "then" after what? (After the taper.) A non-specialist won't parse it.
- $\nu$, $R$, and $N\Delta$ are unexplained symbols; $\nu$ is arguably the most important quantity in the figure (the variance side of the trade-off) and appears only as a bare number.
- In (e)/(f) the curve is an *envelope* of side-lobe peaks, not the kernel itself — visually indistinguishable from a true curve, and a source of misreading.
- The key quantitative claims (half-power widths, % leakage outside $8/(N\Delta)$) exist only in the caption, not on the figure — the punchline "a kernel as wide as a peak is just right" is not verifiable from the plot because peak width is never marked.
- $\nu=98$ in (d) vs $\nu=98.4$ in the caption; caption lists half-power widths 0.8, 1.2, 8.2, 47.8 while panel labels give $R=8$ and $48$. Reconcile or explain.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — overlaid line plots against a common reference (true PSD) are the right choice for "compare four estimators on one realization," and the small-multiple grid with shared axes makes the column-wise progression and row-wise mechanism/consequence pairing legible. The log-y (dB) scale is essential given the 65 dB dynamic range and is used correctly. The insets are the right solution for resolving main-lobe structure that is otherwise a single pixel wide.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- The "True PSD / Estimate" legend in (a) as currently drawn: wrong color for the estimate, unreadable gray text. Either fix it or drop it in favor of direct labels on the curves.
- Blank plotting area below −20 dB in (c) and (d) (and below −50 dB in the row-2 insets of g, h) — a lot of white space, though retaining common limits for comparability is defensible; keep the shared limits, this is a low-priority trim.
- Four separate inset axes with their own tick labels add ~12 redundant numeric labels; (g) and (h) insets in particular add little beyond "the lobe is flat-topped and wide," which is already visible in the parent axes.
- Faint horizontal gridlines in row 1 serve little purpose given no value is read precisely off them.
- Repeated identical x-tick label sets across four panels per row — unavoidable with separate axes, but could be thinned.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the per-panel scalars ($\nu$, half-power width, % broadband leakage for each of four estimators) form a natural 4×3 table, and they are currently buried in prose in the caption where they are hard to compare. That table belongs in the main text (or as an inset annotation row), not in the caption. The figure itself is not redundant with anything else and is clearly a keystone figure. Insets (g) and (h) are the weakest elements and could be dropped or moved to the supplement.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Legend color/identity mismatch: "Estimate" is keyed to gray but drawn orange in (b)–(d). This is a factual error in the graphic.
- [BLOCKING] The central claim "a kernel as wide as a peak is just right" is unverifiable from the figure, because neither the peak half-power width ($7/(N\Delta)$) nor the peak separation ($30/(N\Delta)$) is marked anywhere on the plot. The reader must take the caption's word for it.
- [BLOCKING] Row 2 is uninterpretable without the caption: $\mathcal{K}(f)$ is undefined in-panel, its centering at $f\Delta=0.4$ is unexplained, and the envelope-vs-side-lobes substitution in (e)/(f) is silent.
- [ADVISORY] $\nu=98$ vs 98.4 inconsistency; panel $R$ values vs caption half-power widths need a one-line reconciliation.
- [ADVISORY] Caption is doing the work of a results paragraph (six sentences of numbers and interpretation). It will be skimmed and the numbers lost.
- [ADVISORY] Nearly invisible gray legend text will disappear entirely in print or grayscale reproduction.
- [ADVISORY] Nothing in the figure signals the *direction* of the trade-off across columns (variance down, bias up); the reader must infer it from the $\nu$ values alone.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Fix the legend: make "Estimate" orange (and gray for panel a if the colors genuinely differ), or better, delete the legend and label the black curve "true PSD" and the colored curve "estimate" directly at their right-hand ends in (a).
- In (a), add a horizontal bar or double-headed arrow annotating the peak half-power width $7/(N\Delta)$ and the peak separation $30/(N\Delta)$. Then in (e)–(h) draw the same width bar (or a shaded band of that width) against each main lobe. The claim "just right" and "too wide" then becomes visible geometry rather than caption assertion.
- Add an in-panel y-axis or corner label for row 2 reading "expected-value kernel $\mathcal{K}(f)$, dB rel. peak; centered at $f\Delta=0.4$", and annotate one of (e)/(f) with "envelope of side-lobe peaks" plus a leader line.
- Spell out the column headers as a consistent three-line block: e.g. (c) "25% cosine taper + parabola smoother / $R=8/(N\Delta)$ / $\nu=16.7$". Replace "then parabola" with the full recipe so each panel stands alone.
- Move the numeric summary (half-power width, % leakage beyond $8/(N\Delta)$, $\nu$) out of the caption into a 4-row table, or print each panel's leakage % in its own corner. Cut the caption to the mechanism sentence plus the four panel identifications.
- Add a light arrow or bracket spanning the top of the columns labeled "increasing smoothing: variance ↓, bias ↑" to make the trade-off axis explicit.
- Drop the insets in (g) and (h), or shrink all four insets and label them once ("main lobe, magnified"), removing duplicated tick labels.
- Reconcile the 98/98.4 and $R$/half-power-width discrepancies.

---

### figure3.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** UNCLEAR — the figure carries two claims under one title. Panel (b)'s claim is clear and strong: *the RMS error of a smoothed periodogram has a broad minimum where falling noise crosses rising bias, and that optimum shifts to finer resolution when only the spectral peaks are scored.* Panel (a)'s claim — *a 25% cosine taper suppresses the record's ends* — is a different, prior step in the pipeline and is not "choosing the resolution." As drawn, the figure has no single thesis.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No —
- Panel (b) is close to self-sufficient: direct on-curve labels ("Bias", "Noise", "Total, peak region", "Total, whole band"), annotated extremes ("too noisy", "just right", "too smooth"), and reference lines ("peak width", "peak separation") all do their work without the caption. This is the strongest part of the figure.
- But the x-axis quantity $RN\Delta$ is unexplained on the plot; a reader cannot tell whether large means smooth or rough without following the annotations.
- $\nu = 2, 17, 98$ appear in annotations but $\nu$ appears on no axis and is nowhere defined graphically. The reader must guess that $\nu$ is equivalent degrees of freedom tied monotonically to $R$.
- The three circled points are defined only by cross-reference to another figure ("panels (b), (c) and (d) of Fig. motif"). Purely caption-dependent.
- In panel (a) the y-axis simultaneously carries data amplitude and a taper that has been "scaled to the record's peak amplitude" — an arbitrary rescaling that gives the axis two incompatible meanings. The dashed envelope looks like a data bound, not a weighting function on [0,1].
- In panel (a) the black trace is visible only at the edges, so it reads as a separate high-amplitude edge artifact rather than as the same record before tapering.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate (with one mismatch inside panel a) —
- Panel (b) is a relationship/tradeoff claim and the crossing-curve line plot is exactly the right idiom. Keep it.
- Panel (a) is process illustration. A trace overlay is reasonable, but overlaying a rescaled taper on the data axis is a mismatch. Better: a two-row stack (record on top, taper on a 0–1 axis in the middle, tapered record at bottom) or a twin right-hand axis labelled "taper weight, 0–1".

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Faint horizontal gridlines in panel (b) — the annotated crossings, not gridline readout, carry the claim.
- The rescaled dashed taper envelope duplicated above and below zero: one half conveys the same information.
- Mixed marker shapes on the *same* solid black curve (filled squares at low $R$, filled circles at high $R$) — reads as two series where there is one.
- Dense tick labels 0/255/511/767/1023 in panel (a); the exact sample index is irrelevant to the claim. "0" and "N = 1024" suffice.
- Panel (a)'s three-part header ("Record / Tapered record / Taper (dashed)") functions as an inline legend and is fine, but "Record" points at a trace the reader can barely see.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium-High — the caption states panel (a) is "the record of Fig. motif," so the raw trace is already published earlier in the manuscript, and the three circled points in (b) are themselves panels of that same earlier figure. Panel (a) is therefore largely a re-display. The bias and noise gray curves are smooth analytic functions that could equally live in text as a sentence; only their crossing matters.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Two unrelated claims (tapering; resolution selection) share one figure and one caption titled "Choosing the resolution." Panel (a) does not support that title.
- [BLOCKING] $\nu$ is annotated but never mapped to an axis, so the reader cannot convert between the plotted abscissa $RN\Delta$ and the degrees of freedom used to label the three regimes.
- [BLOCKING] The circled points are identifiable only via cross-reference to another figure; the visualization cannot be read alone.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a)'s y-axis conflates signal amplitude with a rescaled taper weight.
- [ADVISORY] Inconsistent markers within the solid "Total, whole band" curve suggest a spurious second series.
- [ADVISORY] $RN\Delta$ is undefined on the plot; no indication of direction ("finer → coarser resolution").
- [ADVISORY] The caption's disclaimer that curves "carry no error bars" signals that a reader would reasonably expect scatter across the 300 realizations; absence of any spread indication is a visual omission, not just a caption note.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Promote panel (b) to a standalone figure titled "Choosing the resolution." Move panel (a) either into the earlier tapering figure or to the supplement; if it must stay, retitle the whole figure to cover both steps (e.g., "Taper and resolution").
- Add a secondary top axis to panel (b) giving $\nu$, so "too noisy, $\nu=2$" and "too smooth, $\nu=98$" are readable off the plot.
- Annotate the abscissa direction under the axis label: "← finer resolution | coarser resolution →".
- Replace the cross-reference to Fig. motif with three small inset thumbnails (or labels "b", "c", "d" plus a one-word gloss) at the circled points, so the figure stands alone.
- Unify markers on the solid curve to a single symbol; reserve open symbols exclusively for the dashed peak-region curve.
- In panel (a), plot the taper on a right-hand 0–1 axis (or as a shaded band behind the data), show only the upper half, and draw the untapered record in light gray across its full length so the reader sees it is the same series.
- Drop the horizontal gridlines; keep the two vertical reference lines, which are doing real interpretive work.
- Consider a light shaded ribbon (e.g., interquartile range across the 300 realizations) on the total-error curves, which would let you delete the "no error bars" disclaimer and would visually justify the word "broad" in "broad minimum."

### figure4.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** Summing the Slepian eigenspectra — each taper filling the spectral gaps left by the previous ones — builds a kernel that converges to a box of width $R$, so a multitaper spectral estimate is effectively a band-average over $|f|\le R/2$.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the visual logic (a → b → c) is readable, but the reader cannot decode the axes without the text. Specifically: $\alpha$, $R$, $\lambda_i$, $\mathcal{K}(f)$, $H^{(i)}$ and Fejér's kernel are all undefined on the graphic; panel (d)'s claim ("tapers beyond $2\alpha$ carry only leakage") is carried entirely by the caption, not by the plot, since the reader has no anchor for why $1-\lambda_i\approx 1$ matters. Panel (c)'s punchline — that the $L=N$ curve and the box are indistinguishable — is also invisible: the dashed "Box" line is hidden under the thick black curve, so the most important claim in the figure reads as "one line is missing."

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — (a)–(c) are function-vs-axis line plots, correct for showing shape and accumulation; (c)'s sequential-darkening overlay is the right device for "change as $L$ grows"; (d) is a semilog trend for establishing a threshold relationship. No mismatches.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Panel (a): y-limits ±0.20 for curves that never exceed ±0.12 — roughly 40% of the panel is empty; the legend is parked in that void.
- Panel (b): the $|fN\Delta|>4$ region is flat zero across ~half the panel width.
- Panel (b) and (c): horizontal gridlines add ink without supporting any read-off (no quantitative comparison is being made against specific y-values).
- Panel (d): y-axis extends to $10^{-16}$ while the data bottom out at $\sim10^{-10}$; x-axis runs to 17 with six visually identical saturated points.
- Panel (d): open circle markers on a monotone smooth curve are decorative rather than informative.
- Panel (c): the "Box" dashed entry in the legend is effectively invisible in the plot.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panel (b) is largely subsumed by panel (c): the $L=1$ curve of (c) *is* the $i=1$ kernel, and (c)'s progressive filling already conveys the "gap-filling" mechanism. Panel (b) survives only as a pedagogical zoom on the first three terms; if space is tight it is the natural supplement candidate. Panel (a) is setup rather than evidence and could also move, provided $h^{(7)}$'s role is retained somewhere to motivate (d).

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Caption/axis notation disagree. Caption says the kernels in (b) are $|H^{(i)}(f)|^2/\Delta$; the axis reads $|H^{(i)}(f)|^2/N\Delta^2$. Caption says (c) plots $\sum_{i\le L}\lambda_i|H^{(i)}|^2/2\alpha\Delta$; the axis reads $\mathcal{K}(f)/N\Delta$. A reader cannot verify the normalisation that makes the box claim work.
- [BLOCKING] The central claim of (c) — $L=N$ is *exactly* the box (convolved with Fejér) — is not visible, because the dashed box is occluded by the thick black curve. The figure currently shows "black curve approximately flat," not "black curve equals box."
- [ADVISORY] Colour mapping is inconsistent across panels: $i=1,2$ are black/blue in both (a) and (b), but the third series is orange in (a) ($i=7$) and green in (b) ($i=3$). Readers will assume orange and green are the same taper family index.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (d) labels a vertical line in an $i$-indexed plot as "$L=2\alpha-1$", mixing the taper-count variable $L$ with the taper-index variable $i$.
- [ADVISORY] The caption is doing explanatory work (three sentences of derivation in panel c) that belongs in the body text; the figure should be able to assert the claim on its own.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (c) legend has seven entries in a three-column block that overlaps the plot's top margin, and the $L$ values (1,3,5,7,9,N) are not the same set illustrated elsewhere, so it is unclear whether $L=7$ here is the "usual" choice highlighted in (d).

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Reconcile caption and axis labels on a single normalisation; state it once, in the axis label, and drop the formula from the caption.
- In (c), make the box the visible reference: plot it as a wide light-grey band or plot it *on top* with a contrasting dash, and/or add a small inset showing the $L=N$ minus box residual (or a zoom on the shoulder near $fN\Delta=4$) so "indistinguishable at this scale" is demonstrated rather than asserted.
- Annotate directly on the panels: "band $|f|\le R/2$" beside the dotted lines in (b)/(c); "usual choice $L=2\alpha-1$" on the relevant curve in (c); "beyond here: leakage only" with an arrow in (d). This converts three caption sentences into on-figure text.
- Tighten limits: (a) to roughly ±0.14 with the legend moved outside or to a corner; (b) x to ±5; (d) y to $10^{-11}$–$10^{0}$ and x to ~12.
- Harmonise colours across (a) and (b), or use a single sequential ramp keyed to $i$ in both, so colour consistently encodes taper index.
- Remove horizontal gridlines from (b) and (c), and drop the markers in (d) except at the two highlighted indices ($2\alpha-1$, $2\alpha$).
- Consider demoting (a) and (b) to the supplement and promoting (c)+(d) to a two-panel main figure; the argument "the sum is a box, and only the first $2\alpha$ tapers matter" is then delivered in two reads instead of four.
- Rename the vertical lines in (d) to "$i=2\alpha-1$" and "$i=2\alpha$" to keep index and count variables distinct.
---

### figure5.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: Four different routes to a multitaper-like spectral estimate (Welch averaging, periodogram smoothing, a slid sinc window, and Slepian multitapers) at matched bandwidth are all equivalent to a bank of tapers with an eigen-weight ladder, and the flatness of that ladder — not the route — determines the achieved degrees of freedom ν (13.4–17.2).

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — the column layout, shared colours, and the in-panel ν annotations do a lot of work, and a reader can see "four methods, four taper banks, four weight ladders, four ν values." But three things cannot be recovered without the caption: (i) that all four are matched at the same bandwidth R and record length N (the central premise of the comparison); (ii) what ν *means* and which direction is better; (iii) what the lighter shades encode in (a), where there is no legend at all, and why (b) indexes by frequency shift (f′NΔ), (c) by unlabelled slide position, and (d) by taper index i — three different legend semantics for what the caption calls the same thing ("later members of the bank"). The y-axis of (c) is normalized by max h while (a), (b), (d) are not, so the top row cannot be compared vertically even though its visual parallelism invites it.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — the figure has two purposes and uses the right idiom for each: overlaid line plots for the shape/placement of the taper bank, and bar charts for a discrete, ordered set of weights. Bars are correct for c_i (discrete index, magnitude comparison, flatness read at a glance). The 2×4 small-multiple grid with fixed y-limits in the bottom row is the right structure for a four-way comparison.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Repeated y-axis tick labels on (f), (g), (h) despite an identical shared scale — keep them only on (e).
- Repeated "k" and "i" x-axis labels on every panel; one per row (or per outer column) suffices.
- Repeated y tick labels on (b)–(d) in the top row; since the top-row y-scales are not comparable anyway, the numeric values there carry little claim-relevant information — the shapes do.
- Panel (a): seven near-identical Hann bumps in a single hue convey "seven shifted copies"; five would convey it equally and reduce overplotting clutter at the crossings.
- Empty right-hand region of (e) and (h) (bars stop at 7 while the axis runs past 10) — defensible for cross-panel comparability, but it reads as missing data unless the shared axis is made explicit.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Low — the four ν values could be a one-line table, but their point here is the visual correlation between ladder flatness and ν, which a table cannot show. The deferral of eigen-tapers and kernels to Fig. S4 is the right call. Mild risk that the top row duplicates a schematic or earlier method figure; if any of (a)–(d) has already been drawn elsewhere in the paper, drop it here.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Matched bandwidth R and record length N are the premise of the comparison but appear nowhere in the graphic. Annotate R (and N) once in the figure (e.g. a shared header or a bandwidth bar) so the "same resolution, different ν" claim is visible without the caption.
  - [BLOCKING] Legend semantics are inconsistent across (a)–(d), and (a) and (c) have no legend. The caption says all four panels show "later members of the bank," but the graphic indexes them by three different quantities. Unify to a single shade-ordered index (i = 1…4) with the physical meaning stated in a sub-label.
  - [BLOCKING] ν is annotated but never oriented. A reader cannot tell whether 17.2 is good. Add a direction cue (e.g. "flatter ladder → larger ν", or a reference line/marker at the ideal ν = 2L).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c)'s different normalization (h/max h, y-range 0–1) breaks the visual parallelism of the top row and can be misread as "much larger tapers." Either normalize all four the same way or visually separate (c) so no comparison is implied.
  - [ADVISORY] The causal link from the top row to the bottom row is implied only by column position and hue. An explicit cue (a downward arrow or a shared column rule, plus repeating the method name as a small label in the bottom row) would make each column readable as a unit.
  - [ADVISORY] Bottom-row x-axes lack a tick at i = 1, so the first bar's index must be inferred.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) is conceptually the most loaded (it is exactly the λ-weighted all-taper estimate, per the caption) yet is drawn identically to the others; its special status is invisible.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add a shared annotation band above the figure stating N = 256 and R = 8/(NΔ) once, so bandwidth matching is graphical rather than caption-only.
  - Harmonize the top row: same normalization, same y-limits, tick labels only on (a), and a single legend convention "i = 1, 2, 3, 4 (lighter = later)" with the per-panel meaning of i as a short sub-label.
  - Label each column with its method name in *both* rows (short forms: "Welch", "Smoothed", "Sinc slide", "Slepian") so (e)–(h) are self-identifying.
  - Move ν from a bare number to an oriented statement, e.g. "ν = 17.2 of 24 possible" or add a dashed horizontal reference line showing the flat-ladder ideal, so the flatness→ν mechanism is read off the bars.
  - Strip duplicated tick labels and axis titles; one y-label and one x-label per row.
  - Consider adding a thin arrow or bracket between rows on one column only, captioned "weights derived from bank", to establish the top→bottom relation once and let the reader generalize.
  - Fix the shared bottom-row axis explicitly (light gridline or an "i ≤ 12" note) so the blank space in (e) and (h) reads as "no further tapers" rather than truncation.

### figure6.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: At matched half‑power width, the shape of a spectral estimator's smoothing kernel — specifically its side‑lobe/skirt level — determines how badly the estimate is biased upward in the low‑power part of the spectrum, so the cosine‑taper‑plus‑parabola "recipe" and Welch's method track the true PSD while the untapered box and 7‑taper multitaper level off 20 and 10 dB too high.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — the visual logic (top row = estimate vs. truth; bottom row = the kernel that produced it) is legible at a glance and the small-multiple layout invites exactly the right column-wise comparison. But three things stop a caption-free read: (i) "Recipe (b′)" is an internal cross-reference with no meaning on the page; (ii) ν and 𝒦(f) are never defined or unitted in the figure, and ν is the quantity that certifies "matched resolution," which is the whole premise of the comparison; (iii) the headline result — *how far* the green and blue curves sit above the black one, and that this floor is caused by the kernel skirt level visible in (e)/(f) — is left entirely to prose. The reader must be told the punchline rather than seeing it.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — overlaid line plots for spectrum-vs-truth and a matched column of kernel plots is the right pairing for a "mechanism → consequence" argument, and small multiples with shared axes are the correct way to compare four estimators. The insets are a legitimate use of a second scale (flat-top shape vs. far-field skirt differ by 100 dB). No change of chart family needed.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Bottom-row y-range extends to −150 dB, but the claim lives between 0 and −60 dB; most of the panel height is unused descent, which visually shrinks the passband/skirt region that matters.
- Top-row y-range extends to −40 dB although no curve goes below about −22 dB.
- Four different estimate colors encode nothing (one variable, four panels); the color variety implies a categorical distinction that is already carried by the panel titles.
- Faint horizontal gridlines add ink without being read off in any comparison.
- Inset axis tick labels (0.39/0.4/0.41, −25/−50) are repeated four times identically.
- Repeated identical y tick labels are already suppressed in columns 2–4 — good — but the repeated x-axis labels *fΔ* on all four bottom panels could collapse to one.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — the four ν values, four half-power widths, four leakage percentages, and two sets of four median-absolute-error numbers in the caption are all tabular content, and the caption itself points to Table~\ref{tab:ensemble}. The single-realization errors risk conflicting with the ensemble table (11.6 vs. 5.3 dB) and the caption has to apologize for it ("more leakage than is typical"). The kernels in (e–h) also overlap with Fig.~\ref{fig:motif}, which apparently already shows kernels in this style.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] "Recipe (b′)" in panel (c) is unreadable outside the manuscript's internal numbering; a reader scanning the figure cannot tell what method is being advocated.
  - [BLOCKING] The central quantitative claim — the 20 dB and 10 dB bias floors — is invisible in the figure and exists only in the caption. Nothing marks the level-off, and no shared reference line lets the eye measure it.
  - [BLOCKING] ν appears as the matched-resolution credential in every panel but is never defined or given units anywhere in the figure; likewise 𝒦(f).
  - [ADVISORY] Showing one atypical realization ("more leakage than is typical") as the primary evidence undercuts the claim; the caption must immediately walk it back with ensemble numbers.
  - [ADVISORY] The kernels are all centered at f = 0.4 with no indication of why that frequency, or of where the smoothing bandwidth R sits, so the link between "skirt at −22 dB" and "bias at high f" is not drawn.
  - [ADVISORY] The estimate in (b) appears to terminate slightly short of f = 0.5 relative to the other panels, which reads as a plotting artifact.
  - [ADVISORY] The "True PSD / Estimate" key is text-in-panel in (a) only; with four different estimate colors, a reader landing on (d) has no key.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Rename panel (c) to a self-describing label, e.g. "Cosine taper + parabola smoother," and keep the "(b′)" cross-reference in the caption only.
  - Annotate the bias directly: in (a) and (b), add a short horizontal bracket or arrow at high f labeled "+20 dB" and "+10 dB," and mark the corresponding skirt level (−22 dB) with a dashed line in (f) and its absence in (g). This makes the mechanism→consequence link visual and lets the figure be read before the caption.
  - Clip the bottom row to roughly 0 to −60 dB (or −80) and the top row to 60 to −30 dB; the reclaimed vertical space enlarges exactly the features under discussion.
  - Add a ν definition to the axis or a one-line in-figure note ("ν: equivalent degrees of freedom; all four kernels matched to ≈8/(NΔ) half-power width"), and label the bottom-row y axis as "kernel 𝒦(f) (dB)."
  - Draw a light vertical line or shaded band at |f − 0.4| ≤ R in (e–h) so the "power outside |f| ≤ R" statistic in the caption is readable off the plot.
  - Collapse the four estimate colors to one accent color (or keep color but add a single shared legend), and drop the horizontal gridlines.
  - Move the per-realization median absolute errors out of the caption into Table~\ref{tab:ensemble} alongside the ensemble values, and consider replacing this single realization with a median-plus-quantile-ribbon over the 300 realizations, with the single realization moved to the supplement.
  - Confirm the (b) curve is drawn over the full 0–0.5 range, and if it genuinely is truncated, say why in the caption.

### figure7.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: Three practically-used spectrum estimators (7-taper multitaper, smoothed cosine-taper periodogram, and 9-segment Welch), matched in resolution and degrees of freedom, agree to within their own sampling noise on the same EEG epoch, with residual disagreement confined to the valleys between harmonics.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — Panel (a) does convey "the curves lie on top of each other" at a glance, and panel (b) shows where they differ. But the load-bearing part of the claim, *within their own noise*, is invisible: the ±1.5–1.6 dB noise level lives only in the caption. A reader looking at panel (b) sees excursions to +12 dB and has no reference against which to judge "agreement." Secondary gaps: "Recipe (b′)" is internal shorthand that means nothing without the text; the dB reference (relative to what?) is unstated; the "valleys between harmonics" interpretation cannot be recovered from panel (b) alone because the two panels are not visually linked at the relevant frequencies.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — Overlay plus difference panel is the right idiom for an agreement claim (essentially a Bland–Altman in the frequency domain), and putting the residuals on their own axis is what makes near-coincident curves interpretable. Shared x-axis is correct. No change needed to the chart family.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Panel (b) y-range spans −15 to +15 dB while the data occupy roughly −6 to +12; the bottom 40% of the panel is empty, shrinking the features that matter.
- Duplicate legend in panel (b): the colour encoding is already established in (a), and the second legend restates it with *different wording* ("Recipe (b′) − multitaper" vs "Recipe (b′)"), which costs the reader a re-mapping step.
- Horizontal gridlines in both panels add little; in (a) they compete with the many narrow peaks.
- The zero line in (b) is drawn heavier than the data, which is inverted emphasis.
- Frequencies above ~30 Hz add little to the claim beyond what 0–30 Hz already shows; not noise exactly, but low information density.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — The caption carries six summary statistics (median absolute differences, 95th percentiles, three half-power widths, three ν values) that are not readable from the figure; those belong in a small table or as on-plot annotations, not buried in prose. Panel (a) also re-plots the same EEG epoch as the earlier abstract figure, so it partly duplicates established material. Panel (a) is arguably the weaker half: three indistinguishable curves is a null-result visual, and panel (b) alone (plus a noise band) carries the claim. Consider demoting (a) to a thumbnail inset or the supplement.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] The comparison threshold is absent. Without a drawn ±1.6 dB noise band (or equivalent), panel (b) cannot support the claim "agree to within their own noise" — the figure shows differences but not that they are small in the relevant sense.
  - [BLOCKING] "Recipe (b′)" is undefined within the figure, making the orange curve uninterpretable to any reader who has not just read the methods section.
  - [ADVISORY] The "largest differences are in the valleys" claim is asserted in the caption but not marked in the figure; the reader must eyeball-align two panels across a 40 Hz axis.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b)'s y-range wastes ~40% of the panel and compresses the features.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a)'s y-axis has no stated reference level for dB.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend wording is inconsistent between panels.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add a shaded horizontal band at ±1.6 dB in panel (b), labelled in-plot as "estimator noise, 4.3√(2/ν)". This single change converts the figure from "here are the differences" to "the differences are below noise" and makes the main claim readable without the caption.
  - Rescale panel (b) to roughly −7 to +13 dB.
  - Rename the orange series to something self-describing, e.g. "Smoothed periodogram (b′)", and delete the redundant legend in (b), keeping only a single legend in (a) with ν and half-power width appended to each label.
  - Mark the harmonic valleys — e.g. faint vertical bands, or a small inset zoom on one valley near 9 Hz showing the multitaper curve filling in while the other two dip — so the mechanism claim is visible.
  - Move the numeric summaries (ν, half-power width, median |Δ|, 95th percentile) into a three-row inset table or an annotation block; reserve the caption for interpretation.
  - Lighten or remove gridlines and thin the zero line so the data carries the most ink.
  - Consider truncating the x-axis at 30 Hz, or keeping 40 Hz but noting that the high-frequency region is included to show the agreement persists as SNR falls.

### figure8.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** At comparable spectral resolution, a smoothed periodogram ("Recipe b′", ν = 8.7) gives a spectrogram and spectra essentially indistinguishable from — but statistically more stable than — the multitaper estimate, while both vastly out-perform the raw periodogram, on real ictal EEG.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the visual comparison (c vs e vs g, and d/f) reads well on its own, but three elements block a caption-free reading:
- "Recipe (b′)" is an opaque internal cross-reference; a reader arriving at Fig. 8 cannot know what estimator this is. Replace with a descriptive label ("smoothed periodogram, 2 Hz parabola").
- ν = 2.0 / 6.0 / 8.7 is the quantitative crux of the claim (equivalent resolution, unequal stability) but ν is never defined in the figure. Without it, panels e and g look like two arbitrary smooth spectrograms.
- Panel (h) is uninterpretable alone: "Multitaper − smoothed" does not say which panels are differenced, there is no zero reference line, no mean/median/IQR marked, and no cue as to what "good" looks like (a narrow peak at 0). The reader cannot tell whether ±5 dB tails are negligible or alarming.
- Panel (d) has no legend (it lives in f); colour identification requires eye-travel across the figure. Also the 395–410 s discontinuity/flat segment in (a), which produces the conspicuous banding in all three spectrograms, is unexplained and will read as a processing bug.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate, with one weak link — spectrograms for time–frequency structure, overlaid line spectra for estimator-to-estimator comparison, and a histogram for the aggregate difference are all correct matches to purpose. The weak link is (h): a marginal histogram discards exactly the information the reader wants, namely *where* in time–frequency the two estimators disagree. A difference **spectrogram** (multitaper − smoothed, diverging colour map, symmetric limits) on the same axes as c/e/g would answer "are they equivalent?" far more directly, with the histogram as an inset or supplement. Also consider replacing the three near-identical spectrograms with (e) plus a difference panel if page space is tight, since side-by-side judgement of speckle across three panels is a task the eye does poorly.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Three identical colourbars (10–45 dB) for c, e, g — keep one shared bar at the right.
- Panel (b): six seconds of ictal waveform is charming but does no work for the estimator comparison; its content is already implied by the shaded burst in (a) and the 8 Hz peak in (f).
- Repeated x-axis tick labels on c and e (a shared "Time (s)" axis under g suffices).
- Faint horizontal gridlines in d/f add little against data curves.
- Redundant "(d)"/"(f)" callouts appear both as triangles in (a) and as dashed lines in three spectrograms; dashed lines in one spectrogram plus the triangles would be enough.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — (c) functions mainly as a straw man and may already appear in earlier figures of the paper if the periodogram's variance was demonstrated there; (b) is decorative context; (h) partially restates what d/f already show qualitatively. If an earlier figure establishes periodogram variance, (c) is a supplement candidate.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] "Recipe (b′)" is unreadable outside the manuscript's internal numbering; the figure's central estimator is effectively unnamed.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (h) lacks a zero line, central-tendency marker, and explicit statement of which panels are differenced, so the equivalence claim it is meant to carry cannot be read from the graphic.
- [BLOCKING] ν, the quantity that makes the comparison fair and the claim quantitative, is undefined anywhere in the figure.
- [ADVISORY] The claim implies matched resolution, but the bandwidths are not matched (0.5 / 1.7 / 2.0 Hz) and ν differs (6.0 vs 8.7); a reader may object that the smoothed estimate simply trades more resolution for more stability. The figure should make the trade-off explicit rather than implying parity.
- [ADVISORY] Unexplained signal dropout at ~395–410 s reads as an artifact/error.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (d) has no legend; colour-blind readers additionally face blue/orange/pink/grey with no linestyle differentiation.
- [ADVISORY] Jet-like colour map is perceptually non-uniform and manufactures false banding — a real hazard when the claim is about visual smoothness.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Rename panels: "Periodogram (ν = 2)", "Multitaper, L = 3 (ν = 6)", "Smoothed periodogram, 2 Hz (ν = 8.7)", and gloss ν once in-figure as "equivalent degrees of freedom".
- Replace (h) with a diverging difference spectrogram (multitaper − smoothed, ±8 dB), and if the histogram is retained, add a zero line and annotate median and 5–95% range.
- Collapse the three colourbars into one shared bar; drop duplicated tick labels on c and e; switch to a perceptually uniform map (viridis/turbo-calibrated or magma).
- Add the four-entry legend to (d) as well, and differentiate curves by linestyle as well as colour.
- Cut panel (b) (or move to supplement) and reclaim the space for the difference spectrogram.
- Mark or annotate the 395–410 s dropout as an acquisition artifact so it is not mistaken for an estimator failure.
- State the resolution–stability trade-off in-figure (e.g. a small table or inline text: "bandwidth 1.7 vs 2.0 Hz, ν 6.0 vs 8.7") so the fairness of the comparison is visible without the caption.

### figure9.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: UNCLEAR — the figure is carrying two claims at once: (i) window length trades time resolution against frequency resolution for sleep spindles (rows), and (ii) the smoothed cosine-tapered periodogram ("recipe b′") attains a comparable half-power width with substantially higher equivalent degrees of freedom than the practical multitaper rule, with the gap largest at 1 s / small α (columns + panel h). Either is publishable; as drawn, the reader does not know which is the point.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No —
- "Recipe (b′)" is an internal cross-reference; a reader arriving at the figure cannot know what estimator this is. The left column is self-labelling ("Multitaper, L=3") while the right column is not.
- The white rectangle is the single most important piece of interpretive machinery in b–g and is completely unexplained on the graphic. Readers will read it as a region-of-interest marker, not a resolution cell.
- ν appears in six panel titles and in (h) with no on-figure gloss; "equivalent degrees of freedom" exists only in the caption.
- The grey shading in (a) is never identified as spindles on the figure, and the shaded epochs are not carried up into b–g (no vertical guides), so the reader cannot check whether the five spindles are actually better resolved in one column than the other.
- The 11–16 Hz spindle band is stated in (a) but never marked on the spectrograms, so the frequency range that the whole argument is about is not visually delimited.
- (h)'s series legend ("Multitaper" / "Smoothing") is tucked under the bottom row only and reads as an annotation of that row rather than a legend.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — spectrogram grid for the time–frequency trade-off, dumbbell/lollipop for paired within-setting comparison, and a raw trace for provenance are all correct choices. Two caveats:
- The jet colormap introduces false contour bands (visible as the ring structure in panel b) and will make the multitaper panel look noisier than it is for reasons unrelated to the estimator. Use a perceptually uniform map (viridis/cividis, or a sequential "hot"-type map).
- Hard clipping to dark red below ~4 Hz in every panel destroys a fifth of each image. Either crop the frequency axis to ~4–25 Hz, or plot whitened/1-f-corrected PSD so the dynamic range fits the scale.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Saturated sub-4 Hz band in all six spectrograms (no information, large area).
- Repeated x-axis tick labels on b, c, d, e (only the bottom row needs them); repeated y-axis labels on the right column.
- Numeric parameters duplicated between panel titles (b–g) and panel (h).
- Gridlines in (h) at 0.25 Hz intervals are finer than the effect being read.
- The "α=1, L=1: small-α regime" annotation floats in the middle of row 1 in (h) without a clear anchor.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — panel (h) restates the six "X s by Y Hz, ν=Z" strings already printed as panel titles, so one of the two is deletable. More substantively, six spectrograms are shown to support a claim whose quantitative content is entirely in (h); if the paper's argument is the estimator comparison, two spectrograms (the 1 s pair, where the gap is largest) plus (h) carry it, and the 2 s and 4 s pairs belong in the supplement. If the argument is the window-length trade-off, then (h) is the supplementary item.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Two competing claims in one figure; decide whether rows or columns are the message and demote the other axis of variation.
  - [BLOCKING] The white resolution box — the key to reading b–g — has no on-figure explanation or label.
  - [BLOCKING] "Recipe (b′)" is uninterpretable standalone; replace with a descriptive label.
  - [ADVISORY] Jet colormap plus low-frequency clipping biases the visual comparison between the two columns.
  - [ADVISORY] Spindle epochs marked in (a) are not traceable into the spectrograms, so the reader cannot verify the spindle-specific claim.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (h) legend placement reads as a row annotation.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Pick one claim for the title/caption's first sentence. If it is the estimator comparison, retitle the columns "Multitaper (L tapers)" and "Cosine taper + parabolic smoothing" as column headers above the grid, and state the claim as e.g. "Smoothing matches multitaper resolution with ~2× the degrees of freedom, most clearly at 1 s."
  - Label the white box directly in panel (b) with a short leader: "resolution cell: window × half-power width".
  - Draw faint vertical bands at the five spindle times through all six spectrograms, or add a horizontal 11–16 Hz bracket on the left edge of each row, so the claim is checkable.
  - Switch to a perceptually uniform colormap and either crop to 4–25 Hz or whiten the PSD so nothing clips.
  - Strip the numeric parameters from the b–g titles (keep only window length and estimator) and let (h) carry the numbers; or vice versa.
  - Move the Multitaper/Smoothing key to a proper legend box in (h) and drop the 0.25 Hz gridlines to 0.5 Hz.
  - Remove interior tick labels; keep one x-axis label per column and one y-axis label per row.
  - Consider moving the 2 s and 4 s pairs to the supplement and keeping a three-panel main figure: (a) trace, (b) 1 s multitaper vs smoothing, (c) the dumbbell summary.

---

## Composition & Layout Inspector

### efigure1.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Good — spines appear reduced (left/bottom only), legend is frameless, gridlines are faint horizontal rules clearly lighter than the curves, no fills, shadows, or 3D effects. Minor redundancy: the ideal box is drawn twice (panel a as a full box, panel b as two vertical dotted lines) but this is defensible since each panel shows a different aspect of it.

READING FLOW:     Natural — (a) → (b), left to right; the legend sits in panel (a) so it is encountered before the data that needs it. One friction point: the x-ranges differ (±10 vs ±25) with no visual cue linking the two, so the reader must re-read tick labels to realize (b) is a zoom-out, not the same window.

CHARTJUNK:
  - None substantive. The only candidate is the two-column legend, which is more lookup cost than ornament (see LEGEND STYLE).

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate — the empty lower corners of panel (a) are data (kernel ≈ 0), not waste. The legend efficiently occupies the otherwise-empty headroom above the main lobe. Panel (b)'s near-0 dB region is compressed but that is the nature of a dB axis.

AXES:             OK — (a) y begins at 0 (correct for a non-negative kernel density); (b) is dB relative to peak, a documented log-scale exception, so a non-zero origin is appropriate. Symmetric x-axes about 0 are correct for a frequency kernel. One issue: the (b) y-axis floor at −100 dB clips the Bohman (dashed light blue) curve on both sides — the curve runs into the axis boundary and its true behaviour is hidden.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate; these are deterministic kernels, not estimates. No action needed.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a) and (b) both present, both outside the axes at top-left, same parenthetical style and size. Consider bolding for consistency with most journal styles, but this is cosmetic.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — each panel is roughly 2:1 wide, which suits spectral kernels whose structure is in the frequency direction. The wide format also resolves the multitaper ripple in (b), which a square panel would smear.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — no callouts, text boxes, or brackets; no overlap with data. The degrees-of-freedom values (ν = 14.0, 16.7, 17.2, 8.9, 7.3) currently live only in the caption, forcing a caption→figure lookup for the single most comparable number in the figure.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box (frameless, 6 entries, 2 columns) — does not overlap any data (curves peak at ≈0.135; legend sits above 0.15). With six series a detached key is defensible, but the two-column layout is filled column-major (Ideal / Multitaper / Untapered, then Cosine-parabola / Hann / Bohman) while readers scan row-major, and this order also does not match the caption's order. Panel (b)'s skirts are widely separated at both edges, so direct labeling is feasible there.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Bohman curve is clipped by the −100 dB y-limit in panel (b); the reader cannot tell whether the curve continues or the data end.
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend imposes a lookup cost for both panels, and its column-major order conflicts with row-major reading and with the caption's enumeration order.
  - [ADVISORY] Different x-ranges between (a) and (b) are not visually linked; the relationship between the two views must be inferred from tick labels.
  - [ADVISORY] "Untapered" (green) and "Hann" (olive) are close in hue and may be difficult to separate in print or for colour-vision-deficient readers, especially where the two curves cross near |fNΔ| ≈ 4 in panel (a).
  - [ADVISORY] Key quantitative information (ν per kernel) is only in prose, not on the graphic.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Extend the panel (b) y-limit to −110 or −120 dB, or add a short note/tick marker indicating the Bohman skirt is truncated, so no curve terminates on the axis frame.
  - Replace the legend with inline labels at the right-hand skirts in panel (b), where the five curves are maximally separated (order from top: Untapered, Multitaper, 25% cosine + parabola, Hann, Bohman). Colour each label to match its series and stagger vertically; add thin same-colour leader lines only where separation is under ~1.5 line heights. Keep only the "Ideal box of width R" key (or label it inline beside one dotted vertical line).
  - If the legend is retained, switch it to row-major fill order and match the caption's sequence, so the two can be read against each other without re-sorting.
  - Append each ν value to its inline label (e.g. "Hann, ν = 8.9") to move the comparison from caption to graphic.
  - Add a faint vertical span or tick pair in panel (b) at fNΔ = ±10 marking the extent of panel (a), making the zoom relationship explicit at a glance.
  - Differentiate the green and olive series more strongly (shift one hue or apply a distinct dash pattern) to protect the crossing region near |fNΔ| ≈ 4 in panel (a).

### efigure2.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The framing is admirably lean (no top/right spines, frameless legend, light horizontal gridlines that sit below the data). The main inefficiency is *redundant data ink*: three separate blue series (L=7 solid, L=6 dashed, L=7 adaptive dotted) plus the grey periodogram produce saturated ink bands in which no individual curve is resolvable. In panel (b) the blue hash between f≈0.25–0.5 is a solid filled block roughly 12 dB tall — that is ~10⁴ plotted vertices conveying one readable fact (envelope height). The same information is carried by far less ink with a light noise band plus a heavy median/envelope line.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — left→right, (a)→(b), shared x-axis and shared color code. One minor backtrack: panel (b) has no labeling of its own, so the reader must return to the legend in (a)'s upper-right corner, and (b) plots only a *subset* of the legend entries (no grey periodogram, no black reference except the zero line), which is not signaled anywhere.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- None identified in the ornamental sense — no 3D, shadows, fills, or borders. This figure is stylistically clean.
- Borderline: the grey periodogram in (a) functions as texture more than as readable data over f>0.2, where it merely establishes "noisy."

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate in (a); Excessive in (b) — the 20–30 dB band of (b) is entirely empty (~⅓ of the panel height) because the data ceiling is ≈18.3 dB. Panel (a) is well filled.

**AXES:** OK, with one note — Neither y-axis starts at zero, which is correct and requires no defense: (a) is a dB (log-power) scale and (b) is a dB *difference*, where zero is already shown and is the meaningful reference (and it is drawn, in black — good). x-axes both start at 0 and stop at the Nyquist 0.5; identical limits and tick sets across panels, which is exactly right for comparison. Recommend trimming (b) to −10…20 with ticks every 5.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate; uncertainty is conveyed by the ν values in the caption and by the visible scatter. No action needed, though ν=2, 17.2, 14, 12, 16.7 are lookup-only in the caption and could be appended to the legend entries.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a) and (b) both upper-left, outside/above the plotting region, same size and weight, same parenthetical style.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — each panel ≈1.4:1 landscape, correct for a frequency-domain trace; the two panels are equal in size, so the reader is not misled into thinking one is more important.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — no callouts, no bracketed labels, no text boxes over data.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — a 7-entry frameless legend in (a) occupying roughly f∈[0.20,0.42], Ŝ∈[33,58] dB. It does **not** overlap data (the AR(4) peaks top out at ≈48 dB near f≈0.11–0.14, well to the left; the periodogram at f≈0.2 reaches only ≈20 dB), so this is not blocking. With seven overlapping, noisy, co-located series, full inline labeling is genuinely impractical here — I would not force it. However, partial direct labeling is feasible and would cut the lookup cost substantially: the green "untapered, then box," orange "cosine taper, then parabola," and black "true PSD" curves all terminate at cleanly separated y-values at f=0.5 in panel (a) (≈−3, ≈−21, ≈−21 dB) and could be labeled at their right endpoints, leaving only the three blue variants and the grey periodogram in a reduced 4-entry key.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The three blue series are distinguished only by dash pattern in the same color. In panel (b), the region the caption's central claim depends on — "L=7 floors 10 dB high, dropping the seventh taper halves that" — is exactly where the blue ink is fully saturated. The L=6 trace is not visually separable from the L=7 trace anywhere in (b), so the figure does not support the claim it is captioned with. This must be resolved before submission.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) plots a subset of the legend entries with no indication of which. A reader looking for the grey periodogram difference (referenced in the caption as "the smoothed periodogram track[s] the truth") will not find it.
- [ADVISORY] Excessive empty vertical space in (b) above 18 dB compresses the informative −5…+18 dB range.
- [ADVISORY] Dense grey periodogram in (a) over f>0.2 acts as texture and partially masks the green and orange estimates in the 0.2–0.35 range.
- [ADVISORY] The ν values that justify the comparison live only in the caption, forcing a caption↔legend shuttle.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Give the three multitaper variants distinct hues within a blue family (e.g., dark navy L=7, mid-blue L=6, cyan adaptive) rather than relying on dash pattern alone; keep line style as a redundant secondary cue.
- In panel (b), replace the raw high-variance traces with a light 10th–90th-percentile band plus a heavy smoothed median line per estimator. This preserves the floor-height comparison, removes ~90% of the ink, and makes the L=7 vs. L=6 halving immediately legible.
- Rescale (b) to −10…20 dB with 5 dB ticks.
- Label the green, orange, and black curves directly at their f=0.5 endpoints in (a), in their own colors; shrink the legend to the grey periodogram and the three multitaper variants.
- Append degrees of freedom to legend text, e.g. "Multitaper, L=7 (ν=14)", so the ν comparison is readable in the graphic.
- Reduce the grey periodogram's alpha (or plot it only in (a) with a lighter weight) so the green and orange smoothed estimates read cleanly through it.
- Add one clause to the (b) caption naming which series are shown, or annotate the three curves inline in (b) at their right edges.

### efigure3.png

---
