**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — mostly lean (two-spine axes, light horizontal gridlines, no fills, no 3D, no shadows), but there is avoidable redundancy: (i) the identical x-axis label "half-power width × NΔ" is printed three times on three panels that share the same variable and (for a/c) the same limits; (ii) roughly the right 35 % of the plotting area in all three panels is empty except for one Welch marker at ~24.5, so a large fraction of the axis ink and tick ink (x = 20, 25) references almost no data; (iii) series are triple-encoded (colour + marker shape + dash pattern) — defensible for greyscale reproduction, but it does mean the legend carries three redundant channels.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic — panel order (a) top-left → (b) top-right → (c) bottom-left is natural, but the 9-entry legend sits in the last-read quadrant (bottom-right). The reader must decode colour/marker identity before interpreting panel (a), forcing a jump to the far corner and back, then again for (b) and (c). The caption even has to tell the reader where the legend is ("the legend is at the lower right") — a sign the placement is not self-evident.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- None serious. No borders, fills, shadows, or ornament.
- Minor: x tick marks/labels at 20 and 25 in panel (c) and (b) serve only one isolated Welch point.
- Minor: the dotted vertical reference line is unlabelled in-figure, so its meaning is only recoverable from the caption — non-self-sufficient data ink.

**WHITE SPACE:** Excessive in places, crowded in others — the right third of all three panels is nearly empty (single Welch point), while the low-width region (x ≈ 2–6) of panels (a) and (c) is congested with 9 nearly coincident curves and overlapping markers.

**AXES:** OK with minor issues — (a) y begins at 0, correct for a count-like quantity (ν). (b) log y is appropriate for power spanning 10⁻² to 10⁻⁹. (c) dB is inherently logarithmic, so a non-zero origin is justified. No inverted axes. x axes do not begin at zero, which is scientifically reasonable (half-power width cannot approach 0 for these kernels) but is nowhere documented; a one-clause note would remove the ambiguity. Panels (a) and (c) appear to share x limits — verify they are pixel-aligned so the dotted 8/(NΔ) line falls at the same x position across panels.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — correctly and explicitly justified in the caption ("exact kernel and degrees-of-freedom computations, with no sampling variability"). Good practice; no action needed.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a), (b), (c) all present, all top-left outside the axes, same size and style. Note they are not bold; if the journal requires bold panel letters, apply uniformly.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Needs adjustment — each panel is roughly 4:3. These are monotone trend curves over a moderate x range; a slightly wider, shorter panel would separate the near-parallel multitaper curves in (a) better. More importantly, the effective aspect is distorted by the unused right third; trimming or breaking the x axis would improve the data density without changing panel size.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — no text boxes, callouts, or brackets. The dotted vertical line is the only annotation and it does not obscure data, but it is unlabelled.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — justified. With 9 series, inline labelling of every curve is impractical and would collide, so a keyed legend is the correct choice here (the <5-series inline rule does not apply). The legend is borderless, occupies an otherwise empty quadrant, and does not overlap any data — good. The residual cost is the lookup distance from panels (a) and (b), and the near-identical olive ("Hann, then box") vs. green ("Untapered, then box") hues, which are hard to separate at print size in panel (c) where those two curves cross.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Legend sits in the last-read quadrant; every panel requires a long-range lookup. Consider moving it to the top-right of panel (a) or spanning it horizontally above the figure so it is read before the data.
- [ADVISORY] Right ~35 % of all panels is empty except a single Welch point at x ≈ 24.5; low data density and wasted real estate.
- [ADVISORY] x-axis label repeated three times for an identical variable; redundant non-data ink.
- [ADVISORY] Dotted reference line unlabelled in-figure; its meaning (8/(NΔ), Table 2) lives only in the caption.
- [ADVISORY] Curves are severely congested for x ≲ 6 in panels (a) and (c); markers overlap and series identity is unrecoverable there.
- [ADVISORY] Olive and green series are close in hue and cross one another in (c); risk of confusion at print size and for colour-deficient readers.
- [ADVISORY] Three blue series (solid/dashed/dotted) share one hue; correct as a family grouping, but verify dash patterns remain distinguishable at final column width.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Move the legend to the empty upper-right region of panel (a) (borderless, two columns) or place it as a single horizontal strip above the top row; delete the caption sentence "and the legend is at the lower right."
- Truncate the x axis at ~17–18 and either annotate the off-scale Welch point inline ("Welch, 24.5 →") or insert a clearly marked axis break; alternatively plot x on a log scale, which would simultaneously decompress the crowded x ≲ 6 region.
- Label the x axis once: if (a) and (c) are vertically aligned, drop the label from (a) and keep it under (c); keep one label under (b).
- Add a small inline tick label on the dotted line (e.g. "8/(NΔ)" set vertically in grey beside it in panel (a) only) so the figure is self-explanatory.
- Recolour the "Hann, then box" series to a clearly distinct hue (or keep colour and rely on distinct markers with a darker/lighter value contrast) to separate it from "Untapered, then box."
- Widen each panel modestly (aspect ≈ 3:2) to spread the near-parallel multitaper curves in (a) and the two steep box-kernel curves in (c).
- Optionally add direct end-of-line labels for the 2–3 curves discussed most in the text (e.g. Welch, Bohman-then-box) in addition to the legend, reducing lookup for the key comparisons.
- Confirm gridline weight is lighter than the thinnest data line at final print width (they appear adequate, but check after downscaling).
- Add one clause to the caption noting that the x axes do not start at zero because the half-power width is bounded below for all kernels considered.

---

### efigure4.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Good — Spines are despined to left/bottom only, no panel bounding boxes, no fills, no shadows, no 3D. The ν annotations are bare text (no boxes), which is exactly right. Two residual items: (1) the light horizontal gridlines appear **only** in row 4 (m–p) and nowhere else, so they are inconsistent non-data ink; (2) axis labels and tick labels are replicated four times per row ("k" ×8, "i" ×4, "fNΔ" ×4, plus 0/128/256 ticks repeated), which is redundant ink given all panels in a row share an identical x-scale.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — Strict left→right, top→bottom grid; column identity is carried by hue (pink / orange / green / blue), which correctly ties rows 2–4 back to the titles in row 1 without forcing a backtrack. Column titles on row 1 only is the right choice.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Horizontal gridlines in m–p only (inconsistent with the other 12 panels; they also sit at the same visual weight as the −40 dB sidelobe structure in (p), where they compete with data).
- Duplicated legends for the same series set: (d) and (e) both legend *i*=1…4; rows 2's shading scheme is identical in f, g, h but unlabeled — one legend for the whole figure, or direct labels, would suffice.
- Dotted vertical reference lines in m–p are unlabeled in-figure (meaning only recoverable from the caption).

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate — Dense but disciplined; inter-panel gutters are sufficient. The empty right-hand thirds of (i) and (l) (7 bars on an axis scaled to 12) are *justified* white space since a common x-range is required for cross-column comparison — do not "fix" this.

**AXES:** Issues —
- dB ordinate in m–p not starting at zero is correct and documented (relative to peak); symmetric fNΔ abscissa is fine. No inverted axes.
- Row 1 mixes normalized and unnormalized ordinates: (c) plots *h*<sub>k−n</sub>/max *h* on 0–1 while (a), (b), (d) plot raw amplitudes. A reader scanning row 1 will misread relative taper amplitudes.
- Row 2 y-limits are not equal across the row: (e) extends to 0.2 (apparently to clear its legend) and (f) appears to top out near 0.1, while (g)/(h) show 0.2. Unequal limits across a row whose entire purpose is comparison of eigen-tapers.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate; all quantities are deterministic (tapers, eigenweights, kernels).

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(p) all present, parenthesized, same weight/size, uniformly at outer top-left of each axes. Good.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — Panels are near-square, suitable for the oscillatory taper shapes and the symmetric kernels; the 4×4 array is close to 1:1 overall, which suits a full-page supplementary figure.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — ν values are unboxed, top-right, consistent across i–l, and clear of the bars. No overlaps. The only replaceable element is the pair of dotted lines in m–p, which could carry a small inline "±R/2" tick/label instead of relying on the caption.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box (frameless) — Legends in (b), (d), (e) are frameless and do not overlap data ((b): curves cap at ≈0.068, legend sits above ≈0.09; (d) and (e): legends occupy the empty 0.14–0.22 band). However, all three are detached lookup keys for 4 series distinguished only by lightness of a single hue — the highest-cost possible encoding for a lookup legend, since the reader must match shades. In (e) the legend is also the reason the y-limit differs from its row-mates.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Gridlines present only in row 4; visually competing with the −35 to −45 dB sidelobe ripple in (p).
- [ADVISORY] Row 1 mixes normalized (c) and unnormalized (a, b, d) ordinates without flagging it in-panel.
- [ADVISORY] Row 2 y-limits unequal across e–h, partly driven by legend clearance in (e); undermines the row's comparison.
- [ADVISORY] Detached shade-based legends in (b), (d), (e); (d) and (e) are redundant duplicates of the same *i*=1…4 key.
- [ADVISORY] Dotted |f| = R/2 lines unlabeled in-figure.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant repetition of axis labels and tick labels across identical shared x-scales within each row.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Either remove the gridlines from m–p or apply the same faint gridlines to all panels; if kept, lighten them below the weight of the thinnest data stroke.
- Normalize row 1 consistently (either all raw or all /max *h*); if (c) must stay normalized, add a small inline note "(normalized)" under the (c) ordinate label.
- Share a single y-limit across e–h (e.g., −0.12 to 0.12) and move the *i*=1…4 key out of (e) so it no longer dictates the limit.
- Replace the three legends with one shared key: place four short colorless "i=1 2 3 4" swatch labels once (e.g., in the figure margin or beneath the column titles), or label directly — put "i=1"…"i=4" in matching shade at each taper's first peak in (h) only, with a 1-pt leader line where curves crowd near x≈128. Likewise in (b), label each curve at its right-hand endpoint (x≈256) where the four traces separate cleanly, instead of using a corner legend.
- Annotate the dotted lines once, e.g., a small "−R/2" / "+R/2" tick label on the (m) abscissa, and drop the caption's parenthetical reliance.
- Keep the x-axis label on the bottom panel of each row-block only, or place a single centered "k", "i", "fNΔ" label per row; likewise keep 0/128/256 tick labels on the leftmost panel of each row if the shared scale is stated.
- Retain as-is: panel lettering, unboxed ν annotations, despined axes, common bar-chart x-range in i–l, and the color-as-column-identity scheme.

### figure1.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Good — Lean throughout. Only horizontal gridlines, drawn light gray and clearly subordinate to the data; no borders, fills, 3D, or shadows; legend in (a) has no bounding box. Two minor redundancies: the pink inline label "Maximum |difference|" in (c) restates the y-axis label ("max. |difference| (dB)") for what is the panel's only data series, and the open circle markers in (c) are duplicated by the connecting line's vertices.

READING FLOW:     Natural — Full-width (a) on top, then (b) left → (c) right. Legend in (a) sits in the empty upper-right quadrant where the eye arrives after scanning the curve, so no backtracking. Inline labels in (b) and (c) are read in place.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Redundant pink "Maximum |difference|" annotation in (c), duplicating the y-axis label.
  - Marker symbols in (c) add little beyond the five line vertices (defensible if the sampled $N_s$ values must be emphasized — borderline, not a true violation).
  - Duplicate "Frequency (Hz)" x-labels in (a) and (b): acceptable here because (b) is not width-aligned under (a), but see fixes.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate — Upper-right void in (a) is productively occupied by the legend. The large empty band between the two traces in (b) is *itself the result* (twelve decades of separation), so it is data-bearing white space, not waste. Slight crowding only in the lower-left corner of (c), where the two-line pink annotation abuts the $10^{-1}$ tick label.

AXES:             OK — (a) y-axis in dB is a logarithmic ratio scale, so a non-zero origin is conventional and needs no defense; x begins at 0. (b) and (c) are log-scaled by necessity. (c) x-axis tick set (1, 2, 4, 8, 16) matches the sampled design points; no inverted axes.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — Defensible: the comparison is numerical/deterministic, not statistical. However, the caption cites $\nu=17$ equivalent degrees of freedom, which implies a quantifiable sampling uncertainty for (a) that is nowhere shown.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a), (b), (c) all present, all upper-left, same parenthetical lowercase style and size. (a) sits slightly farther from its axes frame than (b)/(c) do from theirs; align to the same offset for tidiness.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — (a) is wide, as a 0–40 Hz spectrum should be; (c) is near-square for a five-point scaling relation. (b) is the weakest: a 15-decade y-range compressed into a half-width panel renders the internal structure of each trace as a featureless band.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — No boxed callouts, borders, fills, or shadows anywhere. The "$1/N_s$" guide-line annotation in (c) is correctly placed in open space beside the dotted line rather than boxed. Only the (c) pink annotation qualifies as *replaceable* (by deletion, since the axis label already says it).

LEGEND STYLE:     Box (borderless, in-axes) — Justified exception. In (a) the three series coincide to within $10^{-13}$ dB, so inline endpoint labeling is impossible: there is only one visible curve. A keyed legend distinguishing linewidth/dash pattern is the correct choice, and it is placed in empty space with no data overlap. Panels (b) and (c) already use direct inline labeling in matching series colors, with no label–curve overlap detected ("Sliding sinc − multitaper" sits ~3 decades above the pink trace; "Box − multitaper" ~2 decades above the green).

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c): the pink two-line label "Maximum |difference|" is redundant with the y-axis label and crowds the $10^{-1}$ y-tick label in the lower-left corner.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b): 15 decades of y-range flatten both traces into featureless bands; the reader cannot judge the frequency dependence of either residual, only its order of magnitude.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a): the deliberate overplot (thick solid / dashed / dotted) carries the paper's central claim of exact agreement, but at final publication width the 3-curve stacking may read as a single messy line. The visual evidence for agreement rests entirely on (b).
  - [ADVISORY] The caption reports $\nu=17$ degrees of freedom yet no confidence band appears in (a); a reader may wonder whether the agreement in (b) is small relative to the estimator's own sampling error (it is, by ~12 orders of magnitude, but the figure does not show this).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) is not width-aligned with panel (a) despite sharing an identical 0–40 Hz x-axis, forcing a duplicated axis label and a small horizontal eye-jump when comparing spectral features to residual features.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the "Maximum |difference|" annotation in (c); the y-axis label already names the quantity, and with a single series plus a labeled guide line there is nothing to disambiguate.
  - Consider making (b) full-width and vertically aligned with (a), sharing a single "Frequency (Hz)" label beneath it, and moving (c) to a third row or an inset. This removes the duplicated axis label and lets the reader drop a vertical sightline from any spectral peak in (a) to the residual at that frequency in (b).
  - If (b) must stay half-width, either (i) split it into two stacked sub-panels with local y-ranges (~$10^{-14}$–$10^{-12}$ and ~$10^{-3}$–$10^{0}$) so each residual's structure is visible, or (ii) keep the single wide range but state explicitly in the caption that the panel is intended to show *separation of magnitude*, not spectral shape.
  - Add a faint $\nu=17$ confidence half-width as a short vertical reference bar in (a) (labeled inline, e.g. "95% CI"), so the ~0.06 dB and $3\times10^{-13}$ dB residuals in (b) can be judged against the estimator's intrinsic uncertainty.
  - Nudge the (a) panel label to the same left/top offset used by (b) and (c).
  - Retain the borderless legend in (a) and state in the caption *why* a legend rather than inline labels is used ("the three curves are visually indistinguishable"), pre-empting the reviewer question.
  - Keep gridlines at their current weight; if anything, restrict (a) to the three labeled decibel levels already shown rather than adding more.

### figure2.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — The core plots are admirably lean (L-shaped spines, hairline horizontal gridlines clearly subordinate to the data, no fills, no 3D, no shadows). The waste is in repetition: the y-tick label set is printed four times per row (60…−40 in a–d; 0…−150 in e–h) although all four panels in a row share an identical scale, and the x-tick label set (0…0.5) is printed eight times although each column is vertically aligned on a common x-axis. Each inset also repeats its own 0/−25/−50 ladder four times. Removing the interior duplicates would cut roughly a third of the non-data ink and let the panels sit closer together (or grow).

READING FLOW:     Natural — Left→right across taper/smoothing conditions, top (estimates) over bottom (kernels), with the column correspondence doing real explanatory work. One minor backtrack: the labels "then parabola" in (c) and (d) require the reader to jump back to (b) to learn what "then" refers to; spell out "25% taper + parabola" so each panel is self-contained.

CHARTJUNK:
  - None of consequence. The inset frames and their two leader lines to the zoom rectangle are non-data ink but are functional (they define the magnified region) and are drawn thin and gray — acceptable.
  - The repeated interior tick-label ladders (see above) are the closest thing to junk here.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate — slightly tight between columns given the repeated y labels; the large empty lower-left region of (e)–(h) and the unused −20 to −40 dB band in (c)/(d) are consequences of shared scales and are justified.

AXES:             OK — All quantities are in dB (log-ratio), so a non-zero origin is legitimate; the x-axes begin at 0 and run to Nyquist. Nothing inverted. Two small items: the top row has x-tick numerals but no axis title (fΔ appears only on the bottom row) — either label both rows or drop the top row's numerals entirely; and the x-range 0.2–0.5 is featureless decay in (a)–(d) while all the structure (two peaks at 0.11 and 0.14) is squeezed into the leftmost fifth.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — Equivalent degrees of freedom ν are quoted instead, which is the right statistic, but the reader cannot see the implied uncertainty. Since ν is already on each panel, consider adding a single small chi-square-based confidence-interval bar (e.g. 95% for ν = 2, 16.7, 98) in a quiet corner of each top-row panel, and define it explicitly in the caption.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(h) all present, all in parentheses, same size, same position at the upper-left outside the axes. Good.

ASPECT RATIO:     Needs adjustment — Panels are near-square, but these are spectra: the diagnostic feature is peak separation on the frequency axis. Widening each panel (or cropping the x-range, or splitting into a broken/zoomed frequency axis) would make the merge of the two peaks in (d) far more legible.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — The condition/ν annotations are borderless, unfilled text in the upper-right dead space of each panel and overlap no data. The inset frames are the only bordered elements; they are thin and gray. One caution: in (h) the inset's lower-right corner sits very close to the flat top of the main lobe near fΔ = 0.4, and in (e) the leader lines pass near the peak — at final column width these clearances may close up.

LEGEND STYLE:     Inline — overlapping (color mismatch) — The key is direct text ("True PSD" in black, "Estimate" in gray) inside panel (a), which correctly eliminates a legend box. But the estimate is drawn in gray only in (a); in (b), (c) and (d) it is orange. A color-coded inline label that does not match the series in three of four panels is a decoding trap: the reader may infer that gray and orange denote different quantities. The gray "Estimate" text is also very low contrast on white and will be hard to read at print size.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Legend/series color mismatch: the inline label "Estimate" is gray, matching (a) only, while the estimate curve is orange in (b)–(d). Either recolor the (a) estimate to the same orange used elsewhere, or label the estimate inline in each panel in that panel's own color.
  - [ADVISORY] "Estimate" text is rendered in a light gray with insufficient contrast for print; darken it or use the series color at full saturation.
  - [ADVISORY] ν in panel (d) reads 98 but the caption states 98.4. Make figure and caption numerically identical.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant tick-label ladders: y labels repeated on all four panels in each row; x labels repeated on both rows of each column; inset y ladders repeated four times.
  - [ADVISORY] Top row carries x-tick numerals but no axis label, while the bottom row is labeled fΔ — inconsistent axis annotation.
  - [ADVISORY] "then parabola" in (c) and (d) depends on (b) for meaning; each panel should be readable alone.
  - [ADVISORY] Near-square panels compress the frequency axis where all the structure lies; the two peaks and their merge in (d) are hard to resolve.
  - [ADVISORY] Error/uncertainty is quoted only as ν; no visual confidence interval is shown despite three very different ν values being the figure's main point.
  - [ADVISORY] Inset frame in (h) crowds the main lobe near fΔ = 0.4; clearance may vanish at publication width.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Unify the estimate color across (a)–(d) (orange everywhere) and place the two inline labels "True PSD" (black) and "Estimate" (orange) once in panel (a) only, in the matching colors.
  - Strip interior tick labels: keep y-tick numerals only on (a) and (e), keep x-tick numerals and the fΔ title only on (e)–(h); keep tick marks themselves so the grid is still readable. Retain inset y labels only in (e).
  - Reclaim the freed margin space by widening each panel (or reduce the x-range to 0–0.3 with a note, or use a broken axis) so the 0.11/0.14 peak pair and its merger in (d) read clearly.
  - Rewrite the in-panel annotations to be self-contained: "no taper", "25% cosine taper", "25% taper + parabola, R = 8/(NΔ)", "25% taper + parabola, R = 48/(NΔ)", each with its ν beneath.
  - Add one 95% confidence-interval bar per top-row panel, sized from the stated ν, placed in the empty region below −20 dB at high frequency, and define it in the caption ("vertical bar: 95% chi-square interval for ν degrees of freedom").
  - Nudge the (h) inset up/left by a few points so its frame clears the main-lobe plateau, and check all four insets for leader-line/curve clearance at final column width.
  - Reconcile ν = 98 vs 98.4 between panel (d) and the caption.
---

### figure3.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Good — Spines are minimal (no bounding boxes), no fills, no 3D, no shadows. Gridlines in (b) are light gray and clearly subordinate to the data. Two minor redundancies: (i) the dashed taper in (a) is drawn as a mirrored ±envelope, so the lower half is redundant data ink that duplicates the upper half; (ii) in (b) horizontal gridlines plus y-tick marks plus tick labels triple-encode the same reference levels — one of the tick marks or gridlines could go.

READING FLOW:     Natural — (a) above (b), both read left→right with x increasing; the (a)→(b) progression (record → error-vs-resolution) matches the caption's narrative. The three top-of-panel labels in (a) read left→right in the same order the elements appear. One small backtrack: in (b) the "Total, peak region" label sits above and to the *left* of the point where its curve terminates, while "Total, whole band" sits to the right of its endpoint; the eye has to move in two different directions to resolve the two most important series.

CHARTJUNK:
  - None substantive. The mirrored lower taper envelope in (a) is borderline decorative rather than informative.
  - Circled annotations in (b) are functional (they key to Fig. motif panels), not ornament, but see ANNOTATION BOXES for crowding.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate overall, with two local problems — the upper-left quadrant of (b) (roughly RNΔ 15–45, 6.5–9 dB) is empty, while the region around the broad minimum (RNΔ ≈ 7–12, 1.5–2.8 dB) is heavily congested: two curves, two marker sets, a dotted reference line, a circled point, and a leader line all converge there.

AXES:             OK — Both panels begin at zero on both axes; no inversions. Axis ranges are honest. Two notes: (b)'s y-axis extends to ~9 dB although the maximum plotted value is ~6.1 dB, and that headroom is used only by the "peak width"/"peak separation" labels — the axis could be capped near 7 dB with the reference-line labels moved inside. In (a) the tick values 0/255/511/767/1023 are sample indices and are justified (powers-of-two record), but consider labeling 0 and 1023 only, or adding "samples" to the axis title, since 255/511/767 are unusual gridpoints for a general reader.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — and explicitly disclaimed in the caption ("the curves are ensemble averages and carry no error bars"). This satisfies the journal requirement; no blocking issue. Consider stating the number of realizations (300, already given) adjacent to the disclaimer as you do — good practice.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a) and (b) both present, both outside the axes at upper left, same size, same parenthetical style, aligned on a common left margin.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — (a) is wide and short, correct for a 1024-sample time series; (b) is close to square, correct for a smooth bivariate curve family. The stacked 990×1252 composite is reasonable for a single-column figure, though (b) occupies slightly more vertical space than its information content requires (see WHITE SPACE).

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean (borderless) but locally overlapping — All annotations are borderless inline text with thin gray leader lines, which is the correct Tufte solution; no boxes, fills, or shadows. Problems are positional:
  - The "just right, ν = 17" leader line runs down-right from the text and appears to cross the rising dashed "Total, peak region" curve between RNΔ ≈ 10–14 before reaching the circled point at RNΔ ≈ 8. A leader line crossing a *different* data series is ambiguous.
  - The circled marker at RNΔ ≈ 8 sits on top of the near-coincident open-square and filled-circle markers and is bisected by the "peak width" dotted line, producing a four-way visual collision at the single most important feature of the plot (the minimum).
  - "Bias" sits very close under the gray bias curve near RNΔ ≈ 17; at column width this will nearly touch the stroke.

LEGEND STYLE:     Inline — clean (with crowding at right) — No detached legend boxes anywhere; all series are labeled directly, which is exactly right. Residual issues: "Total, peak region" and "Total, whole band" are both anchored near the right edge within ~0.5 dB of the region where the dashed curve plateaus (~5.5 dB) and the solid curve terminates (~5.0 dB), so the two labels and two curve ends crowd the same corner; "Total, whole band" is also forced onto two lines against the axis boundary and risks clipping at final width. Labels appear black for both black curves, so color cannot disambiguate them — position alone must do the work.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] "just right, ν = 17" leader line crosses the dashed "Total, peak region" curve, implying a false association.
  - [ADVISORY] Four-element collision at the minimum (RNΔ ≈ 8): circle annotation, open square, filled circle, and "peak width" dotted line.
  - [ADVISORY] Right-edge crowding of "Total, peak region" and "Total, whole band"; the latter is a two-line label pressed against the axis limit and may clip when scaled to one column.
  - [ADVISORY] "Bias" label offset from the gray bias curve is too small; will nearly touch the stroke at publication size.
  - [ADVISORY] ~2.5 dB of unused vertical headroom in (b) used only to park the "peak width"/"peak separation" labels.
  - [ADVISORY] Mirrored lower taper envelope in (a) is redundant data ink.
  - [ADVISORY] Gridlines, tick marks, and tick labels triple-encode y-reference levels in (b).
  - [ADVISORY] (a)'s x tick values 255/511/767 are unusual reading points; axis title "k" gives no unit context.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Move "just right, ν = 17" to the empty area *below* the minimum (e.g., near RNΔ ≈ 14, y ≈ 1.0) and draw a short leader up-left to the circled point, so the leader never crosses the dashed curve.
  - Declutter the minimum: nudge the "peak width" dotted line label and, if the line's exact position permits, draw the circle slightly larger and thinner (hairline) so the underlying markers remain legible; or drop the marker symbols on the solid curve between RNΔ = 6 and 12 where they are densest and the curve is flat.
  - Stagger the two "Total" labels: place "Total, peak region" just above the dashed plateau at RNΔ ≈ 45 on one line, and move "Total, whole band" to sit just *below* the solid curve around RNΔ ≈ 55 on one line, inside the axes — this frees the right margin and removes the two-line wrap and clipping risk.
  - Increase the "Bias" and "Noise" label offsets to at least one text-height from their curves, and set both labels in the same gray as the curves they name so color reinforces the pairing.
  - Cap (b)'s y-axis near 7 dB and set the "peak width"/"peak separation" labels as rotated text alongside their dotted lines (or at the top just inside the axes), reclaiming the empty upper band and enlarging the data region.
  - Draw only the upper half of the taper in (a) (or draw the lower half as a very light gray) to halve the redundant envelope ink; keep the dashed style and the existing "Taper (dashed)" label.
  - In (b), delete the y tick marks and keep the light gridlines (or vice versa) rather than both.
  - In (a), reduce x ticks to 0, 511, 1023 (or 0 and 1023 with a single interior tick) and retitle the axis "sample index k" for immediate legibility.

### figure4.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — four closed bounding boxes (all four spines) per panel; only left and bottom carry information. Horizontal gridlines are light and subordinate to the data (good), but in (b) and (c) they add little given the sparse curve structure. Tick marks appear on all four sides in some panels (redundant). Large fractions of each panel are empty axis range rather than data (see AXES). No 3D, shadows, or fills — good.

READING FLOW:     Problematic — the layout is a clean 2×2 read (a→b→c→d), but the substantive comparison is between (b) and (c), which share an identical x-axis (`fNΔ`, −8→8) and are placed diagonally opposite. The reader must jump top-right → bottom-left and re-read the axis. Placing (b) directly above (c) in a shared-x column would let one x-axis serve both and make the "filling the gaps → sum becomes a box" argument read vertically.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Full rectangular frames around each panel (non-data ink).
  - Detached legend boxes in (a), (b), and (d) where 2–3 series could be labeled directly.
  - Redundant repetition of the `fNΔ` axis label in (b) and (c) (would vanish under a shared-x column layout).
  - Marker outlines in (d): open circles with both a black ring and a visible connecting line through them slightly double-encode the same points; either markers or line suffices at this density.

WHITE SPACE:      Excessive (within panels) — (a) uses y-limits ±0.20 for data spanning ±0.125 (~40% of the panel is empty); (c) uses 0–0.20 for data reaching 0.125; (d) extends to 10⁻¹⁶ while the smallest datum is ~2×10⁻¹⁰ (six decades of blank axis). (b) devotes roughly half its width to identically-zero regions |fNΔ|>4. Inter-panel spacing itself is adequate.

AXES:             Issues —
  - (a) y-range ±0.20 far exceeds the data; tighten to ~±0.13 to recover amplitude resolution.
  - (b) x-range −8→8 is justified only if it must match (c); otherwise crop to ±5 where the kernels live.
  - (c) y-limit 0.20 vs. data max 0.125 flattens the ripple that the caption asks the reader to see. Tighten to ~0.14 so the "rounded shoulders and small ripple" for L=7 are legible.
  - (d) log y is scientifically justified (concentration spans ~10 decades) — document in caption/axis; currently fine. Bottom limit 10⁻¹⁶ should be raised to ~10⁻¹¹.
  - (d) rightmost markers (i≈17) sit on or touch the right spine — data collides with the frame; extend xlim to ~17.5.
  - (d) x ticks at even integers only, but the index i is integer-valued starting at 1; minor ticks at every integer (or ticks at 1,3,5…) would match the data.
  - No inverted axes; zero is included where meaningful.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate for deterministic kernels/eigenvalues.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(d) present on all four panels, top-left, same parenthesized style and size. Consider bolding for faster scanning, but this is optional.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — overall ~1.6:1 and each panel modestly wider than tall, which suits the broad frequency-domain profiles. (d), a monotone ladder in an index, would also read well slightly narrower.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — no callouts or text boxes overlap data. The ±R/2 boundaries in (b) and the L=2α−1 / 2α marks in (d) are drawn as reference lines rather than boxed labels, which is the correct Tufte choice. The two reference lines in (d) could be annotated with small inline text at the top of each line instead of via the legend.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box (all four panels) —
  - (a) three-entry box in the lower-left void. The three tapers are cleanly separable near k≈20–60; label `h_k^(1)`, `h_k^(2)`, `h_k^(7)` inline in matching colors and delete the box.
  - (b) three-entry box in the upper-right. Curves are well separated at their peaks; place `i=1` above the central black peak, `i=2` beside the blue peak near fNΔ≈+1.2, `i=3` beside the green peak near fNΔ≈+1.9, staggered vertically so the three labels do not crowd. No overlap currently, so ADVISORY only.
  - (c) six entries — a box is defensible here, but the two entries that carry the argument (`L=1` and `L=N`, plus `Box`) could be labeled inline at f=0 where the curves are vertically well separated, shrinking the legend to the sequential ramp only.
  - (d) two-entry box in the lower-right. Replace with short rotated inline labels at the top of each vertical line (grey dotted → `L=2α−1`, orange dashed → `2α`), in matching colors. Legend text is also inconsistent: one entry reads `L=2α−1` and the other bare `2α`; make both forms parallel.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (b) and (c) share an identical x-axis but are diagonally opposed, forcing a backtracking read of the figure's central argument.
  - [ADVISORY] Over-extended y-limits in (a), (c), and (d) waste 40–60% of the plotting area and visually suppress the ripple structure the caption relies on.
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend boxes on four line charts with 2–6 series; at least (a), (b), and (d) can be fully inline-labeled.
  - [ADVISORY] In (d), the final data markers touch the right frame; points should not collide with the axis boundary.
  - [ADVISORY] Closed four-sided frames and four-sided tick marks are non-data ink.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend label inconsistency in (d): `L=2α−1` vs. `2α`.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption states the kernels in (b) are `|H^(i)(f)|²/Δ`, but the axis reads `|H^(i)(f)|²/NΔ²`; reconcile so caption and axis agree (also check the (c) normalisation `…/2αΔ` against the axis `K(f)/NΔ`).

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Reorder to a shared-x column: put the kernel panel (current b) directly above the running-sum panel (current c) with one `fNΔ` axis beneath both; move (a) and (d) to the other column. Relabel a–d to preserve left→right, top→bottom reading.
  - Tighten limits: (a) y ∈ [−0.13, 0.13]; (c) y ∈ [0, 0.14]; (d) y ∈ [10⁻¹¹, 1], x ∈ [0.5, 17.5].
  - Delete all four legend boxes where possible: inline colored labels in (a) and (b); inline rotated labels at the top of the two reference lines in (d); reduce (c)'s legend to the L-ramp with `Box` and `L=N` labeled inline.
  - Drop top and right spines and restrict ticks to the left and bottom axes in every panel.
  - In (d), keep either the open markers or the connecting line, not both at full weight; thin the line to ~0.6 pt so the markers carry the data.
  - Add minor ticks at every integer i in (d), and state in the caption that the y-axis is logarithmic because $1-\lambda_i$ spans ten decades.
  - Fix the normalisation mismatch between caption and the (b)/(c) y-axis labels.

### figure5.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The plot frames are already reduced to left/bottom spines, there are no gridlines, no fills, no 3D effects, and no shadows: the core of the figure is commendably lean. Three residual sources of non-data ink:
1. The y-axis tick labels (0.00, 0.05, 0.10, 0.15, 0.20) are repeated identically on all four bottom panels. Only (e) needs them; (f)–(h) share the identical scale and can drop tick labels (keep the ticks, or drop both and state the shared scale).
2. The bottom-row x-axis is labelled only at 5 and 10 — no tick at 1 or at the origin, so the reader cannot anchor where the first bar sits. This is under-labelled rather than over-labelled, but it is inconsistent with the fully anchored 0/128/256 ticks above.
3. Two legend boxes (b, d) reproduce information that could be carried by the curves themselves.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — Excellent column logic: each construction route in the top row sits directly above its eigen-weight ladder in the bottom row, and the four routes read left→right in increasing order of ν-flatness narrative. No backtracking is forced. The one flow snag is that panel (c)'s four curves are unlabelled, so the reader must jump to the caption to learn what the four shades mean, while (b) and (d) answer the same question in-panel.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- None of the classic varieties. No decorative elements, no redundant frames, no gradients.
- Borderline: the two legend boxes are the only ornamental-in-function elements.

**WHITE SPACE:** Excessive in the bottom row — Panels (e) and (h) each carry 7 bars in an x-range that runs past 12, leaving roughly 40% of the panel width empty. The shared x-range is scientifically defensible (it makes the ladder lengths directly comparable across routes, which is the whole point of the row), so this is not a fault so much as a cost; but combined with the tall aspect it produces large dead regions in the upper-right of (e) and (h). The top row's white space is appropriate.

**AXES:** OK — Bottom-row bars all begin at zero, correctly. Top-row y-axes do not begin at zero in (b) and (d), but both carry genuinely negative taper values, which is a documented and sufficient reason. Panel (c) is normalized to max h and (d) is not, so the top row is not on a common scale; the caption states this, which is adequate. No inverted axes. Two smaller points: the bottom-row x-axis lacks an anchor tick at i=1, and the y-axis label c_i/∑c_i appears only on (e) — correct practice, but it means (f)–(h) currently show numbers with no named quantity, which is why removing their duplicate tick labels is the cleaner resolution.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — Appropriately so; these are deterministic windows and eigen-weights, not sampled estimates. No action needed, and the caption should not be pressed to define error bars that do not exist.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(h) are present on all eight panels, all in the same parenthesized lowercase style, same weight and size, all positioned outside the axes at upper-left and vertically aligned with the panel top. This is the correct and least intrusive placement. No action.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Needs adjustment — The top row is near-square, which suits these oscillatory window shapes well. The bottom row is noticeably taller than wide for what are 7–12 category bar charts; bar charts of this length read better wide, and the current proportion stretches short bars (the tails in f and g) into thin slivers while inflating empty headroom. Reducing the bottom row height by ~25–30% relative to the top row would both fix the proportion and absorb the dead space in (e)/(h).

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — The ν = 13.4 / 16.7 / 17.2 / 14.0 annotations are exemplary: unboxed, unfilled, no leader lines, placed in genuinely empty upper-right space, and they never touch a bar. This is exactly the Tufte-preferred form of annotation, and it should be left alone. Note that these four numbers are also enumerated in the caption; that duplication is acceptable (in-panel for reading, caption for citation) and I would not strip either.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — Two detached legend boxes, in (b) (four entries, f′NΔ = 0…3, two columns) and (d) (four entries, i = 1…4, two columns). Neither appears to overlap plotted data: in (b) the curves top out near 0.07 while the legend occupies roughly the 0.08–0.125 band, and in (d) the curves peak near 0.12 with the legend sitting above in the 0.14–0.20 band. So this is advisory, not blocking. However, both are lookup tables the reader must consult, and both sit in space that exists only because the y-limits were extended to accommodate them. In (d) the four Slepian tapers separate cleanly enough near their peaks that inline colour-matched labels (i = 1 at the central peak near k = 128, i = 2…4 at their respective left-shifted peaks) would eliminate the legend entirely and let the y-limit shrink to about 0.15. In (b) inline labelling is harder — the four frequency-shifted cosines interleave repeatedly — so a legend is a reasonable concession there; if kept, it should be borderless and frameless. Panel (c) has four series with no legend and no inline labels at all, which is the real inconsistency: either label its four sinc positions inline (they are well separated at their main lobes, near k = 0, 85, 128 and 256) or accept that the shades are ordinal and say so in-panel.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Detached legend boxes on line panels (b) and (d); recommend inline colour-matched labels, definitely for (d), optionally for (b).
- [ADVISORY] Panel (c) has four distinguishable series with neither legend nor inline labels, inconsistent with (b) and (d).
- [ADVISORY] Y-axis tick labels duplicated identically across (e)–(h) on a shared scale.
- [ADVISORY] Bottom-row x-axis has no anchor tick at i = 1, so the first bar's index is not readable from the axis.
- [ADVISORY] Bottom-row panels are taller than wide for 7–12 category bar charts; (e) and (h) additionally carry ~40% empty width.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Replace (d)'s legend with four inline labels placed at each taper's main peak, coloured to match, then lower the y-limit to ~0.15 to recover the headroom the legend was occupying.
- Label (c)'s four sinc positions inline at their main lobes, or add a one-line in-panel note that lighter shades are later window positions, so all three multi-series panels answer the reader in the same way.
- If (b)'s legend is retained, strip its frame and any fill so it reads as floating text rather than a box.
- Delete the y-tick labels on (f), (g) and (h), keeping the tick marks and the single c_i/∑c_i label on (e).
- Add an x-tick at i = 1 across (e)–(h) so the ladder's first rung is anchored.
- Reduce the bottom-row panel height by roughly 25–30% relative to the top row; this corrects the bar-chart proportion and absorbs the dead area in (e) and (h) without breaking the shared x-range that makes the row comparable.
- Leave the ν annotations and the (a)–(h) panel labels exactly as they are; both are already at the standard this figure should be judged against.

### figure6.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The core design is already quite lean (L-shaped spines, shared y-axis labels on leftmost panels only, no 3D, no shadows, no fills), which is commendable. Remaining non-data ink: (1) the four inset axes are drawn as full four-sided rectangles, inconsistent with the open L-spines of the parent panels; (2) the inset tick labels (`0 / −25 / −50` and `0.39 / 0.4 / 0.41`) are repeated verbatim four times — three of the four sets carry no new information; (3) faint horizontal gridlines in all eight panels are largely redundant with the tick labels for curves of this shape; (4) the estimate curve is drawn in a different hue in each of (a)–(d) although the colour encodes nothing — this is redundant data ink (colour doing the work the panel label already does).

**READING FLOW:** Natural — left→right across methods, top→bottom from estimate to kernel; the column-wise pairing (a/e, b/f, c/g, d/h) is immediately legible. One backtrack: the "True PSD / Estimate" key sits in the *lower-left* of panel (a), while both curves it names live in the upper-right half of that panel, so the eye must jump diagonally across empty space to resolve it.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Four-sided frames around the inset axes (parent panels use open spines).
- Redundant inset tick labels repeated in all four bottom panels.
- Gridlines that serve no reading task the ticks don't already serve.
- Gratuitous colour variation of the "Estimate" curve across panels (a)–(d).

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate, with pockets of waste — The top row reserves −20 to −40 dB although no curve goes below about −22 dB; the bottom row reserves −100 to −150 dB although (e) and (f) never fall below about −60 dB. Roughly the lower quarter of the top row and the lower third of (e)/(f) are empty. This is a defensible price for a common scale, but the top row could be truncated at about −30 dB with no loss of comparability.

**AXES:** OK — Both ordinates are in decibels (ratio/log data), so a zero origin is neither expected nor meaningful; the abscissa correctly starts at 0 and spans the full Nyquist band. No inverted axes. Scales are shared within each row, which is exactly right for cross-method comparison. Minor: x tick labels are duplicated on both rows of every column while the axis title *f*Δ appears only on the bottom row — either label the top row's abscissa or drop its tick labels.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — Sampling variability is conveyed instead by the equivalent degrees of freedom ν annotated in each top panel, but ν is never defined either in the figure or in the caption. A reader cannot convert ν into an uncertainty statement without going to the text.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(h) present on all eight panels, uniformly at the top-left outside the plotting area, same size and weight, parenthesised throughout.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — Each panel is close to square, which suits spectra whose interesting structure is both narrow in frequency (the twin peaks) and wide in dynamic range. The 4×2 grid at 2077×1097 px gives sensible per-panel proportions.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Mostly clean, one risk — The in-panel titles ("No taper, then box", "Multitaper, L=7", etc.) are borderless, unfilled, right-aligned text in empty space: exemplary. The zoom rectangles and their two leader lines are thin and grey, appropriately subordinate. However, in (f) — and to a lesser degree (e) — the lower leader line running from the inset's bottom-right corner down-left toward the zoom rectangle passes very close to, and appears to graze, the rising kernel curve near *f*Δ ≈ 0.30–0.35. At final column width this will read as an intersection.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Inline — clean but distant — No detached legend box anywhere; the only key is the colour-matched inline pair "True PSD" (black) / "Estimate" (teal) in panel (a), which is the Tufte-correct choice. The weakness is placement, not method: the labels are ~0.2 in *f*Δ and ~30 dB away from the curves they name, and the teal swatch only matches panel (a) — in (b)/(c)/(d) the estimate is blue/orange/pink with no key at all, so the reader must infer that "the coloured one is the estimate."

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] ν is used as a quantitative annotation in all four top panels (and in the caption) but is nowhere defined. Add "ν = equivalent degrees of freedom" to the caption, with the associated confidence level or CI width if that is what the reader is meant to take away.
- [ADVISORY] In (f), the inset's lower leader line appears to touch/cross the kernel curve near *f*Δ ≈ 0.3; same geometry is marginal in (e). Annotation lines crossing data is the failure mode to avoid.
- [ADVISORY] Inline key in (a) is placed far from both curves it labels, and no key appears in (b)–(d) where the estimate colour changes.
- [ADVISORY] Estimate colour varies across (a)–(d) without encoding anything; this invites the reader to hunt for a meaning that does not exist.
- [ADVISORY] Inset tick labels repeated identically four times; inset frames are closed rectangles inconsistent with the open parent spines.
- [ADVISORY] Unused ordinate range: top row below ≈ −25 dB, and (e)/(f) below ≈ −65 dB.
- [ADVISORY] Top-row x tick labels are present without an axis title, duplicating the bottom row's labels.
- [ADVISORY] Gridlines add ink without adding a reading task.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Define ν in the caption at first use, e.g. "ν is the equivalent number of degrees of freedom of the estimate (Eq. X), so that ν·Ŝ/S is approximately χ²_ν"; optionally add a single short vertical bar in panel (a) showing the corresponding 95 % interval width in dB, labelled inline — this converts an abstract number into a directly readable uncertainty.
- Move the "True PSD" / "Estimate" labels in (a) to sit immediately beside the two curves where they are maximally separated (around *f*Δ ≈ 0.35, one just above the teal trace, one just below the black), keeping each label in its series' colour. Repeat only the word "Estimate" in (b)–(d) in that panel's colour, or better, adopt one colour for all four estimates and keep the single key in (a).
- Redraw the (e)/(f) leader lines so they leave the inset from a corner that clears the kernel curve — or shift those insets slightly upward/rightward — and confirm at final print width that no leader line touches a curve.
- Keep tick labels on the leftmost inset only; delete the numerals from the other three insets (their axis ranges are identical and stated once). Replace the closed inset frames with the same open L-spines used in the parent panels.
- Delete the horizontal gridlines, or reduce them to the single reference line that matters (e.g. 0 dB in the bottom row, where "power outside the band" is judged).
- Truncate the top-row ordinate at about −30 dB; if (e)/(f) must share −150 with (g)/(h) for comparability, state that explicitly in the caption so the empty lower thirds read as information ("these kernels never get that low") rather than as wasted space.
- Drop the top-row x tick labels (the shared abscissa is established by the bottom row), or add a compact *f*Δ title to the top row so the labels are self-sufficient.

### figure7.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The curves themselves are efficient (thin strokes, no markers, no fills), and the horizontal gridlines are appropriately lighter than the data. Three items cost ink without adding information: (1) the apparent full bounding frame / ticks on all four sides of each panel — top and right spines encode nothing and can be erased; (2) the zero reference line in (b) is drawn heavier and darker (solid black) than any of the data it references, which inverts the intended hierarchy; (3) two legend boxes carry series names that could be attached directly to the data or are already fully specified in the caption.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — Left→right in frequency, top (estimates) → bottom (differences), which matches the caption's narrative order. Shared x-axis with labels only on the lower panel is correct and avoids redundancy. One minor backtrack: the reader must jump from the (a) legend up-right, back down-left into (b), then right again to the (b) legend to re-confirm that orange = recipe (b′) and pink = Welch.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Legend box in (a) sitting inside the data region in an area where the highest peaks come close (see LEGEND STYLE).
- Second legend in (b) largely restating the color code already established in (a).
- Solid black zero line heavier than the plotted differences.
- Possible top/right axis spines and their tick marks.

**WHITE SPACE:** Excessive in panel (b) — the y-range spans −15 to +15 dB while the data occupy roughly −5.5 to +12.5 dB. Roughly a third of panel (b) is empty, and the empty lower third exists mainly to host the legend. Panel (a) is adequately filled.

**AXES:** OK with one note — Non-zero origin in (a) is justified (decibel, i.e., logarithmic, scale; documented in the caption's units). Panel (b) is a difference and correctly brackets zero symmetrically. Ticks and labels are clean and non-redundant; 5-Hz major spacing is appropriate. Recommend tightening (b) to about −8 to +13 dB and, if the symmetric range is retained for visual honesty, stating that choice in the caption.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — The caption quotes the estimators' own noise (4.3√(2/ν) ≈ 1.5–1.6 dB), which is the natural uncertainty scale for panel (b), but this quantity appears nowhere in the graphic. This is not a blocking omission (no undefined error bars are drawn), but the figure's central claim — "agree to within their own noise" — is currently only assertable from the caption, not readable from the plot.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a) and (b) both present, both outside the axes at upper-left, same size and style. Consider matching the caption's use of bold if the journal style requires it; otherwise fine.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Needs adjustment — The overall figure is portrait (990 × 1226), unusual for two stacked frequency-domain series. Panel (a) is appropriately landscape; panel (b) is nearly as tall as (a) despite carrying a much narrower dynamic range. A height ratio of about 2:1 (a:b) would give a wider, flatter panel (b) better matched to a 0–40 Hz sweep and would remove the empty band.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — No callouts or bracketed annotations, which is good. The two legend boxes are the only floating text elements and both are candidates for replacement by direct labeling (below). The caption's key numbers (half-power widths 0.91/1.03/0.97 Hz; noise floor ~1.5 dB) are prime candidates for lightweight in-plot annotation rather than prose.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — Two detached legend boxes on line charts with three and two series respectively; both are advisory targets for inline labeling. In (a) the legend occupies roughly 18–40 Hz at 40–47 dB, and the harmonic peaks in the 14–20 Hz region rise to ~43–44 dB, so the lowest legend entry ("Welch, 9 Hann segments", ~41 dB) passes within a couple of decibels of the data. At final publication width this will read as a near-collision even if the vector paths do not strictly intersect. In (b) the legend sits in genuinely empty space below the data and does not overlap, but it duplicates color information from (a).

Note the special difficulty here: the three curves in (a) are nearly coincident by design, so endpoint inline labels cannot be separated vertically at x = 40 Hz. Direct labeling must exploit a place where the curves *do* separate — e.g. the deep valleys below 2 Hz or between harmonics, where the blue multitaper trace dips several dB below the other two.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Detached legend box in panel (a) on a three-series line chart, placed in the upper right where the 14–20 Hz peaks approach it to within ~2 dB. Risk of apparent overlap after downscaling to column width.
- [ADVISORY] Second legend box in panel (b) is largely redundant with (a); the reader pays a second lookup cost for a color code already established.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) y-range (−15 to +15 dB) is roughly 1.6× wider than the data require, wasting about a third of the panel and flattening the structure the panel exists to show.
- [ADVISORY] Zero reference line in (b) is drawn in heavy solid black, visually dominating the difference traces it is meant to serve.
- [ADVISORY] Probable top and right axis spines plus their tick marks on both panels — non-data ink.
- [ADVISORY] The figure's central quantitative claim (differences smaller than the ~1.5 dB own-noise level) is stated only in the caption and cannot be verified from the graphic.
- [ADVISORY] Overall portrait aspect ratio with equal-height panels is mismatched to the data: (b) needs far less vertical extent than (a).
- [ADVISORY] Orange and pink are adjacent in hue and will be difficult to separate in grayscale reproduction or for readers with red-green deficiency; in (b) these are the only two series.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Replace the (a) legend with three inline labels placed in the low-frequency region (roughly 0.5–2 Hz) or in a deep inter-harmonic valley where the blue multitaper trace separates from the orange and pink: label each in its own series color, staggered vertically, with short thin leader lines in the matching color so no text touches a trace. If the curves prove too coincident anywhere for clean separation, retain a single borderless legend (no frame, no background fill) and move it to the lower-right of (a) at ~30–40 Hz, 40–47 dB, where the spectrum has decayed to ~20 dB and there is genuine empty space.
- Delete the legend in panel (b) entirely and instead label the two difference traces inline near 1–4 Hz, where pink clearly exceeds orange — e.g. "Welch − multitaper" on the pink excursion and "recipe (b′) − multitaper" on the orange, each color-matched. The word "multitaper" need appear only once if the labels are written as "Welch −" / "recipe (b′) −" against a single "…− multitaper" note.
- Rescale panel (b) to approximately −8 to +13 dB and reduce its height to about half that of panel (a) (height_ratios = [2, 1]), which removes the empty band and widens the panel toward a more appropriate landscape shape for a 0–40 Hz sweep.
- Lighten the zero line in (b) to a thin gray rule (thinner than the data strokes) so it recedes behind the traces.
- Add a faint shaded horizontal band at ±1.6 dB in panel (b), labeled inline "estimator noise, 4.3√(2/ν)". This converts the caption's key claim into something the eye verifies directly and is the single highest-value addition to this figure.
- Remove top and right spines and any associated tick marks on both panels; keep only the left and bottom spines, with the bottom spine on (a) suppressed since x is shared.
- Consider replacing pink with a darker, more distinct hue (or add a dash pattern to one of the two difference traces in (b)) to survive grayscale printing.
- Optionally annotate the three half-power widths (0.91, 1.03, 0.97 Hz) as a short horizontal bar of the corresponding width drawn beside one isolated peak in (a), which shows the resolution difference rather than only stating it.

### figure8.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — three separate colourbars (c, e, g) all encode the identical 10–45 dB scale, and each repeats the "PSD (dB)" title: two of the three are pure redundant data ink. Likewise "Frequency (Hz)" appears on three stacked y-axes with identical 0–30 ranges, and the 100–500 s tick labels are repeated on all three spectrograms even though the axes are shared and vertically aligned. Elsewhere the figure is commendably lean: two-spine axes on (a), (b), (d), (f), (h), hairline gridlines lighter than the curves, no 3D, no shadows, no fills.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic — the colour/series key lives in panel (f), but the four colours are first introduced in panel (d), which the reader reaches first. This forces a forward jump and then a backtrack. The cross-referencing of (a)'s triangles to (d)/(f) is otherwise excellent and should be kept.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Two of three colourbars (redundant, not decorative — but still non-informative ink)
- Repeated colourbar axis title "PSD (dB)" ×3
- Repeated x-tick labels on (c) and (e) where the axis is shared with (g)

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate — dense but purposefully filled; the only slack is the gutter between the tall spectrograms and the right-hand column, which is what the colourbars currently consume.

**AXES:** Issues —
- (d), (f), (h) non-zero-ish baselines are fine (dB is a log quantity; (h) is a signed difference centred on 0 and does include 0).
- The colourbar is labelled only to 40 while a large saturated dark-red region exceeds the 45 dB ceiling. The upper limit is unlabelled on the bar, so the reader cannot tell where clipping begins. The ictal band — the subject of the figure — is exactly where the scale saturates, so the comparison between (c), (e), (g) in the seizure is partly hidden.
- Alignment check: (a) spans 0–~590 s with a tick at 0; the spectrograms begin at ~1 s with first tick at 100. Verify the plot boxes of (a), (c), (e), (g) share identical left/right data limits so the shaded seizure in (a) lines up pixel-for-pixel with the dashed lines below; as drawn they appear close but not obviously identical.
- Panel (a) trace is clipped at ±500 µV (caption admits this); a clipped waveform is acceptable here but should be signalled graphically, not only in prose.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — acceptable for spectra, but each estimator has a stated ν, i.e. a known confidence width. A single vertical Jeffreys/χ² CI bar per estimator in the corner of (d) and (f) would convert the ν values in the caption into visible analytical content.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(h) all present, all bold parenthesised, all set outside the top-left corner of their panel, uniform size. No action needed.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — wide time series and spectrograms, near-square spectra, compact histogram. Good match of shape to data.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — the "Before the seizure, t = 247 s" and "In the seizure, t = 318 s" notes are borderless, unfilled, and sit in empty regions. The in-panel titles on (c), (e), (g) ("Periodogram: …", "Multitaper, L = 3: …", "Recipe (b′): …") are an exemplary use of direct labelling in place of a legend. One caution: the "▼ (f)" marker in (a) sits directly above the tallest ictal excursion (~450 µV against a 500 µV ceiling) and risks colliding with the trace when the figure is reduced to column width.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — a four-entry detached key (Periodogram / Multitaper / Recipe (b′) / Welch) in the upper right of (f). It does not currently overlap data, but it imposes a lookup cost on both (d) and (f) and is placed in the *second* of the two panels it serves.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Series key placed in (f) though the colours debut in (d); creates a forward jump and backtrack.
- [ADVISORY] Detached legend on a 4-series line chart; Tufte-preferred solution is inline endpoint labels coloured to match each curve.
- [ADVISORY] Colourbar upper limit (45 dB) unlabelled while a large region of all three spectrograms saturates against it; clipping is invisible to the reader and it occurs precisely in the seizure the figure is about.
- [ADVISORY] Three identical colourbars for one shared colour scale; 2 units of redundant ink plus 2 redundant axis titles.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant x-tick labels on (c) and (e) (axis shared with (g)); redundant y-axis titles on (c)/(e)/(g).
- [ADVISORY] "▼ (f)" marker in (a) may clip the ictal waveform at publication scale.
- [ADVISORY] Clipping of the ±500 µV trace in (a) is documented only in the caption, not on the figure.
- [ADVISORY] Confirm (a) and (c)/(e)/(g) share identical time limits so the shaded seizure and dashed instants register vertically.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Replace the three colourbars with one tall colourbar spanning the full height of the (c)–(g) stack, labelled "PSD (dB)" once, with ticks at 10, 20, 30, 40 and an explicit endpoint label at 45. Add a small triangular cap at the top of the bar (or an "≥45" annotation) to declare saturation. Recovers a column of space and removes two redundant titles.
- Better still for the saturation problem: raise the ceiling to the ictal maximum, or switch the three spectrograms to a scale that resolves the seizure band, so the reader can see the difference the caption claims between (e) and (g).
- Move the series key out of (f) and into (d), and convert it to inline labels: place "Periodogram" in grey, "Multitaper" in blue, "Recipe (b′)" in gold, "Welch" in pink directly beside their curves near f ≈ 25–30 Hz in (d), where the four traces fan apart. Stagger vertically; add short same-colour leader lines if any label sits within one text-height of its curve. Delete the box in (f) entirely.
- Drop the x-tick labels from (c) and (e), keeping ticks only, with the single "Time (s)" label under (g). Keep one "Frequency (Hz)" label, vertically centred on the three-panel stack, and retain numeric y-ticks on each.
- Nudge the "▼ (f)" marker and its label upward (or place the labels above the axis frame) to guarantee clearance from the 450 µV peak at reduced scale.
- Mark the clipping in (a) directly: a pair of faint horizontal reference lines at ±500 µV, or small arrowheads on the y-axis at the clipped extremes.
- Add a single vertical error bar (per-estimator, colour-matched, annotated "95% CI") in a blank corner of (d) or (f) to make the ν values in the caption graphically legible.
- Verify axis limits are set identically for (a), (c), (e), (g) so the shaded seizure interval and the dashed instant lines align exactly across the column.

### figure9.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — Overall strong: the white resolution boxes in (b–g) are genuine data ink (window length × half-power width), the in-panel titles replace legends, and (h) uses a dot-and-line plot rather than bars. Residual non-data ink: (i) the 0–40 s tick labels are repeated on all six spectrogram panels although the axis is identical — only the bottom row (f,\,g) needs numerals; (ii) all four spectrogram spines are drawn as a full box on each of six panels (the image extent already defines the field; left + bottom suffice, or keep the frame but thin it); (iii) the rainbow (jet-like) colormap spends ink on hue transitions that do not correspond to equal PSD steps, manufacturing false contours (the granular blue/red speckle in (b) is partly a colormap artifact).

READING FLOW:     Natural — (a) → (b,c) → (d,e) → (f,g) → (h) reads top-to-bottom, left-to-right, and the row order (1 s, 2 s, 4 s) matches the row order in (h). One friction point: the single shared colourbar is vertically centred on the (d,e) row, so for panels (b,c) and especially (f,g) the eye must travel diagonally; the empty space above and below the bar makes the jump longer than necessary.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Full bounding boxes on six spectrogram panels (redundant with the image field).
  - Six repetitions of the same x-tick numerals across (b)–(g).
  - Long in-plot annotation "α=1, L=1: small-α regime" in (h) duplicates a sentence already in the caption.
  - Rainbow colormap as decorative rather than quantitative encoding (advisory, not ornament per se).

WHITE SPACE:      Mixed — panels themselves are well spaced and uncrowded, but there is a large dead rectangle at top-right (beside panel (a)) and a second at bottom-right (beside panel (h)), because the colourbar column is reserved for the full figure height yet used only in the middle third. In (h), the left ~35 % of the axis (0.40–0.75 Hz) and the strip below the "4 s" row carry no data.

AXES:             Mostly OK — Frequency axes start at 0 Hz; time axes start at 0 s. In (h) the x-axis begins at ~0.40 Hz rather than 0; this is defensible because the marks encode positions on an interval scale (and the joining line encodes a difference, not a magnitude from zero), but it is not documented. No inverted axes. Panel (a) has no y-axis for the orange 11–16 Hz trace — the "offset" label is the only cue, so the orange amplitude is not readable against the ±100 µV scale; state the offset and gain in the caption or add a short scale bar.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — none required; ν already communicates estimator variance. Note that ν is reported as a number only, so readers cannot see the stability of the spectrogram fields; this is acceptable for a methods illustration.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(h) all present, parenthesised, lower-case, same weight, all outside the axes at upper-left. Good.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — wide, short panels suit 40 s time series and spectrograms; (h) is correctly wide for a horizontal dot plot. Only refinement: (h) could lose ~25 % of its height (or the x-range could be trimmed to ~0.7–2.2 Hz) without any loss of information, which would also shorten the colourbar travel.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — the white resolution rectangles are unfilled, thin, placed in the low-power upper-left corner of each spectrogram, and overlap no structure of interest; this is an exemplary Tufte-style annotation. The in-panel titles are borderless text, no fills or shadows. The one replaceable element is the "α=1, L=1: small-α regime" string in (h), which is caption material and sits directly over the connecting line's y-level.

LEGEND STYLE:     Inline — clean, with crowding risk — No detached legend box anywhere; "Multitaper" (blue) and "Smoothing" (orange) are colour-matched inline labels, and each marker carries its own width/ν annotation. However, in the "4 s, R=1 Hz" row the two markers sit at ≈0.83 and ≈1.02 Hz, so (i) the two-line labels "0.8 Hz / ν=6.0" and "1.0 Hz / ν=8.7" nearly abut, and (ii) the series names "Multitaper" and "Smoothing" placed beneath them are separated by only a few pixels and will collide or clip at single-column publication width. Similarly, in the "2 s" row the two labels are close and the right-hand label in the "1 s" row sits near the right axis limit.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Inline series labels "Multitaper"/"Smoothing" are attached to the most tightly spaced row (4 s), where the two markers are only ~0.2 Hz apart; collision risk on down-scaling.
  - [ADVISORY] Numeric labels in (h) for the 2 s and 4 s rows are crowded horizontally; text may touch at final width.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant x-tick numerals repeated on all six spectrogram panels.
  - [ADVISORY] Full box frames on six panels; gridline-free but spine-heavy.
  - [ADVISORY] Non-zero x-origin in (h) not documented in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Rainbow colormap is perceptually non-uniform; it exaggerates mid-scale gradients and adds apparent texture in the 1 s panel.
  - [ADVISORY] Colourbar vertically centred, leaving two large empty blocks at top-right and bottom-right and lengthening the eye path from (f,g).
  - [ADVISORY] Orange 11–16 Hz trace in (a) has an undocumented offset and gain, so its amplitude is not interpretable.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Move the series names "Multitaper" and "Smoothing" to the top row ("1 s, R=2 Hz"), where the markers are ~1 Hz apart, and place them just below their markers in matching colour; alternatively keep them on the 4 s row but stagger vertically (Multitaper below-left, Smoothing above-right) or add a 2–3 pt leader line in the series colour.
  - In (h), stagger the numeric annotations: put the multitaper value above-left of its circle and the smoothing value below-right of its square, so the two never share a horizontal band.
  - Delete the in-plot "α=1, L=1: small-α regime" text (already in the caption), or shorten it to "small-α" and set it in grey so it recedes behind the markers.
  - Remove x-tick numerals from (b)–(e), keeping them only on (f) and (g); keep the tick marks themselves. Add "Time (s)" only under the bottom row (already done).
  - Reduce spectrogram frames to a hairline (0.5 pt) or drop the top and right spines.
  - Replace jet with a perceptually uniform sequential map (viridis, cividis) or, if a diverging emphasis around 0 dB is intended, a uniform diverging map centred at 0; this will remove the false speckle contours in (b) and make the panel-to-panel comparison legitimate.
  - Either extend the colourbar to span the full height of rows (b)–(g), or shorten it and move it to the top-right dead space beside (a) as a horizontal bar; this recovers both empty blocks and shortens the lookup path.
  - Trim (h) to x ∈ [0.7, 2.2] Hz and reduce its height by ~25 %; state explicitly in the caption that the axis does not start at zero because the marks encode achieved widths, not magnitudes.
  - In the caption, give the offset and any amplitude scaling of the 11–16 Hz trace in (a), or add a small vertical scale bar beside it.
