## Color & Accessibility Auditor

---
PER-FIGURE:

**efigure1.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — each hue = one kernel/estimator; black dotted = ideal box reference. No decorative fill.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive (borderline) — 5 chromatic series (blue, teal-green, amber, olive, light blue) + black in a single overlay panel. Given that the scientific point is "all main lobes are the same, skirts differ by 40 dB", the story could be carried by 2 accents + greys.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk — teal-green ("Untapered, then box") vs olive ("Hann, then box") collapse to near-identical tan/khaki under deuteranopia and protanopia, and to nearly the same mid-grey under achromatopsia. Blue vs light blue (Bohman) is only separated by lightness plus a dash pattern; in panel (b) the light-blue dashed and blue solid traces cross repeatedly in a congested region.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — looks like a hand-tuned "pleasant" set rather than a principled palette. Replace with Okabe-Ito (blue #0072B2, orange #E69F00, bluish-green #009E73, vermillion #D55E00, sky blue #56B4E9) and assign a distinct dash pattern to every series so the panel survives grayscale.

**efigure2.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — grey = raw periodogram (correctly de-emphasised), black = truth, blue = multitaper family (with linestyle encoding L and weighting), green = untapered box, amber = recipe (b′). Nesting variants of one method inside one hue is good practice.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 3 accents + grey + black.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk (mild) — green vs amber is the only critical categorical pair in panel (b), and under deuteranopia these converge toward yellow-tan. The two curves are well separated vertically in (b), so the risk is tolerable, but it is not robust in (a) where they overlap near 0.2–0.3.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good — but adopt the same principled palette as above so green→bluish-green #009E73 and amber→#E69F00 (safe pair).

**efigure3.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — hue = method family, linestyle + marker = variant. Redundant marker coding is a genuine strength here.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive — 9 series across 7 hues (blue, olive, teal-green, light blue, amber, red-orange, pink) in three panels. This is the worst offender in the set.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk — three problem pairs: (i) teal-green "Untapered, then box" vs olive "Hann, then box"; (ii) amber "cosine+parabola" vs red-orange "Hann+Gaussian" (already near-identical in normal vision, indistinguishable in CVD simulation); (iii) pink "Welch" vs light blue "Bohman" under protanopia. Markers (circle / square / triangle / diamond) rescue panels (a) and (c) partially, but the amber/red-orange pair share the same class of marker family and are the hardest to separate.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — collapse to ≤5 hues (Okabe-Ito) and let marker shape carry the remaining distinctions; or split the legend into two hue families (multitaper = blues, smoothing = warm) which is closer to the paper's actual argument.

**efigure4.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — one hue per column/route, lightness ramp within a column for bank member index. This is a well-designed, principled use of colour as a grouping cue.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 4 hues, one per route, used consistently down the column.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk (mild) — the pink (Welch) vs teal-green (sliding sinc) column identity is safe, but the within-column lightness ramps are shallow; in the teal-green column (c, g) the 4 shades are hard to order even in normal vision and merge in grayscale. Ramp endpoints should span a wider luminance range.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good structurally — swap in sequential single-hue ramps from a principled family (e.g. ColorBrewer Blues/Greens/Oranges/PuRd, 4 steps from ~L*40 to ~L*80).

**figure1.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — blue/green/pink = three routes to the same estimate; colour-matched inline text labels in (b) instead of a legend is excellent practice.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 3 accents, thick/dashed/dotted redundancy in (a).
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes (functionally) — linewidth + dash coding in (a) makes the overlay readable without colour; in (b) the two traces are 10 orders of magnitude apart. Pink vs green is the only pair and it is not a red/green pair.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good — but see the cross-figure violation below regarding which route "pink" denotes.

**figure2.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful and disciplined — grey = noisy estimate to be de-emphasised, amber = tapered estimate, black = truth and kernels. Inline colour-matched "True PSD"/"Estimate" labels.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 1 accent + neutrals. Exemplary for this set.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — amber vs black vs grey survives all three simulations.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good.

**figure3.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — amber = tapered record, black = data/total error, grey = bias and noise components (correctly subordinate).
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 1 accent + neutrals.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good. Minor: amber here denotes "the tapered record", whereas in figures 2, 6, 7, 8, 9 amber denotes the specific estimator "cosine taper + parabola / recipe (b′)". The two meanings are related but not identical; consider a note or a neutral colour for the tapered trace in (a).

**figure4.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful within the figure — hue = taper index (a, b), lightness ramp = number of tapers L (c), red-orange dashed = the 2α marker (d).
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — blue ramp + 2 accents.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk (mild) — in (a, b) the trio black / blue / teal-green is safe, but in (c) the five-step blue lightness ramp from L=1 to L=9 plus a black L=N curve is difficult to order; the lightest steps sit close to the background gridlines. In (d) the grey dotted (L=2α−1) vs red-orange dashed (2α) pair is distinguished mainly by dash pattern, which is fine.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work (minor) — use a proper sequential ramp (Blues, 5 steps, wider L* span) in (c), and note the semantic collision flagged below.

**figure5.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — one hue per construction route, lightness for bank member, bars inherit the column hue. Very clean.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 4 hues, one per column, no within-panel competition.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes (functionally) — hues never appear in the same axes, so discrimination is not load-bearing; the column titles carry the identity. Within-column lightness ramps are shallow (same issue as efigure4).
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good — widen the lightness ramps.

**figure6.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — one estimator hue per panel, black = truth, black kernels. Colour-matched inline "Estimate" label in (a).
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 1 accent per panel.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — no two accent hues share an axes.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good.

**figure7.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — blue/amber/pink = the three practical estimators; (b) uses colour-matched legend text.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 3 accents + black zero line.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes (borderline) — amber vs pink is the only tight pair; under protanopia pink desaturates toward grey-blue and amber toward yellow, so they remain separable, but the two traces are heavily interleaved in (b) and carry no linestyle redundancy. Adding a dash pattern to one would make (b) safe in grayscale.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good.

**figure8.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful for the line panels (grey/blue/amber/pink as elsewhere). The spectrogram colour is meaningful (PSD in dB) but the mapping is poorly chosen.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive in (c, e, g) — the jet colormap sweeps the entire hue circle (dark blue → cyan → green → yellow → red → dark red) for a single scalar variable.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk (severe) — jet is not CVD-safe: under deuteranopia/protanopia the green–yellow–red half of the ramp collapses, so mid-power and high-power regions become indistinguishable; under achromatopsia jet is non-monotonic in luminance and the ordering of values is destroyed entirely. Jet also creates false edges (the cyan/green and yellow/red transitions read as physical boundaries) which directly confounds the paper's claim about leakage floors and pixel-level agreement in (h).
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — replace with a perceptually uniform sequential map: viridis, magma, or cividis (cividis is explicitly CVD-optimised and would be ideal for a PSD-in-dB image).

**figure9.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — spectrogram colour = PSD; amber = band-limited trace in (a); blue circles / amber squares in (h) = multitaper vs smoothing.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive in (b–g) for the same reason as figure 8: full-hue-circle jet for one scalar.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk (severe) — same jet problems. Here it matters more, because the entire point of (b–g) is judging whether spindle blobs are resolved; false contouring in the green-to-red range will be read as structure. The white resolution box is a good, colour-independent annotation and should be retained.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — same recommendation (cividis/viridis/magma). The (a) and (h) panels are fine and consistent with the rest of the paper.

CROSS-FIGURE:
- **Same-group-different-colour (pink vs green):** In figure1, "Sliding sinc window, N_s=16N" is **pink** and "Untapered periodogram, box of width R" is **green**. In figure5 and efigure4, "Slide a sinc window" is the **green** column and **pink** is reserved for "Average segments (Welch)". The paper itself states that the sliding-sinc route, the untapered-box route and the all-taper multitaper route are the *same* estimate — so the reader is being shown pink=sinc in Fig. 1 and green=sinc in Fig. 5, while pink=Welch in Figs. 5, 6, 7 and efigure4. This is the single clearest violation of "same group = same colour" and it undermines the identity argument.
- **Two oranges with different referents:** an amber/marigold (#E6A0-ish) consistently denotes "25% cosine taper, then parabola / recipe (b′)" (efigure1–3, figures 2, 3, 6, 7, 8, 9), while a distinctly redder orange (#D9531E-ish) denotes "Hann, then Gaussian" in efigure3, the 7th Slepian taper in figure4(a), and the "2α" marker line in figure4(d). Readers scanning the figure set will read both as "the orange method".
- **Method colours reused as index colours:** figure4 uses blue = taper i=2 and green = taper i=3 and black = i=1, while blue and green are load-bearing *method* colours everywhere else (blue = multitaper, green = untapered-then-box). Within figure4 this is locally coherent but globally confusing, especially as figure4 is about the multitaper kernel.
- **Green/olive sibling problem recurs:** "Untapered, then box" (teal-green) and "Hann, then box" (olive) appear together in both efigure1 and efigure3 and are the least separable pair in the whole set under all three CVD simulations.
- **Palette harmony:** the line figures (1–7, efigures 1–4) are stylistically unified — same muted, medium-saturation family, same seaborn-style light gridlines, same panel labelling. The two spectrogram figures (8, 9) are jarring against them: full-saturation jet introduces hues (pure cyan, pure red, dark maroon) that appear nowhere else and clash with the muted accents used in figure8(d, f) *inside the same figure*.
- **Colorbar treatment inconsistent:** figure8 repeats three identical colorbars for a single shared scale ("on one colour scale of 10 to 45 dB"); figure9 correctly uses one shared colorbar for six panels.

ISSUES:
- [BLOCKING] figures 8 and 9 use the jet/rainbow colormap for PSD spectrograms. Jet is non-monotonic in luminance (fails achromatopsia and print-greyscale entirely), collapses in the green–yellow–red range under deuteranopia and protanopia, and introduces false contours that can be mistaken for spectral structure — a direct threat to the figures' claims about resolution and leakage floors.
- [BLOCKING] Same-group-different-colour violation between figure1 and figure5/efigure4: "sliding sinc window" is pink in figure1 but green in figure5/efigure4, where pink means Welch's method. Because the manuscript argues these routes are mathematically identical, the colour swap actively obstructs the argument.
- [ADVISORY] efigure3 carries 9 series in 7 hues, exceeding any reasonable economy budget, with three CVD-unsafe pairs (teal-green/olive, amber/red-orange, pink/light-blue).
- [ADVISORY] Teal-green ("untapered, then box") vs olive ("Hann, then box") are indistinguishable under deuteranopia, protanopia and greyscale in efigure1 and efigure3.
- [ADVISORY] Two distinct oranges (amber, red-orange) carry four different meanings across the set.
- [ADVISORY] figure4 reuses the method colours blue and green as taper-index colours, and its L=1…L=9 blue lightness ramp in (c) has too little luminance spread to be ordered reliably.
- [ADVISORY] Within-column lightness ramps in figure5 and efigure4 (especially the teal-green column) are too shallow to order the bank members.
- [ADVISORY] figure7(b) relies on hue alone to separate two heavily interleaved traces (amber vs pink), with no linestyle redundancy.
- [ADVISORY] figure8 repeats three identical colorbars for one shared scale; inconsistent with figure9's single shared colorbar.
- [ADVISORY] No figure states a colour-vision-safe palette or is demonstrably greyscale-robust; several panels (efigure1a/b, efigure3) would be unreadable if printed in black and white.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
- Replace jet in figures 8(c, e, g) and 9(b–g) with **cividis** (CVD-optimised, monotonic luminance) or **viridis**/**magma**. Keep the identical value range across panels and use a single shared colorbar per figure; reduce figure8 to one colorbar, as in figure9.
- Fix the sinc-window colour: either recolour figure1's "sliding sinc window" to the teal-green used for the sinc route in figure5/efigure4, or recolour figure5/efigure4's sinc column to pink and move Welch to another hue. Then add a one-line statement in the Methods or first caption: "Throughout, blue = multitaper, green = untapered periodogram / box / sliding sinc (the same estimate), amber = cosine taper + parabola (recipe b′), pink = Welch, grey = periodogram, black = truth."
- Adopt the **Okabe-Ito** palette globally for categorical series: multitaper #0072B2 (blue), untapered/box/sinc #009E73 (bluish-green), recipe (b′) #E69F00 (orange), Welch #CC79A7 (reddish-purple), Hann-then-box #F0E442 darkened or #999999, Bohman #56B4E9 (sky blue), Hann-then-Gaussian #D55E00 (vermillion). This removes the green/olive and amber/red-orange collisions at a stroke.
- In efigure1 and efigure3, add redundant encoding: a unique dash pattern per series in efigure1, and in efigure3 make marker shape the primary discriminator (already partly done) while cutting to ≤5 hues; consider splitting efigure3's legend into "multitaper variants" (blue family, varying dash) and "taper + smoother variants" (warm family, varying marker).
- In figure4, recolour panels (a, b) to a neutral/sequential scheme (e.g. black, mid-grey, light-grey with distinct dashes, or a single-hue Blues ramp) so that blue and green are not overloaded with a non-method meaning; widen the L-ramp in (c) to span ~L*35–L*85 and label the L=1 and L=9 curves inline.
- Widen the within-column lightness ramps in figure5 and efigure4 to a 4-step ColorBrewer sequential scale per hue, and label the lightest/darkest member inline (or keep the existing i=1…4 legend but reorder it dark→light to match perceptual order).
- Add a dash pattern to one of the two overlaid traces in figure7(b), and to the amber/pink pair in figure8(d, f).
- Verify all figures with a CVD simulator (e.g. `colorspace::deutan/protan/desaturate`, Coblis, or Color Oracle) and confirm each remains readable when converted to greyscale; state this check in the figure-preparation note.
- Retain and extend the practices this manuscript already does well: colour-matched inline labels instead of legends (figures 1b, 2a, 6a, 9h), grey for de-emphasised raw periodograms, black for ground truth, and one accent hue per panel in figures 2 and 6.

## Typography & Annotation Inspector

---
PER-FIGURE:

**efigure1.png** (double column, ~2063 px → 6.875 in ≈ 300 dpi)
```
FONT:               Consistent — single sans-serif for all text; math symbols
                    (f, N, Δ, L, ν, R) set in matching sans italic. Only the
                    calligraphic 𝒦 appears to come from a different math face;
                    verify it is not Computer Modern.
SIZE:               Adequate — tick labels ≈7–7.5 pt, axis titles ≈8.5 pt,
                    legend ≈8 pt, panel labels ≈10 pt. Smallest element:
                    y-tick labels ≈7 pt.
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: dB is standard (OK); "Hann", "Bohman" are proper
                    names (OK). ν is defined in caption. No undefined items in
                    the figure itself, but ν, R, α, L rely entirely on the
                    caption/main text.
AXIS LABELS:        Complete in the sense that all quantities are dimensionless
                    (fNΔ, 𝒦(f)/NΔ) or in dB. Recommend marking the
                    dimensionless axes explicitly, e.g. "fNΔ (dimensionless)".
STAT ANNOTATIONS:   Not present.
TITLE PLACEMENT:    Correct (no title inside figure).
```

SIZE:               Adequate — ticks ≈7 pt, axis titles ≈8.5 pt, legend ≈8 pt,
                    panel labels ≈10 pt.
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: "PSD" (used in the in-figure legend "True PSD");
                    defined only in the figure1 caption. "AR(4)" in the caption
                    is not expanded here. Expand both in this caption
                    (supplementary figures are often read standalone).
AXIS LABELS:        Ambiguous units — "Ŝ(f) (dB)" gives no reference level
                    (dB re what?). Simulated data, so state "dB re arbitrary

CROSS-FIGURE:
- **Font family:** consistent — one sans-serif family across all 13 figures, with math set in a matching sans italic. The only risk is the calligraphic 𝒦 (efigure1, efigure4, figure2, figure4, figure6), which is drawn from a math font and may be a serif (Computer Modern) glyph; confirm it matches, or replace with an upright sans "K".
- **Panel-label style:** consistent — lowercase letter in parentheses "(a)", "(b)", … in all figures, matching the captions. Good.
- **Capitalization of axis titles is INCONSISTENT:** efigure3 uses all-lowercase titles ("half-power width ×NΔ", "kernel power beyond twice the half-power half-width", "peak beyond 1.5 times the half-power half-width (dB)"), whereas figure1/3/7/8/9 use sentence case with an initial capital ("Frequency (Hz)", "Time (s)", "RMS error (dB)", "Difference (dB)", "Achieved half-power width (Hz)", "Pixels (thousands)"). Pick one convention (sentence case recommended) and apply everywhere.
- **dB reference level is INCONSISTENT:** figure9 correctly gives "PSD (dB re 1 µV²/Hz)"; figure1, figure7 and figure8 plot the same kind of real EEG data but label only "dB"; figure2, figure6, efigure2 label "Ŝ(f) (dB)" and "𝒦(f) (dB)" with no stated reference. Harmonise: "dB re 1 µV²/Hz" for EEG panels, "dB re arbitrary units" for simulated PSDs, "dB re peak" for kernels.
- **Quantity notation is INCONSISTENT:** the PSD axis is "Ŝ(f) (dB)" in efigure2, figure1, figure2, figure6, figure7 but "PSD (dB)" in figure8 and "PSD (dB re 1 µV²/Hz)" in figure9. Use one label for the same quantity throughout (or state the equivalence once).
- **Font sizes for equivalent elements:** approximately consistent when each figure is placed at its implied width (990 px files → 3.25 in single column; ~1830–2090 px files → 6.875 in double column). However the multi-panel grids (efigure4, figure8, figure9, and the insets of figure2/figure6) run ~1–2 pt smaller than the sparse figures (efigure1, figure1, figure4), so equivalent elements are not equal across the set. Standardise on one stylesheet: ticks 7.5 pt, axis titles 9 pt, legends/annotations 8 pt, panel labels 10 pt bold, and let panel count change panel size rather than font size.
- **In-figure legend vs. key style is INCONSISTENT:** boxed/standard legends in efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure1, figure4, figure5, figure7, figure8(f), figure9(h); in-plot coloured text keys in figure1(b), figure2(a) ("True PSD"/"Estimate"), figure3(a), figure6(a). Unify, or at minimum make the coloured text keys the same size and weight as legend entries.
- **Shared-axis labelling is INCONSISTENT:** figure2/figure6 (top row), figure8 (c,e) and figure9 (b–e) omit x-axis titles on upper rows, while other figures label every panel. State the convention in each caption.
- **Supplementary vs main abbreviation coverage:** PSD is expanded only in the figure1 caption; the e-figures (which are read separately from the main text) use PSD, AR(4) and ν without local definition. EEG is never expanded in any caption.

ISSUES:
- [BLOCKING] Undefined abbreviations across the set: "EEG" (figure1, figure7, figure8, figure9) is never expanded; "PSD" is expanded only in the figure1 caption yet used in-figure/in-caption in efigure2, figure2, figure6, figure8, figure9; "AR(4)" is unexpanded in efigure2 and figure3; "ELROND", "C4", "M1", "C4-M1", "common average" and sleep stage "N2" are undefined (figure8, figure9); "Recipe (b′)" appears as an in-figure label in figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9 without a self-contained definition; "max h" (figure5c, efigure4c) and "Q" (efigure4 caption) are undefined.
- [BLOCKING] dB axes without a reference level on real-data figures: figure1(a) "Ŝ(f) (dB)", figure7(a) "Ŝ(f) (dB)", figure8 colorbars and (d)/(f) y-axes "PSD (dB)". These are µV-scaled EEG spectra and must state the reference (figure9 already does it correctly), otherwise the values are uninterpretable.
- [BLOCKING] Axis label / caption mismatch in figure4: panel (b) y-axis is "|H^(i)(f)|²/NΔ²" but the caption writes "|H^(i)(f)|²/Δ"; panel (c) y-axis is "𝒦(f)/NΔ" but the caption describes "Σλ_i|H^(i)(f)|²/2αΔ". One of the two is wrong.
- [BLOCKING] Cross-figure capitalization inconsistency: efigure3's three axis titles are lowercase while all other figures use an initial capital.
- [ADVISORY] Smallest text approaches the 6 pt floor at the intended widths: efigure4 in-panel ν annotations, column headers and legends (~6–6.5 pt); the inset tick labels of figure2(e–h) and figure6(e–h) (~6–6.5 pt); figure8 colorbar labels and figure9(h) point annotations (~7 pt). These break the floor if the figures are reduced at all in production.
- [ADVISORY] Index and dimensionless axes carry bare symbols with no descriptor or unit statement: "k", "i" (figure4, figure5, efigure4), "ν" as the sole y-axis label (efigure3a), "x_k" (figure3a), "fNΔ", "fΔ", "RNΔ", "N_s/N", "𝒦(f)/NΔ", "c_i/Σc_i", "1 − λ_i", and efigure3(b)'s fractional y-axis.
- [ADVISORY] Numeric inconsistency between figure and caption: figure2(d) annotates "ν = 98" while the caption gives 98.4 (and figure3's annotation says "too smooth, ν = 98").
- [ADVISORY] In-figure column/panel headers (efigure4 a–d, figure5 a–d, figure8 c/e/g, figure9 b–g) function as mini-titles inside the figure. This is normally acceptable for method/condition labels, but if the target journal forbids any in-figure titling, move them to the caption and keep only panel letters.
- [ADVISORY] Low-contrast text: the grey "Estimate" key in figure2(a) and the light-green "Estimate" key in figure6(a) sit on white at ~8 pt; the pink "Maximum |difference|" annotation in figure1(c) is also low contrast.
- [ADVISORY] Typography: en dashes needed for numeric ranges in figure9(a) in-plot text ("0.3−35 Hz", "11−16 Hz" use minus signs) and check caption ranges (e.g. "0.3 and 35 Hz") for consistency.
- [ADVISORY] Mixed legend conventions (boxed legends vs. coloured in-plot text keys) across the set.
- [ADVISORY] Verify the intended column widths: the 990 px files (figure3, figure7) are single-column-scaled and the ~1830–2090 px files are double-column-scaled. If any of the large multi-panel figures (efigure4, figure8, figure9) were placed at single-column width, all of their text would fall to ~3–4 pt.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
- Create one plotting stylesheet (rcParams) and regenerate all 13 figures from it: single sans-serif family (Helvetica/Arial) for both text and mathtext, tick labels 7.5 pt, axis titles 9 pt, legend/annotation 8 pt, panel labels 10 pt bold, inset tick labels 7 pt minimum. Control panel size, not font size, when panel count grows.
- Add a standing abbreviation block to the first caption and repeat the minimum set in every caption that uses them: "EEG, electroencephalogram; PSD, power spectral density; AR(4), fourth-order autoregressive process; RMS, root-mean-square; ν, equivalent degrees of freedom; R, design resolution (half-power width); L, number of tapers; α, time-bandwidth product (NΔR/2)." Define "recipe (b′)" in full in every caption where the label appears (figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9).
- Expand electrode/dataset terms: "channel C4 (international 10–20 system) referenced to the common average", "C4-M1 (right central derivation referenced to the left mastoid)", spell out ELROND at first use with a citation, and "stage N2 (AASM criteria)".
- Standardise all dB axes: EEG panels → "PSD (dB re 1 µV²/Hz)"; simulated spectra → "Ŝ(f) (dB re arbitrary units)"; kernels → "𝒦(f) (dB re peak)". Use one symbol/word for the PSD axis across all figures (either Ŝ(f) or PSD, not both).
- Append "(dimensionless)" or a descriptor to bare-symbol axes: "k (sample index)", "i (taper index)", "Equivalent degrees of freedom, ν", "Normalized frequency, fΔ (dimensionless)", "fNΔ (cycles per record)", "Kernel power beyond twice the half-power half-width (fraction of total)".
- Recheck and reconcile figure4's panel (b) and (c) y-axis normalisations against the caption and equations; fix figure2(d)'s ν annotation to 98.4 (or round consistently everywhere to 98).
- Convert efigure3's three axis titles to sentence case with an initial capital, matching figures 1, 3, 7, 8, 9.
- Increase inset tick-label size in figure2(e–h) and figure6(e–h) to ≥7 pt, and raise efigure4's in-panel annotations, column headers and legends to 7.5–8 pt (or promote efigure4 to a full-page landscape supplementary figure).
- Darken the in-plot "Estimate"/"True PSD" and "Maximum |difference|" key text to at least 60% contrast, or replace these coloured text keys with standard legends so that legend styling is uniform across the set.
- Replace minus signs with en dashes in all numeric ranges in figure9(a) and check captions for the same.
- Either label the x-axis of every panel, or add one sentence to each multi-row caption stating that upper-row panels share the x-axis of the bottom row.
- State the intended reproduction width for each figure in the submission (990 px files at 3.25 in; all others at 6.875 in) so that typesetting cannot shrink the dense panels below the 6 pt floor.

---

## Technical Format Checker

### efigure1.png

---

**DIMENSIONS:** 2063 × 677 px (aspect ratio 3.05:1) — recommended **double column** (181 mm / 7.16 in) — **288 DPI** at that width
 - At single-column width (88 mm / 3.5 in): 589 DPI, but the 3.05:1 aspect ratio would render the two panels only ~28 mm tall each, and all annotation would fall to ~4 pt. Single column is not viable without re-layout (e.g., stacking (a) over (b)).
 - At double column the figure height is 677/288 = 2.35 in ≈ 60 mm — well within the 230 mm limit.

**RESOLUTION:** **Below threshold** — line art (vector-style 2-D line plots, no photographic content or gradients); actual 288 DPI at the recommended 181 mm width. IEEE requires ≥300 DPI minimum and prefers 600 DPI for colored line art.

**FORMAT:** **Needs conversion** — PNG → TIFF (LZW), EPS, or PDF for final submission. PNG is acceptable for the review upload only.

**FILE SIZE:** OK — 0.2 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample headroom to increase resolution or embed vectors.

**TEXT RENDERING:** **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick numerals, legend entries, and the math glyphs ($\mathcal{K}(f)/N\Delta$, $fN\Delta$, $L=7$) are pixel data in a PNG. Subscript/italic math and the thin dotted box lines are the first elements to degrade.

**COLOR MODE:** Assumed **RGB** (no color_mode field supplied in the metadata block — please confirm). RGB is correct for IEEE. If a CMYK conversion is applied downstream, the saturated blue (multitaper), orange (cosine-taper/parabola) and green (untapered) traces are the most at risk of shifting; the green/olive pair could become hard to separate.

**ISSUES:**
- **[BLOCKING]** Resolution at the recommended double-column width is 288 DPI, below the 300 DPI minimum for colored line art and far below the 600 DPI preference.
- **[BLOCKING]** Text is rasterized. Rasterized text in line-art figures is disallowed at this quality level; the math-heavy axis labels will show pixel edges at print size.
- **[BLOCKING]** PNG is not an accepted final-submission format; convert to TIFF/EPS/PDF.
- **[ADVISORY]** Estimated type size at 181 mm is ~7–8 pt for tick labels — at or just under the 8 pt preferred floor and uncomfortably close to the 6 pt hard floor. Verify after regeneration.
- **[ADVISORY]** Color/line encoding is partly redundant but not fully accessible: "Untapered, then box" (green) vs. "Hann, then box" (olive) are both solid and similar in hue; "Multitaper" (blue) vs. "Bohman" (light blue) differ mainly in lightness (Bohman is at least dashed). Print-grayscale or deuteranopic readers may confuse green/olive.
- **[ADVISORY]** Panel (b)'s dB curves are clipped at the axis bottom (−100 dB) without a statement that the floor is a plotting limit rather than the kernel value; the Bohman curve runs off the frame near |fNΔ| ≈ 20.
- **[ADVISORY]** Caption uses "recipe (b$'$)" in the same sentence space as panel label (b); readers may momentarily conflate the recipe label with the panel label.
- **[ADVISORY]** Statistical reporting: the caption correctly gives the equivalent degrees of freedom ν for every estimator compared (14.0, 16.7, 17.2, 8.9, 7.3), which satisfies the "state the estimator's degrees of freedom where noise levels are compared" requirement. However, it does not state whether these kernels are computed analytically/deterministically or averaged over realizations — no error bars are shown, so the basis must be made explicit.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Re-export directly from the plotting library as **vector PDF or EPS** sized to exactly 181 mm width (e.g., matplotlib `figsize=(7.16, 2.35)`, `savefig(..., format='pdf')`). This simultaneously resolves the resolution, text-rendering, and format blockers and keeps file size trivial.
- If a raster deliverable is mandatory, regenerate at **600 DPI for a 181 mm width = 4276 × 1404 px** and save as TIFF with LZW compression (expected well under 10 MB).
- Set all text to a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) at **8 pt minimum at final size**; verify tick numerals, legend, and axis labels after resizing rather than scaling a completed figure.
- Consider a slightly less extreme aspect ratio (e.g., 181 × 75–85 mm) to give the legend and tick labels more room at 8 pt; the current 3.05:1 is very flat for two panels with six legend entries.
- Strengthen non-color encoding: give each of the five estimators a distinct dash pattern (e.g., solid, long-dash, dash-dot, dotted-dense, dash-dot-dot) or increase the lightness separation between green and olive, so the panels remain readable in grayscale.
- In panel (b), either extend the y-axis to capture the Bohman skirt or add a note that −100 dB is the display floor; keep the dotted ideal-box lines at ≥0.5 pt so they survive rasterization.
- Confirm/declare **RGB** color mode on upload and do not pre-convert to CMYK; if a CMYK proof is generated, check the blue and orange traces for hue shift.
- Add one clause to the caption stating how the kernels were obtained, e.g. "Kernels are computed analytically from the taper designs (no Monte-Carlo averaging); ν denotes the equivalent degrees of freedom of each estimator." This makes the absence of error bars unambiguous.
- Optionally reword to avoid the label collision: "…followed by a parabola of the same half-power width (ν = 16.7, orange), i.e. recipe b′…".

### efigure2.png

---

**DIMENSIONS:** 2078 × 737 px — recommended **double column** — **302 DPI** at 6.875 in (301.9); at the 181 mm full-measure trim this falls to **291 DPI**. At single column (3.25 in) the file would be 639 DPI, but see text-size note below.

- Aspect ratio 2.82:1. At double-column width the printed height is 2.44 in (62 mm), well inside the 230 mm limit — appropriate for a two-panel side-by-side spectral comparison.
- Single-column placement is **not** viable: scaling 2078 px down to 3.25 in shrinks all type by a factor of 2.1, putting tick labels and the 7-entry legend near 3–4 pt.

**RESOLUTION:** Borderline / Below preferred — figure type: **line art** (axis rules, curves, text; no photographic content or continuous-tone gradients). 302 DPI at 6.875 in meets the 300 DPI color line-art minimum but is well short of the 600 DPI preferred for line art, and drops below the 300 DPI floor (291 DPI) if the figure is set to the full 181 mm column measure.

**FORMAT:** Needs conversion — **PNG → PDF/EPS (vector) preferred, or ≥600 DPI TIFF** if raster is unavoidable. PNG is acceptable for initial review only.

**FILE SIZE:** OK — 0.24 MB (limit 10 MB). There is ample headroom to supply a much higher-resolution or vector file.

**TEXT RENDERING:** **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick numerals, panel letters, and legend entries are baked into the bitmap. Italic math ($f\Delta$, $\hat S(f)$, $L$) and subscript-scale glyphs are the first to degrade at this sampling density.

**COLOR MODE:** OK for submission (RGB required) — but flag for print: the teal-green ("Untapered, then box"), saturated blue (multitaper), and amber-orange (cosine taper + parabola) curves are exactly the hues most prone to shift under CMYK conversion. The green and blue traces overlap in the high-frequency floor of panel (b), where a hue shift plus the dense blue "grass" would make them hard to separate.

**ISSUES:**
- **[BLOCKING]** Rasterized text in a line-art figure. All labels are pixel-based; at 302 DPI effective output the stroke edges of the italic math symbols will be visibly stepped in print.
- **[BLOCKING]** Format: PNG is not an accepted final-submission format for IEEE TBME (TIFF/EPS/PDF/PS required).
- **[BLOCKING if set to 181 mm]** 2078 px across 181 mm = 291 DPI, below the 300 DPI minimum for color line art. Only compliant if typeset at ≤176 mm.
- **[ADVISORY]** Resolution is less than half the 600 DPI preferred for line art; thin grey periodogram traces and the dotted/dashed blue distinctions are the features most likely to alias.
- **[ADVISORY]** Line-style encoding carries critical information (solid = L=7, dashed = L=6, dotted = adaptive) in a single hue. In panel (a) the dashed L=6 trace is essentially invisible under the solid L=7 noise band; a reader cannot verify the "dropping the seventh taper halves that" claim from panel (a).
- **[ADVISORY]** Statistical reporting is mostly complete (ν is given for each smoothed estimator and "one realization" is stated), but the **adaptively weighted L=7 estimate has no ν reported**. Because adaptive weights make the effective degrees of freedom frequency-dependent, this omission matters in a figure whose stated purpose is comparing noise floors.
- **[ADVISORY]** Panel (b) plots differences of estimates with *different* ν (12, 14, 16.7, 17.2) on a common axis; the visual "width" of each band is therefore partly an artifact of differing dof rather than bias. This should be stated explicitly.
- **[ADVISORY]** Caption opens with a title-like fragment ("Dropping the leakiest taper."). IEEE style prefers a descriptive caption sentence rather than an embedded title.
- **[ADVISORY]** The in-axes legend occupies a large part of panel (a)'s upper-right quadrant; at 7–8 pt final size it is legible but crowds the data.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Re-export directly from the plotting library as **vector PDF or EPS** with fonts embedded (e.g. `savefig('efigure2.pdf')` with `pdf.fonttype=42` / `ps.fonttype=42`). This resolves the rasterized-text and format issues simultaneously and keeps the file far under 10 MB.
- If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at **≥600 DPI for the final 181 mm width** (≥4280 px wide) as RGB TIFF with LZW compression, not by upsampling the existing PNG.
- Set the figure width in the plotting script to the physical target (7.13 in / 181 mm) and specify fonts in points (8–9 pt for tick labels and legend, 9–10 pt for axis labels) so type size is correct by construction rather than by scaling.
- Add ν for the adaptively weighted estimate (or state that it varies with frequency and give its range), and add one clause to the caption noting that band widths in (b) reflect the differing ν listed in (a).
- Improve discriminability of the three multitaper variants: give L=6 and the adaptive estimate distinct hues (or plot them in a separate inset/panel), and verify the green/blue/orange set survives a grayscale and a deuteranopia check.
- Confirm the journal will not CMYK-convert; if it will, pre-check the teal-green and amber against a CMYK proof and desaturate slightly if the green/blue separation in panel (b) collapses.
- Consider moving the 7-entry legend outside the axes (e.g. a horizontal legend spanning both panels below the x-axes) to free the peak region of panel (a).

### efigure3.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2086 x 1307 px — recommended **double column** (181 mm / 7.16 in) — **291 DPI** at that width (303 DPI if laid out at 6.875 in; 596 DPI if forced to 88 mm single column)
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (multi-panel line/marker plots, no photographic content); 291 DPI at double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum and 600 DPI preferred for colour line art
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG (review-acceptable only) → PDF or EPS (vector) preferred; TIFF at ≥600 DPI acceptable as a raster fallback
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.28 MB (limit 10 MB); large headroom for a 600-DPI re-render
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick labels, legend entries and panel letters are bitmap; diagonal strokes in the italic math glyphs ($\nu$, $\alpha$, $\Delta$) and in "%" show pixel stepping
COLOR MODE:     OK (RGB assumed; not reported in metadata) — nine-series palette includes saturated blue, teal-green, olive, orange and magenta; these are the hues most at risk if the production house converts to CMYK, and the two blues (Multitaper solid/dashed) plus the two greens (Untapered vs. Hann) are already close in hue
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at the intended double-column width is 291 DPI — below the 300 DPI floor and well below the 600 DPI target for colour line art. Fine dotted lines (the $8/(N\Delta)$ marker, the sine-taper series) and open-circle/open-square markers will look soft or broken in print.
  - [BLOCKING] Text is rasterized. For line-art figures IEEE expects vector text; at 291 DPI the 6–7 pt tick labels will not survive printing cleanly.
  - [ADVISORY] Format: PNG is accepted for review but not for final submission; convert before the production stage.
  - [ADVISORY] Estimated final type size at 181 mm width is ~7–7.5 pt for axis labels and ~6–6.5 pt for tick labels — legal but at the bottom of the 8–10 pt preferred range, and the tick labels sit essentially at the 6 pt hard floor. Single-column use is not viable: all type would fall to ~3–4 pt.
  - [ADVISORY] Layout inefficiency: the lower-right quadrant contains only the legend, leaving roughly a quarter of the canvas as whitespace. This inflates the physical width needed per panel and is the root cause of the small type. Panel (b)'s two-line y-axis label and panels (a)/(c)'s multi-line y-axis labels also consume width.
  - [ADVISORY] Colour/linestyle redundancy: "Multitaper, $L=2\alpha-1$" (solid blue, open circle), "$L=2\alpha-2$" (dashed blue, open square) and "sine tapers" (dotted blue, filled triangle) are distinguished mainly by dash pattern; at 291 DPI the dotted and dashed variants are hard to separate where curves cross near x ≈ 5–8 in panel (c). Likewise olive "Hann, then box" vs. teal "Untapered, then box".
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is satisfied but could be more explicit: the caption correctly states the curves are exact kernel and degrees-of-freedom computations with no sampling variability (hence no error bars are required), and reports $\nu$ as the estimator's degrees of freedom in panel (a). Consider stating in-caption that $N=256$ applies to all three panels and that no averaging over realizations was performed, so a reader does not look for missing error bars.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting source as **PDF or EPS with embedded vector text** (matplotlib: `savefig('efigure3.pdf')` with `pdf.fonttype=42`/Type 42 fonts). This resolves both blocking issues at once and will remain well under 10 MB.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥600 DPI for a 181 mm final width, i.e. **≥4280 px wide** (target ~4280 x 2680 px), as RGB TIFF with LZW compression.
  - Move the legend into a horizontal strip beneath the three panels (3 columns x 3 rows) or inside the empty upper-right region of panel (b), then re-tile the panels as 2x2 or 1x3. Recovering the wasted quadrant lets every panel grow ~15–20% at the same page width, lifting tick labels to 8 pt.
  - Set axis label text to 9 pt and tick labels to 8 pt **at the final 181 mm width** (i.e. specify sizes after deciding the physical width, not in figure-relative units).
  - Increase the $8/(N\Delta)$ reference line from a fine dotted to a 0.8 pt dashed grey line so it survives rasterization; similarly raise all data line widths to ≥0.75 pt at final size.
  - Differentiate the three multitaper variants by marker shape and a small lightness offset in addition to dash pattern, and shift "Hann, then box" further from "Untapered, then box" in hue; verify legibility in greyscale and with a deuteranopia simulation.
  - Confirm and declare RGB in the submission metadata; if the publisher converts to CMYK, request a proof check on the magenta (Welch) and orange (cosine-taper) series.
  - Caption: it is correctly placed below the figure with lowercase panel labels and no in-figure title — no change needed there. Add "$N=256$ throughout; curves are deterministic (no Monte-Carlo averaging), so no error bars are shown" for unambiguous statistical reporting, and consider replacing the sentence "the legend is at the lower right" with a layout-independent phrasing in case the legend is repositioned during production.

---
DIMENSIONS:     2088 x 1934 px — recommended **double column** (181 mm / 7.16 in) — **292 DPI** at that width

  - Aspect ratio 1.08:1 → height at 181 mm = 168 mm, within the 230 mm limit. Ratio is fine for a full-width block figure.

RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (vector-style graphs, dotted rules, bar charts, no photographic content); actual 292 DPI at 181 mm vs. 300 DPI minimum and 600 DPI preferred for line art.

FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → PDF or EPS (vector) preferred; TIFF (LZW, ≥600 DPI) acceptable if raster must be kept. PNG is acceptable for review upload only.

FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.42 MB (limit 10 MB). Ample headroom to re-export at 600 DPI or as vector.

TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis numerals, italic math labels ($h_k^{(i)}$, $fN\Delta$, $\nu$), and legend entries are pixel-based (PNG container). Sub-pixel antialiasing is visible on thin glyph strokes and on the dotted vertical rules in (m)–(p).

COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB) — **potential shift if CMYK is ever applied**: the saturated teal/green (column 3) and the mid-orange (column 2) are the two most at-risk hues; the light tint steps (i = 3, 4 traces at ~20–30 % tint) may also flatten together in CMYK. Keep RGB and do not let the typesetter auto-convert.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at the recommended 181 mm width is 292 DPI, below the 300 DPI floor for colour line art and far below the 600 DPI preference. Raster figure cannot be up-sampled to fix this.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and all line work are rasterized in a 16-panel line-art figure; thin curves (the pale i = 4 tapers, the ±R/2 dotted lines, the −80 dB gridlines) will alias or partially drop out at print size.
  - [ADVISORY] Type size is marginal: at 181 mm the tick numerals and legend text measure roughly 7 pt, inside the 6 pt hard floor but below the 8–10 pt preferred band. Axis labels are ~8 pt. After conversion, verify no glyph is under 6 pt.
  - [ADVISORY] In-figure column titles ("Average segments (Welch)", "Smooth one periodogram", "Slide a sinc window", "Multitaper, L = 7") function as titles inside the figure, which the journal disallows. Panel letters (a)–(p) in lowercase parentheses are compliant.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting incomplete. The equivalent degrees of freedom ν (13.4, 16.7, 17.2, 14.0) appear in (i)–(l) but the caption never names the estimator's DOF, nor the record length N, sampling interval Δ, segment count / overlap for the Welch column, smoothing bandwidth for column 2, or the taper count L = 7 and time-bandwidth product for the multitaper column. The number of realizations/windows behind each ν must be stated even though no error bars are plotted.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption opens with "Fig. 5 in full" and defers colour/column definitions to Fig. 5; a supplementary/extended figure should be self-contained.
  - [ADVISORY] Series within each panel are distinguished by tint alone (i = 1…4). The lightest tint in (d), (e), (f) is low-contrast against white and will be hard to follow in print or for colour-deficient readers.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting library as vector PDF or EPS at exactly 181 mm width (`figsize=(7.16, 6.63)`), with fonts embedded (Type 42/TrueType, `pdf.fonttype=42`); this resolves both blocking items at once.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandatory, re-render at ≥600 DPI for 181 mm (≥4280 px wide) and save as LZW-compressed TIFF, RGB.
  - Set base font to 8 pt Arial/Helvetica at the 7.16 in figure size; increase tick-label size from its current ~7 pt equivalent and keep legend text at 8 pt.
  - Increase linewidth of the pale tint traces and the ±R/2 dotted rules to ≥0.75 pt so they survive print reproduction; consider darkening the i = 4 tint or adding a dash pattern.
  - Move the four column titles out of the image and into the caption (e.g. "columns, left to right: averaged segments (Welch); smoothed periodogram; sliding sinc window; Thomson multitaper, L = 7").
  - Expand the caption with: N = 256 (or actual), Δ, R and NW, number of segments and overlap fraction for column 1, smoothing kernel half-width for column 2, and an explicit statement that ν is the equivalent chi-square degrees of freedom of each estimator, with the number of windows/realizations contributing to each value.
  - Confirm RGB is preserved end-to-end; if the publisher requires CMYK proofs, pre-check the teal and orange columns and substitute slightly desaturated equivalents.

### figure1.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     1830 x 1052 px (aspect 1.74:1) — recommended **double column** (181 mm / 7.16 in) — **256 DPI** at that width (522 DPI if forced to 88 mm single column; 266 DPI at the 6.875 in text-width convention)
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (multi-panel line plots, no photographic content); 256 DPI at the required 7.16 in width, vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG acceptable for initial review only → **PDF or EPS (vector) preferred; TIFF at ≥600 DPI acceptable** for final submission
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.28 MB (limit 10 MB); a 600 DPI TIFF replacement (~4300 x 2470 px, LZW) will still be well under the limit
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick labels, legend and in-panel annotations are bitmap pixels in the PNG; stroke edges on the dotted pink/green traces show pixel stair-stepping
COLOR MODE:     OK (RGB assumed; color mode not supplied in metadata — confirm RGB, not CMYK or indexed/palette PNG). Potential shift only if a print CMYK conversion is later applied: the saturated teal-blue (multitaper), sea-green (box) and magenta-pink (sinc) are all in the at-risk gamut region and the blue/green pair could lose separation.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at the recommended double-column width is ~256 DPI, below the 300 DPI floor for color line art (and far below the 600 DPI preferred value). Enlarging the existing PNG will not fix this.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and curves are rasterized. For line art, IEEE expects vector text/strokes or ≥600 DPI raster; at 256 DPI the thin dotted pink series in (a) and the dense noise traces in (b) will alias and partially disappear in print.
  - [ADVISORY] Figure cannot be reduced to single-column width: measured glyph height is ~28 px ≈ 7.9 pt at 181 mm, which would become ~4 pt at 88 mm — below the 6 pt absolute minimum. Double column is therefore mandatory, which makes the resolution deficit unavoidable unless the file is regenerated.
  - [ADVISORY] Fonts are serif (Times-like) throughout. Times is listed as acceptable, but a sans-serif (Arial/Helvetica) family is preferred for IEEE TBME figures; whichever is chosen, it should match across all three panels and the math italics (N, λ_i, N_s, R, α).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) shows three curves that coincide by construction, so the green dashed and pink dotted series are almost entirely hidden beneath the thick blue line; at 256 DPI the dotted series may vanish. The visual claim of agreement is carried entirely by panel (b).
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical/estimator reporting is partially satisfied: the caption gives N = 1024, one epoch, and ν = 17 equivalent degrees of freedom for the multitaper estimate. It does not state the degrees of freedom (or equivalent smoothing bandwidth) of the two comparison estimators — the box-smoothed periodogram and the sliding sinc-window average — even though panels (b) and (c) compare their noise/bias behaviour directly. Journal policy requires the estimator's degrees of freedom wherever noise levels are compared.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) plots single points with no indication of variability; the caption should state explicitly that each point is from the same single 8 s epoch (one realization, no averaging across windows/records) so readers do not read the markers as means.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) y-axis spans 10⁻¹⁵ to 10⁰ with decade gridlines only at 10⁰, 10⁻⁵, 10⁻¹⁰, 10⁻¹⁵; the "3×10⁻¹³ dB" agreement quoted in the caption is near double-precision floor and should be flagged as numerically limited rather than a physical agreement level.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption is dense; the sentence describing the sliding sinc window runs long. Also "de-identified scalp EEG ... from the authors' archive" should point to an IRB/ethics statement elsewhere in the manuscript.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting script as **vector PDF or EPS** sized to 181 mm width (e.g. matplotlib `figsize=(7.16, 4.12)`, `savefig('figure1.pdf')`). This resolves both blocking issues at once and keeps the file far under 10 MB.
  - If a raster is required, re-render at **600 DPI** for the 7.16 in width → ~4300 x 2470 px, saved as LZW-compressed RGB TIFF. Do not upsample the existing 1830 px PNG.
  - Set all fonts at final size: 8–9 pt for tick labels, 9–10 pt for axis labels, 8 pt for legend and in-panel annotations; embed fonts (`pdf.fonttype: 42`) so no text is converted to outlines-as-bitmap.
  - Increase visibility of the coincident traces in (a): reduce the blue line to ~1.5 pt, increase dot/dash spacing and marker weight for the green and pink series, or add a short zoomed inset showing the three curves resolved.
  - Verify the color mode is RGB (not indexed PNG); if a CMYK print version is ever produced, re-check blue/green separation or swap the sea-green for a higher-luminance-contrast hue.
  - Add to the caption the equivalent degrees of freedom (or equivalent bandwidth) of the box-smoothed periodogram and the sliding sinc-window estimator alongside the multitaper ν = 17, and state explicitly that panels (b) and (c) derive from the same single realization (one 8 s epoch, no averaging over windows).
  - Note in the caption that the ~3×10⁻¹³ dB agreement in (b) is at the double-precision arithmetic floor.
  - Keep panel labels as lowercase (a), (b), (c) — currently compliant — and confirm no title text is placed inside the figure canvas (currently compliant).

Both are far below the 600 DPI preferred for line art, and 291 DPI at 181 mm is below the 300 DPI hard floor.

FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.2 MB (limit 10 MB; ample headroom for a 600 DPI or vector replacement)
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all labels, tick numerals, and math symbols are bitmap; anti-aliased pixel edges visible on the italic *f*Δ, ν, and the script 𝒦(*f*) glyphs
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB) — the single accent colour is a saturated orange (~#E3A02A). If any production step converts to CMYK, expect a noticeable dulling/shift toward brown; keep RGB and state so on submission.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. This is the primary quality failure: at 291–302 DPI the thin 1-px curve strokes (especially the dense side-lobe envelopes in e–h and the noise traces in a–b) and the small inset tick labels will alias and partially drop out in print.
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at the true double-column width (181 mm) is ~291 DPI, below the 300 DPI minimum and well below the 600 DPI preferred for line art.
  - [ADVISORY] Inset tick labels (0.39 / 0.40 / 0.41, −25, −50) are the smallest type in the figure — approximately 6–7 pt at 181 mm width. They sit at the 6 pt absolute floor and will be the first elements to become illegible if the figure is reduced at all.
  - [ADVISORY] Numerical inconsistency between figure and caption: panel (d) annotation reads "ν = 98" while the caption states "ν = 98.4". Also caption lists half-power widths "0.8, 1.2, 8.2 and 47.8/(NΔ)" against panel annotations R = 8/(NΔ) and 48/(NΔ) — the relationship between the nominal R and the realized half-power width should be made explicit so the reader does not read these as contradictory.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend/colour mismatch: the in-panel key in (a) labels "Estimate" in light gray, but the estimate is drawn in orange in (b)–(d). The gray "Estimate" text is also very low contrast against white and may vanish in print.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption states the inset "is shown on the top left," but the insets are positioned top-centre/upper-left of each panel and in (h) extend to mid-panel. Either reposition consistently or reword.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is mostly compliant — the caption states one realization (N = 1024) and gives ν for every panel, which satisfies the degrees-of-freedom requirement. However, because only a single realization is shown, add an explicit statement that no averaging over realizations was performed and that the visible scatter in (a)–(b) is the χ²₂ variability of a single record, so readers do not mistake the traces for ensemble means.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption is unusually long and mixes specification with interpretation. IEEE prefers a brief descriptive caption; the leakage/side-lobe discussion (last three sentences) belongs in the body text.

SUGGESTED FIXES:

  - Recolour the (a) legend so "Estimate" matches the trace colour actually used in each panel, or move the legend to a shared position and use the orange swatch; darken the gray trace in (a) to ~40% K for adequate print contrast against the black true-PSD curve.
  - Add to the caption: "Single realization; no ensemble averaging. Scatter in (a) and (b) reflects the χ² distribution with ν = 2 of the raw periodogram." Retain the per-panel ν values, which already satisfy the degrees-of-freedom reporting requirement.
  - Trim the interpretive final sentences of the caption into the main text; keep the caption to panel identification plus the ν/width/leakage numbers.
  - Confirm RGB is preserved end-to-end; if the production workflow forces CMYK, substitute a darker orange (e.g. ~#C87A12) that survives conversion without shifting.

DIMENSIONS:     990 × 1252 px — recommended **single column** (88 mm / 3.5 in) — ≈283 DPI at 3.5 in (≈305 DPI if the narrower 3.25 in text measure is used); at double column (7.16 in) only ≈138 DPI. At double-column width the same aspect would give ≈230 mm height, exactly at the page-height ceiling, so double column is not advisable.
RESOLUTION:     Below threshold — figure type: **line art** (vector-style traces, annotations, axes; no photographic or continuous-tone content). Actual ≈283 DPI at the recommended width vs. 600 DPI preferred / 300 DPI absolute minimum for color line art.
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG (review-acceptable only) → **PDF or EPS (vector preferred)**, or TIFF (LZW) at ≥600 DPI if raster is unavoidable.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.22 MB (limit 10 MB); ample headroom to submit a 600–1200 DPI raster or vector file.
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all labels are baked into the PNG raster; edges of the italic math symbols ($x_k$, $\nu$, $RN\Delta$) and the thin gray leader lines will show pixel stepping at print size.
COLOR MODE:     OK for RGB submission (PNG assumed RGB). Potential shift on CMYK conversion: the single accent colour is a saturated amber/orange trace, which is the highest-risk hue for hue/lightness shift and will darken/muddy in coated CMYK. Thin orange strokes on white are also the lowest-contrast element in the figure.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution ≈283 DPI at single-column width is below the 300 DPI minimum and far below the 600 DPI preference for line art. Fine structure — the dense oscillations of the AR(4) record in (a), the open-square markers, and the dotted vertical guides in (b) — will alias or partially disappear in print.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Not acceptable for final submission; must be vector or re-rendered at ≥600 DPI.
  - [ADVISORY] Format: PNG is accepted for review but must be replaced with TIFF/EPS/PDF/PS at acceptance.
  - [ADVISORY] Smallest in-figure text (the gray "peak width" / "peak separation" annotations and the "too noisy, ν = 2" / "just right, ν = 17" callouts) scales to roughly 6–7 pt at 3.5 in width — at or just above the 6 pt floor. The gray annotation colour further reduces legibility at that size.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a)'s in-plot legend text ("Record", "Tapered record", "Taper (dashed)") sits above the axes in a title-like position. It is a legend, not a title, but reviewers/production may read it as an embedded title; consider moving it inside the axes or into the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting in (b) is only partly complete. The caption correctly states 300 independent realizations and explicitly notes that the curves are ensemble averages with no error bars, and it gives ν at the three circled points. However, the ν values quoted apply to the smoothed estimator at those specific R, and the reader is not told how ν maps to R across the rest of the abscissa, nor the equivalent degrees of freedom of the reference/"Noise" curve used for the noise comparison.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) relies on line style plus marker shape (filled circle vs. open square) to separate "whole band" from "peak region"; at 283 DPI the open-square interiors partially fill in, weakening that distinction.
  - [ADVISORY] Orange/black is distinguishable for most colour-vision deficiencies, but at a hairline stroke width the orange trace in (a) may read as light gray in grayscale reproduction.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting tool as vector **PDF or EPS** sized to 88 mm width (matplotlib: `figsize=(3.46, 4.38)`, `savefig(..., format='pdf')`). This resolves both blocking issues at once and keeps file size well under 10 MB.
  - If a raster is required, re-render at 600 DPI minimum for the 3.5 in width → **2100 × 2656 px** (1200 DPI → 4200 × 5312 px), saved as TIFF with LZW compression, RGB.
  - Set all text with a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) at 8–10 pt *at final 88 mm size*; raise the gray annotation and callout text to ≥7 pt and darken it (e.g. 40% → 60% black) for legibility.
  - Increase the orange trace linewidth to ≥0.75 pt at final size, or darken to a deeper amber, to survive both thin-stroke print loss and any CMYK conversion.
  - Increase the open-square marker size or reduce its edge width so the open/filled marker contrast survives printing; alternatively add a short in-axes legend keyed to marker shape.
  - Keep RGB for submission; if the journal converts to CMYK, supply the orange as a defined spot value or check a CMYK proof of panel (a).
  - Move the panel (a) legend text inside the axes box (or into the caption) so nothing sits in a title position above the plot frame.
  - Extend the caption's statistics sentence to give the R → ν relation (e.g. "ν ≈ 2RNΔ for the parabolic kernel") and the degrees of freedom of the plotted noise curve, so the noise comparison is fully specified. Panel labels are already correctly lowercase (a), (b).
  - Verify no embedded font subsetting problems in the exported PDF/EPS (flatten or outline fonts only if the publisher's preflight rejects the embedded set).

Meets the 300 DPI color-line-art minimum only marginally, and falls well short of the 600 DPI preferred value for line art.
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG (review-acceptable) → **PDF/EPS preferred** (vector) or 600+ DPI TIFF (LZW) for final submission
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.25 MB (limit 10 MB); ample headroom to re-export at 600 DPI
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all axis labels, tick numbers, panel letters and legend entries are pixel-based; visible anti-aliasing on thin glyph strokes (e.g. superscript indices in $h_k^{(i)}$, exponents in $10^{-16}$) and on 1-px grid/dotted lines
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB for PNG) — palette is black / mid-blue / teal-green / orange. If the publisher converts to CMYK, the saturated orange (panel a, d) and the teal-green (panel b) will desaturate most; keep RGB per TBME requirements and verify the blue/green pair remains distinguishable in grayscale print.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Line-art resolution: 293–304 DPI at the intended double-column width is below the 600 DPI preferred standard for line art, and leaves zero margin against the 300 DPI hard floor if the figure is placed at the full 181 mm. Thin curves (panel c, $L=1,3$ traces) and dotted vertical lines will alias.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Superscripts/subscripts ($h_k^{(7)}$, $|H^{(i)}(f)|^2$, $1-\lambda_i$) and the log-axis exponents are the first elements to break down; these must be vector at final size.
  - [ADVISORY] Estimated type size ≈7–7.5 pt at 181 mm width (tick labels ≈30 px at 292 px/in). This is above the 6 pt floor but below the 8–10 pt target; superscript/subscript glyphs will render near 5 pt and risk falling below the minimum.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) vertical axis spans −0.20 to 0.20 while the data occupy only ±0.12, wasting ~40 % of the panel; the legend is also placed inside the empty lower-left region, where the orange trace passes close to it.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (d): the open-circle markers at the right-hand end ($i\approx17$) are clipped by the axis spine, and the two-entry legend sits flush against the lower-right corner. Panel (d) also uses an unlabeled second dashed vertical line ($2\alpha$) whose color (orange) is reused for a data curve in panel (a), which may cause a cross-panel reading ambiguity.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption/axis-label inconsistency: caption (b) writes the ordinate as $|H^{(i)}(f)|^2/\Delta$ but the axis is labeled $|H^{(i)}(f)|^2/N\Delta^2$; caption (c) writes $\sum_{i\le L}\lambda_i|H^{(i)}(f)|^2/2\alpha\Delta$ but the axis is $\mathcal{K}(f)/N\Delta$. Reconcile the normalizations so the caption matches the rendered labels.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: the figure is deterministic (analytic tapers/kernels), so no error bars are present — but because panel (d) and the caption discuss leakage and the choice $L=2\alpha-1$, the caption should state the estimator's degrees of freedom implied by each $L$ (nominally $2L$ for $L$ orthogonal tapers, or $2\sum\lambda_i$ weighted) so readers can connect the kernel shape to noise level. Also state $N=256$, $\alpha=N\Delta R/2=4$, and the number of frequency samples used to draw the curves.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption begins with a title-like phrase ("Why the multitaper kernel is a box"); TBME prefers a descriptive caption rather than an embedded title. No title appears inside the figure — correct. Panel labels are lowercase parenthesized (a)–(d) — correct.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting library as **vector PDF or EPS** at a physical canvas size of 181 mm × 113 mm (7.16 × 4.47 in), keeping fonts embedded as Type 1/TrueType (not outlines, so they remain editable/searchable). This simultaneously resolves the resolution and rasterized-text blockers.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥600 DPI for the 181 mm width, i.e. ≥4280 × 2673 px, and save as TIFF with LZW compression (expected <10 MB).
  - Set all text to a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) at 9 pt for axis labels and 8 pt for tick labels and legends at final size; ensure superscripts/subscripts do not drop below 6 pt.
  - Tighten panel (a) y-limits to approximately −0.14…0.14 and move the legend outside the data area (or to the upper-left gap) to recover panel area and avoid the orange trace.
  - In panel (d), extend the x-limit to ~17.5 (or set clip_on=False for markers) so the final circles are not cut by the spine; move the legend inside the upper-left area where the curve is low, and consider using a neutral gray dash for the $2\alpha$ guide so orange is reserved for data.
  - Harmonize the normalization notation between caption and axis labels for panels (b) and (c), and add the $N$, $R$, $\alpha$, frequency-grid size, and degrees-of-freedom statement to the caption.
  - Verify accessibility: the blue ($L$ ladder) and teal-green ($i=3$) hues are close in luminance; consider adding line-style differentiation (solid/dash-dot) so panels (b) and (c) remain readable in grayscale.

COLOR MODE:     OK (RGB assumed for PNG) — but the four scheme colours (saturated magenta/pink, amber-orange, emerald-green, medium blue) are all in the high-risk band for CMYK gamut compression; the green and blue in particular will dull if the publisher converts. Keep RGB and verify proof.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Vector text is required for acceptable print quality; at 292–304 DPI the small italic math subscripts (h_{k-n}, c_i/Σc_i, f′NΔ) will be visibly soft.
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution below the line-art target. At the true TBME double-column width (181 mm) the figure resolves to ~292 DPI, i.e. below even the 300 DPI colour-line-art minimum and far below the 600 DPI preference.
  - [BLOCKING] Format: PNG is not an accepted final-submission format; must be supplied as TIFF/EPS/PDF/PS.
  - [ADVISORY] In-figure panel titles ("Average segments (Welch)", "Smooth one periodogram", "Slide a sinc window", "Multitaper, L = 7") violate the "no titles inside the figure" rule. They are descriptive headings, not panel identifiers.
  - [ADVISORY] Smallest text (numeric tick labels, legend entries) measures roughly 7–8 pt at 181 mm reproduction; anything that drops below 6 pt after scaling will be rejected. Legend text in (b)–(d) is the most at risk.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: the caption gives the degrees of freedom ν and its estimator (ν = 2(Σc_i)²/Σc_i²), which satisfies the DOF requirement, but the number of realizations/windows behind each estimate is stated only implicitly (seven Hann segments in (a), "four of a continuum" in (b), "four positions" in (c), L = 7 in (d)). State N, the segment count/overlap, and the number of eigen-weights retained explicitly for each panel.

  - [ADVISORY] Bars in (e)–(h) extend to i = 12–13 in some panels and i = 7 in others while all x-axes are drawn to ~11; the visual asymmetry can read as missing data rather than as zero weight.

COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB; not reported in metadata — confirm before upload). Palette is teal, blue, orange, pink on black — all four remain distinguishable in RGB. No CMYK conversion required; if one is ever made, the teal in (a) and the blue in (b) are the most likely to shift.

  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at the intended double-column width is 290 DPI — under the 300 DPI floor and less than half the 600 DPI target for line art. Rejected at pre-flight.
  - [BLOCKING] PNG is review-only; a TIFF, EPS, PDF or PS file is required for final submission.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and line work are rasterized. In a figure whose scientific content includes −100 dB kernel skirts and fine side-lobe ripple (panels f, h), rasterization at 290 DPI will visibly thicken/merge features and degrade the inset traces.
  - [ADVISORY] Tick-label type measures roughly 7–7.5 pt at 181 mm — inside the 6–10 pt window, but it leaves no margin. Do not scale this figure below double-column width; at 88 mm the same text would fall to ~3.5 pt.

Degrees of freedom are given for all three estimators (ν = 14, 16.7, 17.6) and the Welch segment count (nine) is stated, which is good, but the figure shows a single EEG epoch with no error bars, and the number of realizations behind the quoted median/95th-percentile differences is not stated. State explicitly that panel (b) is one realization (N = 1 epoch) and give the number of frequency bins over which the median and 95th percentile were computed.

### figure8.png

---

**DIMENSIONS:** 1835 × 1657 px (aspect 1.11:1) — recommended **double column** — **267 DPI** at 6.875 in (≈257 DPI if placed at the full 181 mm / 7.16 in text width; 565 DPI if forced to 3.25 in single column)

An eight-panel composite with three wide spectrograms, 30-Hz frequency axes and a 600-s time axis cannot be legibly reduced to 88 mm — double column is the only viable placement. At 181 mm the printed height is ≈158 mm, comfortably under the 230 mm limit, so the aspect ratio is fine.

**RESOLUTION:** **Below threshold** — mixed figure (spectrograms in c/e/g and the colour bars are halftone/continuous-tone raster; a/b/d/f/h and all axes are line art), actual **267 DPI at double column**. Halftone minimum is 300 DPI and line-art minimum is 300 (600 preferred); this file misses both at the recommended width. → **BLOCKING**

**FORMAT:** **Needs conversion** — PNG → TIFF (LZW) or, preferably, **PDF/EPS with vector axes/text and the three spectrogram rasters embedded at ≥300 DPI**. PNG is accepted for review only.

**FILE SIZE:** OK — 1.64 MB (limit 10 MB); a 300–600 DPI TIFF of this composite will still fit if LZW/ZIP compressed, but check after export.

**TEXT RENDERING:** **Rasterized** — all labels, tick numbers, panel letters, italic ν/L/α glyphs and the legend in (f) are baked into the bitmap. Thin-stroke items (the 1-px grey periodogram trace in d/f, the white dashed markers, the ▼ glyphs) show pixel-edge aliasing. Rasterized text in a line-art/mixed figure is **BLOCKING**.

**COLOR MODE:** Assumed **RGB** (not reported in metadata — confirm). Potential shift: the jet-style colour map used in (c,e,g) puts large areas in fully saturated blue and red, both of which are outside the CMYK gamut; if the journal or printer converts, the deep-blue background will flatten and the dark-red saturation ceiling (the clipped ≥45 dB regions) will lose the little remaining differentiation. Keep RGB and state it in the submission metadata.

**ISSUES:**
- **[BLOCKING]** 267 DPI at double-column width is below the 300 DPI halftone minimum and well below the 600 DPI line-art preference. Raster up-sampling will not fix this; re-export from source.
- **[BLOCKING]** All text is rasterized. Panel letters, axis labels and the (f) legend must be vector at final size.
- **[BLOCKING]** PNG is not an accepted final-submission format.
- **[ADVISORY]** Smallest text (the in-panel annotations "Before the seizure, t = 247 s", "In the seizure, t = 318 s") measures ≈7 pt at 181 mm; tick labels ≈8–9 pt. All are above the 6 pt floor but leave no margin — do not scale the figure down for placement.
- **[ADVISORY]** The in-panel strings "Periodogram: 2 s by 0.5 Hz, ν = 2.0", "Multitaper, L = 3: …" and "Recipe (b′): …" read as panel titles. IEEE prohibits titles inside the figure. They are technically parameter annotations, but an editor may flag them; either move the parameters to the caption or re-style them as left-aligned annotations clearly inside the axes box.
- **[ADVISORY]** Statistical reporting is largely compliant — 599 windows of 2 s stepped by 1 s is stated, and every estimator carries its equivalent degrees of freedom (ν = 2, 6, 8.7, 7.7), which satisfies the noise-comparison requirement. However, panel (h) plots a distribution whose N is not stated: give the total number of pixels (599 windows × number of frequency bins) and the histogram bin width in the caption.
- **[ADVISORY]** Panel (h) has no error bar, but it is a single-realization histogram — say so explicitly ("one realization; no resampling") so the reader does not infer a sampling distribution.
- **[ADVISORY]** Jet/rainbow colour map is not perceptually uniform and is problematic for readers with colour-vision deficiency; the ≥45 dB clipping in the ictal band is invisible against the dark-red ceiling. Consider viridis/turbo and an explicit "≥45" tick or over-range arrow on the colour bars.
- **[ADVISORY]** Three identical colour bars are repeated for one shared 10–45 dB scale, consuming width that the spectrogram time axes need. One shared colour bar would gain ~4 % usable width.
- **[ADVISORY]** Colour mode is not declared in the file metadata; confirm RGB before upload.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
1. Re-export from the plotting source at 181 mm (7.16 in) width as **PDF or EPS**, with fonts embedded as vector outlines/Type 1 and the three spectrogram `imshow`/`pcolormesh` layers rasterized at 600 DPI (e.g. Matplotlib: `rasterized=True` on the mesh artists plus `savefig(..., dpi=600)`), leaving axes, ticks and text vector. This satisfies both the halftone and line-art thresholds in one file.
2. If a raster-only workflow is unavoidable, export TIFF at ≥600 DPI for 7.16 in → **≥4300 px wide** (≥2150 px is the bare 300 DPI minimum); use LZW compression and verify the file stays under 10 MB.
3. Set figure width in the source to the physical target (7.16 in) and specify fonts in points (labels 9–10 pt, tick labels 8 pt, annotations ≥8 pt) so nothing is scaled after export; raise the two in-panel annotation strings from ~7 pt to 8 pt.
4. Keep RGB; add "RGB, no CMYK conversion requested" to the submission notes, and avoid relying on hue alone to distinguish the four traces in (d,f) — the blue/orange/pink/grey set is distinguishable, but add short dash patterns as a safeguard.
5. Extend the caption for (h): "Distribution over all N = 599 × <n_f> = <total> spectrogram pixels of the difference between (e) and (g); bin width <x> dB; single realization."
6. Replace the three colour bars with one shared bar (or state "common scale" once) and mark the 45 dB clip explicitly.
7. Consider moving the ν values and window parameters from inside panels (c,e,g) into the caption to remove any appearance of in-figure titles.

FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → TIFF (LZW) or, preferred here, **vector PDF/EPS with embedded raster image layers** for the spectrogram panels.

FILE SIZE:      OK — 1.79 MB (limit 10 MB). Note: a flat 600 DPI TIFF of this canvas would be ~4100 x 4300 px; use LZW/ZIP compression to stay under 10 MB.

TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick labels, panel letters and in-panel annotations are baked into the PNG bitmap. Stroke edges on the thin black EEG trace in (a) and the 1-px gridlines in (h) show pixel stair-stepping consistent with a single-pass raster export.

COLOR MODE:     **Potential shift** — assumed RGB (correct for IEEE). The jet colormap's saturated blues (~-5 dB end) and deep reds (>12.5 dB end) are exactly the hues that compress most in CMYK conversion; the dark-red saturation plateau and the dark-blue background risk collapsing to similar tones in print.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at the recommended 181 mm width is 291 DPI, below the 300 DPI minimum for the halftone spectrogram content and far below the 600 DPI preference for the line-art/text content.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and line work are rasterized. In a figure dominated by thin traces, hairline gridlines and ~8 pt annotations, raster text at 291 DPI will show visible edge artifacts; IEEE treats rasterized line art below spec as a quality failure.
  - [BLOCKING] PNG is review-only; a TIFF/EPS/PDF deposit is required at acceptance.
  - [ADVISORY] Smallest text (colorbar tick labels, "Multitaper"/"Smoothing" legend words in (h), the "1.1 Hz / ν = 2.0" callouts) computes to roughly 7–8 pt at 181 mm. That is compliant, but it leaves no margin — any reduction below full double-column width pushes these under 6 pt.
  - [ADVISORY] Jet colormap is perceptually non-uniform (false banding at the green-yellow transition, which sits right in the 5–7.5 dB range where the spindle/alpha contrast is being judged) and is not colorblind-safe.
  - [ADVISORY] The PSD color scale appears clipped at both ends (large uniform dark-red regions below ~3 Hz in (d)–(g)); the caption does not state that values are saturated.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting incomplete: the caption gives ν (equivalent degrees of freedom) for each setting, which satisfies the "degrees of freedom" requirement, but does not state the **number of windows** behind each spectrogram (40 s stepped by 0.1 s implies ~400/391/371 windows for the 1/2/4 s cases) nor the number of subjects/epochs (n = 1 recording, 1 N2 epoch) underlying the figure.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (h) shows point markers with no uncertainty indication; if the achieved half-power widths are single deterministic design values (not estimates), say so explicitly so readers do not expect error bars.
  - [ADVISORY] The in-panel header strings ("Multitaper, L = 1: 1 s by 1.1 Hz, ν = 2.0", etc.) read as mini-titles. They are defensible as parameter annotations, but IEEE prohibits figure titles inside the artwork — consider moving them into the caption or shortening to a tag (e.g. "MT, 1 s") to remove ambiguity.
  - [ADVISORY] Large empty region to the right of panel (h) beneath the colorbar wastes ~10% of the canvas and slightly lowers effective DPI of the data panels.
  - [ADVISORY] "colour scale" — IEEE house style is US spelling ("color"); the figure's own axis label already uses "PSD (dB re 1 µV²/Hz)" so keep spelling consistent across caption and artwork.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the plotting source as **vector PDF or EPS** at a 181 mm canvas width, letting the six spectrograms remain embedded raster layers rendered at ≥300 DPI (ideally 600 DPI) while all text, axes, gridlines, white resolution boxes and the (a)/(h) traces stay vector. This resolves the resolution and text-rasterization blockers simultaneously.

  - Add to the caption: the number of windows per spectrogram at each window length and step, the number of subjects/epochs (n = 1), and a note that ν is the equivalent degrees of freedom of each estimator (the quantity being compared across panels).
