Individual-record identity should be stated on the figure.

**COMPARISON:** Concern — Three issues.
1. *Paired structure not exploited.* The three estimators are computed from the *same* data windows, so (c), (e), (g) are a fully paired comparison, yet they are shown as three independent images. The only paired view is (h), and it covers only two of the three estimators (multitaper − smoothed); the periodogram is excluded from any difference view even though it is the panel that differs most.
2. *Post-hoc channel selection.* "The channel is the one with the largest ictal rise in 2 to 20 Hz power" — the displayed channel was chosen by maximising the very effect the figure illustrates. This is a selection-over-channels procedure and makes the displayed contrast an upper bound, not a typical case.
3. *Non-comparable y-axes in (d) vs (f).* Panel (d) spans roughly 0–45 dB and (f) roughly 20–80 dB, with different tick spacing and no shared reference. The pre-ictal/ictal magnitude change — arguably the main biological content — is therefore not readable across the two panels, and the apparent "wiggliness" of the grey periodogram trace is rescaled differently in each.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present (three instances, one material)
- *Panel (a):* the trace is clipped at ±500 µV and the ictal burst plainly hits the limit. This is disclosed in the caption ("displayed between ±500 µV"), so it is honest, but the clipping is not marked on the plot and a reader will under-read ictal amplitude. This truncation *understates* rather than exaggerates, so it is advisory.
- *Panels (c,e,g) colour axis:* the 10–45 dB scale is saturated at both ends. Large regions — the low-frequency band throughout, and essentially the whole seizure block in (g) — sit in the clipped dark-red terminal colour. Because the three estimators clip *different* fractions of pixels, the visual impression that the smoothed estimate is "cleaner" is partly an artefact of more of its dynamic range being flattened into saturation. This one is material and affects the figure's central claim.
- *Panel (h) x-axis:* appears to terminate near ±8 dB with the distribution still off-zero at both edges. If tail mass falls outside the plotted range, the histogram is truncated and the impression of a tightly concentrated difference is optimistic.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — Two forms. (1) Raster downsampling: 599 windows × the frequency bins are being rendered into a panel a few hundred pixels wide, so the speckle in (c) is partly display aliasing rather than estimator variance; at a different output resolution the panel will look different. (2) In (d) and (f), four traces overlay in the same axes with the grey periodogram running beneath three coloured curves; in (f) the multitaper (blue) and Recipe (orange) curves are largely coincident and mutually occluding, so the reader cannot judge which is smoother where.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** Flag for verification — No p-values or significance brackets are annotated, so the classical concern does not arise. However, the channel-selection rule ("largest ictal rise in 2 to 20 Hz power") is an implicit maximum over an unstated number of channels, and should be reported as such: how many channels were screened, and how the figure looks for a non-selected channel. Please also confirm whether the estimator parameters (25% cosine taper; α = 2, L = 3; 2 Hz parabola; 0.7 s Hann) were fixed a priori or tuned after inspecting this record.

**TIME SERIES:** Concern — The interval from roughly 365 s to 400 s is near-flat in (a) and appears as a uniform dark-blue block in all three spectrograms, with an abrupt step and narrow-band horizontal streaks around 395–410 s. This is consistent with either genuine post-ictal suppression or with a data dropout / amplifier saturation-recovery / disconnection artefact — and the figure gives the reader no way to distinguish them. Nothing marks the segment as excluded, interpolated, or artefactual, and the spectrogram is drawn continuously across it. Separately, the x-axis of (a) begins at 0 while (c,e,g) begin at the first window centre (~1 s) with the first labelled tick at 100; the stacked panels are therefore not exactly co-registered, which undermines the vertical visual alignment that the dashed lines and triangles are meant to support.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Colour-axis saturation at 45 dB clips a substantial and *unequal* fraction of pixels in (c), (e) and (g). Because the figure's purpose is to compare the three estimators' variance and smoothness, differential clipping directly confounds the comparison.
- [BLOCKING] The 365–400 s flat/blue segment is not identified. If this is a dropout or artefact it must be masked and labelled; if it is physiological post-ictal suppression that must be stated. As drawn, the spectrograms interpolate colour across a possibly invalid interval.
- [BLOCKING] Post-hoc channel selection is disclosed but its scope is not: the number of channels screened is absent, so the displayed contrast cannot be interpreted as typical.
- [ADVISORY] Rainbow colour map introduces false structure and is not colour-vision-accessible.
- [ADVISORY] n = 1 record/seizure/channel is not stated on the figure; the pixel count underlying (h) is not given.
- [ADVISORY] Pixels in (h) are not independent: 2 s windows stepped by 1 s overlap 50%, and adjacent frequency bins of a smoothed estimate are correlated by construction. Any spread or percentile read off (h) is not an independent-sample statistic.
- [ADVISORY] (d) and (f) use different y-ranges, preventing cross-panel magnitude comparison; curve occlusion in (f).
- [ADVISORY] Signal clipping at ±500 µV in (a) is unmarked on the axis.
- [ADVISORY] (h) x-axis appears to cut the tails; no central-tendency or zero reference line is drawn.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Extend the colour scale to cover the full data range, or state in the caption the percentage of pixels clipped at each limit for each of (c), (e), (g), and add out-of-range triangle markers to the colourbars. If a fixed 10–45 dB range is retained for legibility, add an inset or supplementary panel with the unclipped range so the comparison is verifiable.
- Replace the rainbow map with a perceptually uniform sequential map and note the choice in the caption.
- Mask 365–400 s with a hatched/grey "excluded" or "artefact" band across (a), (c), (e) and (g), or, if the segment is valid physiology, label it "post-ictal suppression" with an arrow. Do not leave it to inference.
- State in the caption: number of subjects/recordings (n = 1), number of channels screened before selection, and — ideally — add a supplementary figure repeating the comparison on a randomly chosen channel and a second seizure, to show the result is not an artefact of maximisation.
- Add a third difference panel (periodogram − smoothed), or convert (h) into a small matrix of the three pairwise differences, so the paired structure of the three estimators is fully exploited.
- Report on (h) the total pixel count, the median and interquartile range, and a vertical line at 0; extend the x-limits until the histogram reaches zero density at both ends, or annotate the mass beyond the plotted range.
- Put (d) and (f) on a common y-range (or add a shared secondary axis), and either offset the four curves slightly or add a small residual sub-panel so the coincident multitaper and Recipe traces can be distinguished.
- Mark the ±500 µV clipping in (a) explicitly (e.g. axis-break glyphs or a note "trace clipped at display limits; peak |EEG| = ___ µV").
- Co-register the x-axes of (a) and (c,e,g) to identical limits, including a 0 tick on the spectrogram panels, so the dashed markers and triangles align exactly.
- Confirm whether estimator parameters were pre-specified; if tuned on this record, say so.
---

### figure9.png

---
PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — No bar graphs, pie charts, or summary-statistic-only displays. Panel (a) is a raw trace (fully disaggregated data), (b)–(g) are spectrograms (heatmaps, the correct choice for time–frequency estimates), and (h) is a paired dot/lollipop plot rather than a bar chart, which is the recommended alternative for a small number of values. No plot-type substitution is needed. Two secondary encoding concerns are noted under ISSUES: (i) the rainbow/jet-like colormap in (b)–(g) is perceptually non-uniform and can manufacture apparent contour boundaries that are not in the data, and (ii) the low-frequency band is visibly saturated at the top of the colour range, so dynamic range is clipped.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Not visible — This is an illustrative single-case figure: one channel (C4-M1), one 40-s N2 epoch, presumably one subject, but n is nowhere stated in the figure or caption. Individual data are effectively fully shown (the raw trace in (a) and the full time–frequency surfaces in (b)–(g)), so the usual small-n concealment problem does not arise; however, readers cannot tell whether the epoch is representative or hand-picked. Panel (h) plots 6 deterministic quantities (one per method × window), not sampled estimates, so n = 1 per point by construction — this should be said explicitly so readers do not read the connected points as group means.

COMPARISON:         Valid — The central comparison (multitaper vs. smoothed periodogram) is inherently paired: both methods are applied to the same data at the same window length. Panel (h) correctly renders this pairing with connecting lines within each window setting, and (b)–(g) place the two methods side by side at matched rows. The shared colour scale across all six spectrograms is the right decision and is stated in the caption. The white resolution box is an excellent, and unusually honest, way to make the resolution–variance trade-off visually auditable. One residual concern: the comparison is only fair if both estimators were computed on identical samples with identical detrending, padding, and step size; this is not verifiable from the figure.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    Present — Panel (h)'s x-axis (achieved half-power width) begins at ~0.40 Hz rather than 0. Half-power width is a ratio-scale quantity with a meaningful zero, so truncation inflates the apparent relative separation between the paired markers. The 1-s row (1.1 vs 2.1 Hz, a ~1.9× ratio) and the 4-s row (0.8 vs 1.0 Hz, a ~1.25× ratio) render as segment lengths of roughly 2.1× ratio, which visually overstates how close the 4-s pair is relative to the 1-s pair. This is milder than truncation on a bar chart because dot position, not bar length, carries the encoding, but the connecting-line length does read as a magnitude. Panel (a)'s EEG axis is centred and symmetric and is fine; note, however, that the offset 11–16 Hz trace has no amplitude scale of its own, so its apparent spindle amplitude is uncalibrated.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present — Not in the conventional scatter sense. (1) In (b)–(g), windows are stepped by 0.1 s while window lengths are 1–4 s, giving 90–97.5% overlap; adjacent columns are therefore highly correlated and the surfaces look far smoother and better-resolved in time than the independent information content supports. The white box mitigates this, but the oversampling factor should be stated as such. (2) Colour saturation in the 0–3 Hz band (uniform dark red across large regions) is a form of data occlusion: all values above the upper colour limit are rendered identically, hiding the true low-frequency dynamic range and any artefact contamination there. (3) Panel (a)'s broadband trace is dense enough at this aspect ratio that individual spindle morphology in the shaded windows is hard to verify; the reader is asked to trust the shading.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No p-values, significance brackets, asterisks, or inferential annotations appear anywhere in the figure. All quantities shown (half-power width, ν, PSD) are deterministic or descriptive properties of the estimators, so no correction is applicable. Nothing here requires author verification on this axis.

TIME SERIES:        Concern — Three items. (a) The 40-s epoch is presented as continuous; if any segment was artefact-rejected, interpolated, or spliced, that is invisible and would constitute misleading interpolation across a gap. Confirm contiguity explicitly. (b) The spectrograms in (b)–(g) appear to span the full 0–40 s axis, but window centres for a 4-s window can only span ~2–38 s without padding or edge extrapolation; whatever edge handling was used (zero-padding, reflection, truncation) inflates or distorts the first and last window-length/2 of each panel and should be documented or masked. (c) Panel (a)'s time axis and the spectrogram time axes are not visibly aligned by a shared vertical guide, so the shaded spindles in (a) cannot be read off against (b)–(g) precisely — which is the figure's main claim.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Colour saturation/clipping in (b)–(g) is undocumented. Large low-frequency regions render as uniform dark red, indicating values exceed the upper colour limit (~13 dB). Because the caption asserts "one colour scale," readers will assume the full range is shown. The actual data range must be reported and the clipping disclosed, otherwise cross-panel PSD comparisons in the delta band are unsupportable.
  - [BLOCKING] Edge handling for the spectrograms is unspecified while the panels span the full 0–40 s time axis. With 1–4 s windows this is impossible without padding or extrapolation; the near-edge columns are therefore partly synthetic and must be either masked or explicitly described.
  - [ADVISORY] Truncated x-axis in panel (h) (starts ~0.40 Hz, not 0) exaggerates the relative separation of the paired multitaper/smoothing markers, particularly in the 4-s row.
  - [ADVISORY] Rainbow/jet-like colormap is perceptually non-uniform: it creates spurious edges at the cyan–yellow and yellow–red transitions that can be misread as genuine spectral boundaries, and it is poorly distinguishable under red–green colour-vision deficiency.
  - [ADVISORY] Sample provenance is not stated in the figure: no subject/recording identifier, no n, no statement of whether this epoch is representative or selected.
  - [ADVISORY] 90–97.5% window overlap (0.1 s step) makes adjacent columns strongly dependent, giving a visual impression of temporal resolution that exceeds the true resolution indicated by the white box.
  - [ADVISORY] The offset 11–16 Hz trace in (a) has no amplitude calibration, so spindle amplitude cannot be judged and the visual prominence of the five shaded events is scale-arbitrary.
  - [ADVISORY] Contiguity of the 40-s epoch is unconfirmed; any artefact rejection or splicing would be invisible.
  - [ADVISORY] Spindle shading in (a) reflects an unspecified detection procedure (visual scoring? automated detector?), making the five highlighted events an unreproducible annotation.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Extend panel (h)'s x-axis to 0 Hz, or, if compression is necessary, add an explicit axis break and state in the caption that the axis does not start at zero.
  - Report the full PSD range in the caption (e.g. "colour limits −6 to 13 dB; N% of pixels clipped at the upper limit") and either widen the limits, apply a shared robust percentile stretch, or hatch/mask saturated pixels.
  - Replace the rainbow colormap with a perceptually uniform sequential map (viridis, magma, cividis) or a uniform divergent map if a reference level is meaningful; cividis additionally preserves ordering under colour-vision deficiency.
  - Mask or shade the first and last window-length/2 of each spectrogram, and state the edge-handling and detrending scheme applied identically to both estimators.
  - Add n and provenance to the caption: number of subjects, recording, epoch start time, scoring source, and whether the epoch was chosen as representative or arbitrarily.
  - State the window step (0.1 s) and the resulting overlap fraction in the caption, and note that adjacent columns are not independent.
  - Add an amplitude scale bar for the 11–16 Hz trace in (a), or plot it in its own sub-axis with its own labelled µV axis.
  - Add faint vertical guide lines (or a shared linked x-axis with the spindle shading repeated as thin markers along the top of (b)–(g)) so the five spindles can be located in every spectrogram.
  - Specify how the five spindles were identified (scorer, detector, criteria) so the shading is reproducible.
  - In the caption, state explicitly that panel (h) shows analytically determined estimator properties, not sampled estimates, so the absence of error bars is correct and expected.

---

## Cross-Figure Consistency Orchestrator

# FINAL CROSS-FIGURE SYNTHESIS

## Scope note
Specialist agents have already documented per-figure deficiencies (resolution/format, rasterized text, caption completeness, in-panel annotation gaps, jet colormap, clipped axes). Those are **not** repeated here except where the defect is a *set-level inconsistency* that no single-figure review can see. The set comprises 9 main figures and 4 extended figures produced from what is evidently a shared plotting template — which makes the remaining inconsistencies both conspicuous and cheap to fix.

---

BLOCKING ISSUES (must resolve before submission):

1. **Semantic collision in the categorical palette: the same estimator is assigned two different colors, and one color is assigned to two different estimators.** The manuscript's central identity claim is that the sliding-sinc average, the box-smoothed untapered periodogram, and the λ-weighted all-taper multitaper estimate are *the same estimator*. Yet "Sliding sinc window" is **pink** in figure1 while the sinc-slide column is **green** in figure5 and efigure4 — and **pink** in figure5/efigure4/figure6/figure7/figure8 denotes **Welch's method**, a genuinely different estimator. A reader tracking pink across the narrative is led from "sinc window" (Fig. 1) to "Welch" (Figs. 5–8). This actively obstructs the paper's thesis. — affects: [figure1, figure5, figure6, figure7, figure8, efigure4]

2. **Two distinct oranges carry four different referents across the set.** A marigold/amber (~#E6A02A) consistently means "25% cosine taper, then parabola / recipe (b′)" (efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure2, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9). A redder orange (~#D9531E) means "Hann, then Gaussian" (efigure3), "7th Slepian taper" (figure4a), and "the 2α reference line" (figure4d). At print size and after CMYK conversion these two oranges are not reliably separable, so four unrelated objects share one apparent color identity. — affects: [efigure3, figure2, figure3, figure4, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9]

3. **Method colors are overloaded as index colors in figure4.** Blue = multitaper and green = untapered-then-box are load-bearing method colors in eleven of thirteen figures, but figure4(a,b) uses blue = taper *i* = 2 and green = taper *i* = 3. Because figure4 is specifically the multitaper-kernel figure, the reader will read the green curve as "the box method" and the blue as "the multitaper method." — affects: [figure4, and by contamination figure1, figure5, figure6, efigure1–4]

4. **Colormap convention is inconsistent with the rest of the set and internally within figure8.** Figures 8 and 9 use a jet/rainbow map for PSD, introducing pure cyan, saturated red and dark maroon — hues that appear nowhere else in a set otherwise built from a muted, medium-saturation categorical palette. Worse, figure8 places jet spectrograms (c,e,g) immediately adjacent to line panels (d,f) drawn in the muted grey/blue/amber/pink palette, so two incompatible color systems coexist inside one figure. Both figures must adopt the same perceptually uniform map (cividis recommended for CVD safety) and the same colorbar convention. — affects: [figure8, figure9]

5. **dB reference level convention is not uniform across figures plotting the same physical quantity.** figure9 correctly labels "PSD (dB re 1 µV²/Hz)". figure1(a), figure7(a) and figure8 (colorbars and d/f ordinates) plot the *same class of real EEG spectra* but label only "dB" or "PSD (dB)". Simulated-data figures (efigure2, figure2, figure6) label "Ŝ(f) (dB)" with no reference at all. A reader cannot compare dB levels between figure1, figure7, figure8 and figure9 as drawn, even though the manuscript invites exactly that comparison (same EEG epoch appears in figure1 and figure7). — affects: [figure1, figure2, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9, efigure2]

6. **The same quantity is denoted by two different axis labels across figures.** The estimated PSD is "Ŝ(f)" in efigure2, figure1, figure2, figure6, figure7 but "PSD" in figure8 and figure9. Similarly ν appears as a bare in-panel number in figure2/figure5/figure6/figure8/figure9, as a bare y-axis symbol in efigure3(a), and only in caption prose in efigure1/efigure2. One symbol per quantity, defined once, used everywhere. — affects: [all 13 figures]

7. **Axis-title capitalization is inconsistent.** efigure3 uses all-lowercase titles ("half-power width ×NΔ", "kernel power beyond twice the half-power half-width", "peak beyond 1.5 times the half-power half-width (dB)") while figure1, figure3, figure7, figure8, figure9 use sentence case with an initial capital ("Frequency (Hz)", "RMS error (dB)", "Achieved half-power width (Hz)"). This is the single most visible typographic discrepancy in the set. — affects: [efigure3 vs. figure1, figure3, figure7, figure8, figure9]

---

ADVISORY ISSUES (strongly recommended):

1. **Font sizes for equivalent elements differ by 1–2 pt between sparse and dense figures.** At their implied reproduction widths, tick labels run ~8 pt in efigure1/figure1/figure4 but ~6.5 pt in efigure4, figure8, figure9 and in the insets of figure2/figure6. Because the panel count varies, the template evidently shrinks *type* rather than growing the *canvas*. Standardize on ticks 7.5 pt / axis titles 9 pt / legends and annotations 8 pt / panel labels 10 pt, and let panel geometry absorb the density. — affects: [efigure4, figure2, figure6, figure8, figure9]

2. **Legend convention is mixed: boxed legends vs. colored in-plot text keys.** Boxed legends appear in efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure1(a), figure4, figure5, figure7, figure8(f), figure9(h); colored inline text keys appear in figure1(b), figure2(a), figure3(a), figure6(a). Both are defensible individually, but the set should pick one primary convention and use the other only where curves coincide. — affects: [figure1, figure2, figure3, figure6 vs. efigure1–3, figure4, figure5, figure7, figure8, figure9]

3. **Legend *position* convention is not fixed.** Legends sit inside-upper-right (efigure1a, efigure2a, figure1a, figure7a), inside-upper-left (figure4c), inside-lower-left (figure4a), inside-lower-right (figure4d), in a dedicated empty quadrant (efigure3, lower right), and inside panel (f) only for a two-panel series (figure8). efigure3's legend is in the *last*-read quadrant while panels (a) and (b) need it first. Establish a single rule (e.g. always upper-right inside, or always a shared strip above/below the panel block). — affects: [efigure1, efigure3, figure4, figure8]

4. **Colorbar treatment differs between the two spectrogram figures.** figure8 repeats three identical colorbars for one shared 10–45 dB scale; figure9 correctly uses a single shared colorbar for six panels. Adopt figure9's convention in figure8 — this also recovers ~4% of usable panel width. — affects: [figure8, figure9]

5. **Within-column lightness ramps are shallower in some figures than others.** figure5 and efigure4 encode bank-member index by a single-hue lightness ramp, but the ramps (especially the teal-green sinc column) span too little L* to be ordered; figure4(c) has the same problem with its five-step blue ramp plus a black L=N curve. Use a consistent 4–5 step sequential ramp spanning roughly L*35–L*85 in all three. — affects: [figure4, figure5, efigure4]

6. **Shared-axis labelling convention is not stated or applied uniformly.** figure2/figure6 omit x-axis titles on the top row; figure8(c,e) and figure9(b–e) omit them on upper rows; other figures label every panel. Either label all, or adopt "bottom row only" everywhere and state it once per caption. — affects: [figure2, figure6, figure8, figure9, efigure4]

7. **The green/olive sibling pair recurs in exactly the two figures where it is least survivable.** "Untapered, then box" (teal-green) and "Hann, then box" (olive) appear together in both efigure1 and efigure3, and cross one another in both. Under deuteranopia, protanopia and greyscale these collapse. Because this pair recurs across figures, fixing it in one place is not sufficient — it needs a set-level palette decision. — affects: [efigure1, efigure3]

8. **Visual weight is unbalanced across the set.** figure8 (1.64 MB, 8 panels, three spectrograms, four line panels, three colorbars) and figure9 (1.79 MB, 8 panels, six spectrograms) are dramatically denser and more saturated than figure3 and figure7 (single-column, 2 panels, near-monochrome). efigure4 (16 panels) is denser than the main figure it expands (figure5, 8 panels). The set currently reads as three separate visual registers rather than one cohesive family; harmonizing the colormap (Blocking 4) and font stylesheet (Advisory 1) will resolve most of this without re-layout.

9. **Panel-label style is consistent and should be preserved** — lowercase letter in parentheses, outside the axes at upper-left, uniform size, in all 13 figures and matching all captions. Only two refinements: (i) decide once whether these are bold (they currently appear non-bold in the e-figures and figure1–7 and bold in figure8) and apply uniformly; (ii) align the label offset from the axes frame, which drifts slightly in figure1(a) relative to (b)/(c).

10. **Error-bar convention is uniform by absence, but the *reason* is not uniformly stated.** No figure draws error bars. This is correct — efigure1, efigure3, efigure4, figure4, figure5 are deterministic; figure2, figure6, efigure2 are single realizations; figure3 is an ensemble mean explicitly declared to carry none. However only figure3 and efigure3 say so in the caption. Because ν is used throughout as the *proxy* for uncertainty, the set needs one consistent statement: ν is the equivalent degrees of freedom, defined once, reported for every estimator whose noise is compared, with an explicit note wherever curves are analytic vs. single-realization vs. ensemble-averaged. Currently efigure2 omits ν for the adaptive estimate and efigure1/efigure2 never define ν at all.

11. **Figure ordering and citation appear logical, with one flag.** The narrative runs: identity of three routes (1) → kernel/bias–variance mechanism (2) → resolution selection (3) → why the multitaper kernel is a box (4) → unified taper-bank view (5) → practical estimators compared (6) → practical estimators on EEG (7) → seizure application (8) → spindle application (9). This is a coherent build. One flag: figure3's caption refers to "the record of Fig. \ref{fig:motif}" and to "panels (b), (c) and (d) of Fig. \ref{fig:motif}", and figure6 and figure7 also cite `fig:motif` and `fig:abstract`. Confirm that `fig:motif` = figure2 and `fig:abstract` = figure1, and that no figure is cited before its number; as drafted, figure3 depends entirely on figure2 for the meaning of its three circled points, which is acceptable only if figure2 precedes it in both numbering and first citation. Also confirm efigure4's "Fig. 5 in full" and figure5's forward reference "Fig. S4 adds the eigen-tapers and kernels" are mutually consistent in the final numbering (S4 vs. efigure4).

12. **Two internal numeric inconsistencies have a cross-figure dimension.** figure2(d) annotates ν = 98 while its caption says 98.4 *and* figure3's annotation says "too smooth, ν = 98" — so three places in two figures disagree on one number. Likewise figure2 gives peak half-power width as 7/(NΔ) in the preamble but describes R = 8/(NΔ) as "that of a peak", while figure3's dotted line marks "peak width" at RNΔ ≈ 7. Reconcile once and propagate.

---

OVERALL ASSESSMENT:

**Publication readiness: Major revisions**

The set is the product of a single, well-built plotting template and shows genuine craft — uniform panel lettering, despined axes, subordinate gridlines, grey for de-emphasized periodograms, black for ground truth, and colored inline labels where curves coincide. Its cohesion problems are therefore narrow and highly tractable: they are almost entirely (i) a categorical palette whose color→estimator mapping is not stable across figures, (ii) two figures that abandon the set's color system for jet, and (iii) unharmonized label conventions (dB reference, quantity symbol, capitalization, font size) that a single regenerated stylesheet would fix in one pass.

The two items that must be resolved before submission are the palette collisions and the colormap. The pink sinc/Welch swap between figure1 and figure5 is not cosmetic — it inverts the color evidence for the manuscript's central identity claim, and a reviewer who tracks color rather than legend text will conclude the figures contradict the text. Jet in figures 8 and 9 is independently disqualifying on accessibility grounds and additionally manufactures false contours in exactly the panels whose claim is that one estimate is smoother than another.

Recommended execution order: (1) fix the color→estimator mapping globally and state it once in the first caption ("throughout: blue = multitaper, green = untapered periodogram / box / sliding sinc, amber = cosine taper + parabola, pink = Welch, grey = periodogram, black = truth"); (2) replace jet with cividis and consolidate figure8's colorbars; (3) regenerate all 13 figures from one rcParams stylesheet with fixed type sizes, sentence-case axis titles, and a uniform dB reference convention; (4) re-export as vector PDF/EPS, which simultaneously clears the resolution and rasterized-text blockers raised by the format agent across every figure in the set.
